manufactures the despair the panel exists to prevent).

| Family | Wire / effort dial (on KIE) | Cache profile | Usage namespace | Delivery on KIE (measured 2026-08-03/04) |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `claude` (10 ids, unchanged) | Messages; `thinking:{adaptive, summarized}` + `output_config.effort` | **derived** 0.1× read / 2.0× write — pair REFUSED in the price list | `anthropic` | **batched** (100% of chars in the final second) |
| `codex` (`gpt-5-6-sol`/`-luna`/`-terra`) | Responses; `reasoning.effort` (`max` clamps DOWN to `xhigh`) | **explicit-pair** — the row quotes BOTH KIE-published rates or neither | `openai` | **streamed** (2,382 deltas, 1.0% in the final second) |
| `gemini` (`gemini-3-5-flash`) | native; `generationConfig.thinkingConfig {includeThoughts, thinkingLevel}` (`medium`→`low`, above→`high`) | **none** — KIE quotes no cached rate and reports no cached-token counter, so cached tokens bill at FULL input rate (owner decision, flagged and accepted) | `google` | **streamed** (19 deltas, 6.8% in the final second) |
| `chat` (Comet's OpenAI-compatible resale ids: grok-/kimi-/qwen/glm-/deepseek/minimax-) | chat-completions | **none** — same reason as gemini: no vendor cached rate we can verify and no cached-token counter to observe, so cached tokens bill at FULL input rate | `openai` | *not served by KIE* — assumed streaming on Comet (unprobed) |

- **🔴 An unknown model id REFUSES loudly at model resolution — it never defaults to a wire.** `requireFamily` throws (naming the id and the accepted prefixes) as the FIRST thing `getModelInstance` does, before the key lookup and before any wire is built. The alternative is not a sensible default, it is a guess: `capabilities.ts`'s tables default to modern **Claude**, so a `gpt-*` id would have had an Anthropic `thinking` block written into an OpenAI request body — a hard 400 before a token, on a model the operator believes is configured.
- **🔴 The Claude wrappers wrap the CLAUDE branch ONLY**, by branch structure rather than by care: `thinkingFetch`, `kieFetch`, `stripSamplingParams` and `dropOrphanReasoningSignatures` all encode Anthropic wire facts, and applying one to another family is that same 400. Pinned by a default-deny source scan (`kie-dispatch.spec.ts`). Claude behavior is otherwise **byte-identical to before** — including `KIE_BASE_URL`, which stays Claude-scoped (one override silently repointing all three would be a config value whose meaning changed under the operator).
- **`thinkingMode` per family — and its absence here was a silent no-op for a week.** The proxy's last-resort retry passes `thinkingMode: 'disabled'` (the mitigation for KIE's ~30s silent-step kill); `anthropic.ts` declared the parameter and **`kie.ts` dropped it on the floor** as an excess property, so on the provider the mitigation was written FOR, the third attempt was byte-identical to the first two. It is declared now, and maps per family: claude → `canDisableThinking(model, effort)` with the existing clamp-to-`adaptive`; codex → `reasoning.effort: 'low'`; gemini → `thinkingLevel: 'low'`. **Never an off switch on the foreign wires** — neither has one, and a 400 for an unsupported value would land on the attempt that had already failed twice.
- **The usage cache counters are the one thing the `ai` SDK does not normalise**, so one reader (`agent/usage-metadata.ts`) serves both money paths (`step-usage.ts` settlement + the persisted step log) instead of two hardcoded `providerMetadata.anthropic` literals. A missing individual value bills silent-zero (the honest reading); a **wholly missing namespace on a cache-priced family** is warned once per generation, because that means the counter we bill from disappeared and every generation would silently bill as if the cache did not exist — with the credit total going DOWN, which reads as a cheaper turn. Gemini is exempt (absence is expected there).
- **🔴 A model id ships ONLY after a live probe** (accepted + streaming observed + usage captured), added to `KIE_MODELS` **and** the price list in the same edit. **KIE's pricing feed is not evidence an id exists** — the feed says `gpt-5.6-sol` (dots) where the API answers to `gpt-5-6-sol` (dashes), so a row copied from the feed prices a model that cannot be called. ⚠️ On the 2026-08-04 probe run the **Claude** family was failing 25–100% with `Server exception, please try again later` — `claude-sonnet-5`, the platform default, at **0/4** — while gpt-5-6-sol/luna went 2/2. That is a live vendor incident on one adapter, a snapshot rather than a verdict; **re-probe before reading it as a reason to move a rung** (`node scripts/kie-model-health.mjs`).
- **The delivery table is DATA** (`agent/delivery.ts`), keyed by (provider, family) and never by model — a per-model table would report `streamed` the day someone points `LLM_MODEL` at another Claude model on the same buffering adapter. An **unmeasured** provider or family is assumed to STREAM: the `batched` sentence tells the user to expect nothing for minutes, and claiming it about a surface we never measured manufactures the despair the panel exists to prevent. If KIE's Claude adapter heals, re-measure with `scripts/stream-probe.mjs` and flip one word.
- **Bumped, behind a hard gate:** `@ai-sdk/openai` 1.1.2 → **1.3.24**, `@ai-sdk/google` 0.0.52 → **1.2.22**, both resolving to the same `@ai-sdk/provider@1.1.3` / `provider-utils@2.2.8` as the **untouched** `@ai-sdk/anthropic@1.2.12` + `ai@4.3.16`.
- ⚠️ **NOT established, and not to be implied:** no generation has been driven end-to-end through the platform proxy on `codex` or `gemini` (the wires are probe-measured and unit-pinned; the full request path is not). Output QUALITY on those families is **unevaluated** — every skill, brief and measurement in this repo is Claude-tuned, and there are no output-quality evals at all. **No rung has been reassigned**: all three §4.6.1a selectors still name Claude models. Which models earn rungs is an operator decision after a manual eval, not a code change.

**🔴 `AUTO_MODEL_SELECT` — THE PLATFORM MAY CHOOSE THE GATEWAY PER REQUEST (BUILT 2026-08-10, DEFAULT OFF; `agent/provider-select.ts`).** `LLM_PROVIDER` names a gateway; `LLM_PROVIDER_CHAIN` (default `KIE,Comet,Anthropic`) names a preference order, cheapest first. With the flag off the provider is exactly `LLM_PROVIDER`, byte for byte — the ladder cannot move a single generation until an operator opts in (the `cache-warmer` lesson: a capability that ships on by default is one nobody measured before it started billing).

*Why a ladder at all:* credits are cost-proportional, so a cheaper gateway is **not** our margin — it is the user's pack going further ("any discount on the model shows up in the user's amount of credit power"). And a fixed provider made a vendor outage a config edit plus a redeploy, which is how the 2026-08 KIE outage was actually experienced.

- **Chosen ONCE, at request start, never mid-stream (owner decision).** `getPlatformConfig` resolves it and `config.provider` flows to both the wire AND `settleGeneration`, so the gateway that spent the tokens is by construction the one whose rates bill them. Failing over mid-stream would settle gateway A's tokens at gateway B's rates — a mis-bill with no honest correction, since `rawCostUsd` prices a turn against ONE rate table.
- **Three gates.** Configured (has a key) → **can price the model it would run, in its own right** → not in failure cooldown. The middle one is the money gate: `ratesFor` falls back to a provider's MOST EXPENSIVE row for an unpriced model, so failing over to a gateway that cannot price the model would run the turn and bill it at the top of that table — the rung chosen for a discount becoming the biggest bill in the product.
- **Cooldown is a PREFERENCE, never a wall.** A gateway that fails before producing any output is skipped for `PROVIDER_COOLDOWN_MS` (5 min) and returns to the front on its first success. If every rung is cooling the turn still runs on the best one: "all providers had a blip" must not become "the product is down" — one refunded generation instead of every generation. ⚠️ The cooldown is minutes and not seconds because **the ~150k cached prefix is per gateway**: every switch pays a full cache WRITE at 2×, ~8× one prefix before it warms, so flapping costs more than the outage does.
- **Only a gateway FAILURE cools a rung.** The signal is the retry site (`shouldRetryGeneration` — the provider broke before a single billed token), never the generation's `failed` flag: a turn can fail for a zod violation, an unproductive answer or a refusal while the gateway behaved perfectly, and cooling a healthy rung on that evidence moves every following turn onto a cold prefix.
- **Everything that resolves a provider takes the SELECTED one, not `LLM_PROVIDER`.** `getPlatformModel`, `getTierModel` and the cache warmer all accept/resolve it, the marketplace price ensure covers the whole chain (`providersToPrice`), and the warmer follows the ladder so it never warms a prefix nobody is sending. Each of those was a real defect found in review; a reader that re-derives the provider is the `kieEnvModel` two-readers class.
- **The ladder degrades, never 503s.** `/api/me` and the health check both call `getPlatformConfig`, so a retired price variable or a typo'd chain must not take the session endpoint down for every user (the 2026-07-25 `modelTiersSessionHint` lesson). `resolvePlatformProvider` catches and falls back to `LLM_PROVIDER` — the same path the flag-off deploy runs — and logs at error level; `getProviderChain` still throws for anything calling it directly.

**The gateway variables, in one place** (all in `.env.example`; §5's never-`VITE_`-prefix rule binds every key here):

| Variable | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `LLM_PROVIDER` | The gateway. Accepts **three** values now — `KIE` \| `Comet` \| `Anthropic`. Default `KIE`. |
| `LLM_MODEL` | The platform model, validated against the ACTIVE price list for the selected gateway. |
| `AUTO_MODEL_SELECT` | Opt in to the ladder above. **Default off.** |
| `LLM_PROVIDER_CHAIN` | Preference order for the ladder. Default `KIE,Comet,Anthropic`. |
| `COMET_API_KEY` | Comet's key. Its absence is what `isConfigured` gates the Comet rung on. |
| `COMET_BASE_URL` | Override Comet's base URL. Optional. |
| `MEDIA_PROVIDER` | The §4.16 media gateway, INDEPENDENT of `LLM_PROVIDER` and falling back to it when unset — where "unset on an Anthropic deploy" honestly means *no media*, not *broken media*. |

⚠️ **`COMET_BASE_URL` and `MEDIA_PROVIDER` sit in `.env.example` as PROSE, not as commented
assignments** — deliberately. A later assignment wins in a real `.env`, and this repo has already
shipped `SIGNUP_GRANT_CREDITS` twice with different values, handing out the wrong grant to anyone who
copied the file. A commented `MEDIA_PROVIDER=` line one paste away from being uncommented is that bug
waiting for a second author. Pinned by a duplicate-key spec.

**Delivery for Comet is `streamed` for the `claude` family (MEASURED with `scripts/stream-probe.mjs`)
and assumed-streaming elsewhere** — per the delivery table's rule above, an unmeasured surface is
never claimed to be `batched`.

**Three wire-level failures that MUST stay fixed (full rationale: `spec/anthropic-models.md`, kept in-repo):**
1. **`temperature is deprecated for this model` (400).** Sonnet 5 / Opus 4.8 / 4.7 / Fable 5 removed sampling params. `ai@4` *injects* `temperature: 0` when the caller supplies none (`temperature != null ? temperature : 0`) — so it CANNOT be suppressed from a call site (passing `undefined` still sends `0`). Fix lives at the `LanguageModelV1` boundary: `stripSamplingParams()`, gated by `supportsSamplingParams()` (older models still accept them).
2. **`Expected 'text' | 'tool_use'` schema failure.** Sonnet 5 runs adaptive thinking BY DEFAULT (omitting `thinking` does not disable it); Fable 5 cannot disable thinking at all (`{type:"disabled"}` → 400). Requires **`@ai-sdk/anthropic ^1.2.12`** (upstream pins `0.0.39`, which predates thinking). Never downgrade below 1.x. (2.x needs `ai@5`.)
3. **`InvalidStreamPart: reasoning-signature without reasoning`.** Thinking blocks default to `display: "omitted"` → empty thinking text + a signature, which `ai@4`'s state machine rejects. Fix: `dropOrphanReasoningSignatures()` wrapping `doStream`, applied to **every** Claude model.

`getModelInstance` composes both wrappers. `capabilities.ts` MUST live outside `~/lib/.server/**` (the provider registry is imported by client code; a `.server` import breaks the client bundle).

**`getDynamicModels` reads the right fields** — the Models API is self-describing: `max_input_tokens` → context window, `max_tokens` → output cap. Upstream conflated them (and used substring heuristics). Fallbacks are deliberately asymmetric: undershooting output truncates, overshooting is a 400 — which is why `PROVIDER_COMPLETION_LIMITS.Anthropic` stays **64000** (a FLOOR, not a ceiling; it is only consulted when a model's cap is unknown).

**Thinking is ON and VISIBLE — and its cost is an explicit decision (full detail: `spec/anthropic-models.md` §3.4–§3.6).** Current Claude models think by default; omitting `thinking` does NOT turn it off. The defect was never that the model thinks — it was that `thinking.display` defaults to **`"omitted"`**: the model reasons, we are billed for every token at the full **output** rate, and the API returns a thinking block whose text is EMPTY. We paid for reasoning and had nothing to show, so a 90s think rendered as a dead spinner (measured: 90.5s of total silence, not even HTTP headers, before the first byte). `display: 'summarized'` costs **nothing extra** and turns those tokens into a stream the user watches (`ThinkingPanel`).
- `@ai-sdk/anthropic@1.2.12` cannot express any of this (`providerOptions` hardcodes the legacy `{type:'enabled', budget_tokens}` — a hard 400 on current models). The **only** seam is a `fetch` wrapper: `thinkingFetch(mode, effort, modelId)`.
- **Reasoning is a separate stream channel (`g:`), NEVER merged into text** — the client feeds `text` straight into the artifact parser, so leaked reasoning would be written into the user's file. It also does not count as "produced output": a generation that only thought and never wrote must still fail and refund (§4.6).
- **Config:** `THINKING_MODE=adaptive|disabled` (default `adaptive`). Flipping it invalidates the prompt cache **once**; that one-off is not a regression.
- **The liveness heartbeat covers the stream the provider cannot silence us out of (2026-07-24, `spec/anthropic-models.md` §3.4a).** `display:'summarized'` only helps when the provider actually returns the text — and KIE's adapter regressed to returning EMPTY thinking text for EVERY model (fable-5 went 224 chars → 0 on a forced 2,980-token think; api.anthropic.com control streams 209 chars with the same body; measurements in `kie-wire.ts`, send-ready report in `KIE_BUG_REPORT.md`), while still billing the thinking tokens. So a long think was minutes of dead dots, indistinguishable from a hang, with credits genuinely burning. The proxy now emits `agent-status` DATA parts on a timer while the model stream is quiet (`agent/heartbeat.ts`, ≥2.5s silent, every 3s: phase + server-clock elapsed + monotonic `seq`), and the client renders a ticking "Thinking — 1m 12s" panel in place of the dots (`stores/agent-status.ts`, `StreamingStatus.tsx`). **It is a liveness signal, never a thinking channel**: data stream only (never `text`/`g:`, zero tokens), it never fabricates reasoning, real content resets its quiet clock (so when KIE fixes their side, real thinking streams through the untouched reasoning pipe and the heartbeat stands down with no code change), empty deltas do NOT count as activity (KIE streams `""` thinking deltas), and the client gates replay on `(generationId, seq)` because `useChat` re-presents the data array every chunk. Pinned by `heartbeat.spec.ts` + `agent-status.spec.ts`.
- **The panel also says what to EXPECT, because a ticking clock alone is pure anxiety (2026-08-03, `agent/delivery.ts`, `scripts/stream-probe.mjs`).** Measured: KIE does not merely buffer the *thinking* window — it buffers the **answer**, delivering 26,539 chars 100% in the final second after 130s of silence, identically with thinking disabled and with no thinking fields at all (3/3 request shapes, `claude-opus-5`). So on a first build turn the user watched ~4 minutes of nothing and then received 46KB at once, on a generation whose step log read a perfectly healthy 69 tok/s. **No server-side total can see this** — "streamed for 244s" and "buffered and flushed at 244s" produce identical durations and token counts — which is why the per-delta probe is committed beside `cache-probe.mjs`. The heartbeat therefore carries two more facts it already holds: `deliveryMode` (**keyed by provider + model FAMILY since 2026-08-04, and never by model** — the buffering is in the ADAPTER, and KIE runs three of them: the same key and account measured KIE-claude batched and KIE-codex/gemini streaming, so provider alone can no longer answer the question; a family is a property of the endpoint, a model id is not) and `typicalMs`, and the client renders an expectation bar that **never fills** plus a sentence naming the batching. **A provider we have not measured is assumed to stream** (claiming `batched` would manufacture the despair this exists to prevent), the note is never shown on a streaming provider, and it suppresses the stall clause rather than restating a symptom beside its own explanation. Live-driven on the real UI; the underlying defect is KIE's, and `LLM_PROVIDER=Anthropic` remains the config-only escape hatch at ~2.3× cost.

**Effort — the dial that bounds spend, and the floor beneath it.** `output_config.effort` (GA) **defaults to `high` server-side**, so never sending it is not "no opinion" — it silently buys the second-most-expensive setting on every generation. Setting it to `medium` cut a creation from $0.232/103s to $0.200/80s with an identical deliverable.
- **`low` is REMOVED from the `EffortLevel` union — it is a correctness bug, not a discount.** It looked free on creation turns (22% cheaper, full-quality output), but on a real **edit** turn it wrote to `src/routing/router.tsx` — READ-ONLY SHELL (§4.4c) — and rewrote whole files instead of patching them, where `medium` created `src/scripts/BoostController.ts` in the correct zone with 5/5 clean diff blocks. An under-thinking model does not return a smaller correct answer; it returns a confident wrong one, and the file zones are the first constraint it drops. `parseEffort()` clamps a literal `THINKING_EFFORT=low` back to `medium` (a `.env` file is a string file; a cast cannot stop an operator) and rejects typos rather than 400-ing mid-generation.
- **Per-turn policy (`effort-policy.ts`) decides by turn KIND, never by reading the prompt** — a prose classifier is wrong in both directions, undebuggable when a bill doubles, and puts a language model in charge of spend. It **only ever escalates**: repair attempt 1 → `high`; repair attempt 2 → `xhigh`; `/slash` skill invocation → `high`; **creation and ordinary edits → the operator default (`medium`)**. Precedence is **policy > `THINKING_EFFORT` > `medium`** — a build that has already failed twice is not the place to economise, and repairs are capped (`MAX_REPAIR_TURNS = 2`) so the escalation is bounded.
- **The USER may raise the floor, and only to `high` (§4.2.9, 2026-07-27).** `/effort` sets a session-scoped base effort — `medium` (default, every session, every reload) or `high` — which `effortForTurn` takes as a **floor**: escalation still fires above it, and it never caps or lowers anything. Only those two are offerable; `xhigh`/`max` are what the ladder spends on evidence, and `parseUserEffort` refuses them at the boundary because this value arrives in a browser body, on the platform's credit pool. It is not a prose classifier — the user is choosing a visible session floor up front, not describing the turn.
- **Config, never hardcoded:** `THINKING_EFFORT=medium|high|xhigh|max` (`DEFAULT_EFFORT` in `capabilities.ts`). There is nothing below `medium` to drop to.

**Verification is mandatory** (these bugs are invisible to typecheck): `anthropic.spec.ts` asserts on the **serialized request body** (stub `fetch`, parse `init.body`) and drives the **real `ai.streamText` pipeline** with a replayed SSE stream containing an empty thinking block + `signature_delta` and NO `thinking_delta` (the production shape). Prove each test fails without its fix before trusting it.

**Restricted models:** Fable 5 requires access; Mythos-tier models are not publicly available. Include Fable 5 in the table but never make it a default or a required path. It was the §4.6.1a **SuperMax** rung until 2026-08-08 and is the **Platinum** rung's default model since 2026-08-10 (`PLATINUM_MODEL`), and it stays opt-in, threshold-gated and resolving DOWN to Standard whenever it cannot be served. ⚠️ This said it was "reachable only if an operator points `PREMIUM_MODEL` at it — which the owner's deploy does" until 2026-08-11; the owner's deploy points `PREMIUM_MODEL` at `claude-opus-5` and fable-5 is reached as the Platinum default.

**Ops notes:** use **pnpm** (`npm install` dies on this lockfile); `rg` skips hidden dirs — always `rg --hidden` or the entire `app/lib/.server/` tree is silently missed.

**Outstanding (tracked, not blocking):** the Amazon Bedrock provider still ships retired models and legacy ARN-versioned IDs; migrating it is a real integration change (different client + request shape), not a find-and-replace.

**Prompt constraints baked into every version:** Toolkit + BabylonJS only; ESM/ES6 style enforced; **TypeScript over JavaScript; WebGPU preferred over WebGL** (`project-installer.md` global rules); **work within the React Framework's conventions — game flow lives in GameMode classes (wired through unified navigation: `gameMode` + optional `sceneUrl`) and per-object behavior lives in Script Components with the toolkit's full lifecycle (awake/start/update/late/after/step/fixed/ready/destroy), registered via `TOOLKIT.SceneManager.RegisterClass`; never ad-hoc scene bootstrapping or logic outside these constructs unless asked**; **FILE ZONES (§4.4b/§4.4c): author game code in `src/scripts/` and frontend in `src/pages/`+`src/components/`; `src/babylon/classes/**` (demo source library), `src/babylon/system/**` (framework internals), `app.tsx` (routing shell) and `src/routing/**` are READ-ONLY — read for reference, never modify; to change a demo class, copy it into `src/scripts/` first**; **PLAY CONTRACT (§4.4c): gameplay is entered ONLY via `navigate('/play', { gameMode, sceneUrl? })` with a REGISTERED mode class — the frontend may be redesigned freely (title screens, track/car select, options → Start), with or without a play button on the landing page, but this call must never be broken, stubbed, or bypassed**; **read the user's prompt for FRONTEND/landing-page intent, not just gameplay intent, and honor it**; **BATTERIES-INCLUDED RULE: always prefer the Toolkit's built-in systems over custom implementations** — StandardPlayerController/ThirdPersonPlayerController for characters, the RacingSystem (Need-for-Speed-style, e.g. StandardCarController) for driving, TOOLKIT.CharacterController, NavigationAgent (Recast/Detour), AnimationState (Mecanim-style), the Havok joint suite, DefaultCameraSystem (incl. split-screen 1–4 and WebXR), MobileInputController for touch, AudioSource, and the Colyseus multiplayer stack — writing custom versions of these from scratch is a generation-quality bug; never restructure the starter's Vite/React scaffold unasked; **never modify the starter's `vite.config` `optimizeDeps` exclude list or `dedupe` settings** (they prevent the Babylon dual-instance hazard); keep the project runnable; three.js only on explicit user request as a utility; fix surfaced build errors before new features.

### 4.3 Doc-Sync Subsystem (system prompt build, refresh, caching)

**PLATFORM IDENTITY — we are a NEW HOST PLATFORM in the Agent Reference's Platform Detection Table:**
`project-installer.md` defines a BLOCKING platform-detection procedure (Lovable / Replit / Bolt.new / Base44 / V0 / Generic), each mapping to a platform-specific reference doc. Our builder is a **distinct host platform** — a bolt.diy fork with its own runtime, file zones, and creation flow. **Action (owner, agent repo):** author a platform row + reference doc (e.g. `references/web-app-builder.md`) covering: AppTemplate clone (self-contained — `src/babylon` is vendored, NO submodules), remote-origin removal, ES6 package set, our §4.4b file zones (`src/scripts` write zone; `babylon/classes` + `babylon/system` + router read-only), the §4.4c play contract + bundle-integrity rule, and the required `public/` binary assets. Until it exists, bake **Generic** (`web-app-generic.md`) behavior. **Baked-prompt corollary:** the detection procedure's "fetch the doc" step is satisfied at doc-sync time, not per generation — the agent must NEVER be instructed to fetch platform docs at runtime (§1.3 principle 3).

The Agent Reference repo (`github.com/babylontoolkit/agent`) and skills repo (`github.com/babylontoolkit/skills`) remain the single editable sources of domain knowledge — **no changes to how those docs are authored or organized**. The platform consumes snapshots:

1. **`buildSystemPrompt()`**: fetch root `reference.md` + linked sub-docs — Modern ES6 (`node-esm.md`, primary), `scene-components.md`, `react-framework.md`, `ui-design-system.md`, `training-reference.md`, **`project-installer.md`**; `shader-materials.md` held as an on-demand block; **the Component Reference tree (`training/components/README.md` overview baked; the 14 system docs — SceneManager, ScriptComponent, AnimationState, CharacterController, NavigationAgent, RigidbodyPhysics, AudioSource, Materials, InputController, ProComponents, Enums, StarterContent, RacingSystem, GamePatterns — as on-demand cached blocks routed by topic); declaration files (`babylon.toolkit.d.ts`, `default.playground.d.ts`) synced for editor IntelliSense (§4.1) and available to the agent on demand**; Classic UMD **excluded** (platform is ESM-only) — plus the **skills index** (name + description + when-to-use per active skill, §4.11); validate (all expected docs present, non-empty); concatenate with platform sections (action protocol rules, hard constraints, self-healing directive, skill-usage directive); store as `prompt_versions` row: `id, content, content_hash, source_commit_sha, created_at, is_active`.
2. **Activation:** exactly one active version; generations read it — never a live GitHub fetch. User generations have zero network dependency on GitHub.
3. **Refresh triggers:** admin endpoint `POST /api/admin/prompt/refresh` (Phase 2; curl-able before the dashboard exists); GitHub webhook on push to `main` of the agent/skills repos → auto build + activate on success (Phase 3). Failed builds keep the previous version active and alert the admin — a broken doc push can never take down generation.
4. **Rollback:** reactivate any previous version (one click).
5. **Anthropic prompt caching:** assembled prompt sent as cached prefix (`cache_control`); byte-identical between refreshes → nearly every generation pays the reduced cached-input rate on the largest context chunk. This is a primary margin lever. Verify current cache block limits/TTL at docs.claude.com.
6. **On-demand blocks** (shaders) appended as separate cached blocks only when keyword-routed in, keeping the base prefix stable.
7. Every `generations` row records `prompt_version_id` → cost/quality regressions traceable to doc changes.

### 4.4 Starter Template & Game Registry (template-first, GameMode-centric)

- **One universal starter:** `github.com/babylontoolkit/AppTemplate` — the Babylon Toolkit React Framework scaffold (ES6, MIT, template repo; Vite + TypeScript; unified navigation with a `/play` route taking `gameMode` + optional `sceneUrl`). **Default project origin** (the promoted New Project path; there are no per-genre repos). **Advanced paths retained from upstream:** blank prompt-driven start (agent scaffolds per `project-installer.md`; UI notes it's the less-reliable path), git import, folder import — power users bring their own Toolkit-shaped projects; the wizard and non-developer funnel always route through the registry.
- **Genres are registry DATA, not repos.** The `game_registry` (replaces the old multi-repo `templates` table concept; the table can keep the name) rows are: `id, title, genre, source_class (the file in src/babylon/classes/ to COPY — §4.4b), scene_url (optional), match_keywords text[] (§4.4a prompt seeding), thumbnail_url, wizard_config_ref, toolkit_version, is_active`. (The project's actual GameMode class is the renamed copy in `src/scripts/`, not the library source.) Adding a genre = adding a GameMode preset (+ optionally a hosted scene) — no new repo, no new snapshot pipeline.
- **Two-tier gameplay architecture (the agent's output taxonomy):** the **GameMode** is the main controller — it defines game flow and may load any assets it wants (`scene_url` is an optional convenience preload the framework performs before the mode runs; Unreal-style). **Script Components** carry per-game-object behavior — MonoBehaviour-like classes with a Unity-like lifecycle, attached to game objects (player controllers, pickups, hazards, UI hooks). Typical generation: new/modified script components + GameMode wiring. The "blank canvas" option is simply a minimal default GameMode with no scene — full creative freedom on a scaffold that always compiles.
- **Project setup hygiene (mandated by `project-installer.md`/`web-app-bolt.md` — the template-snapshot pipeline MUST bake these in):** **remove the git remote origin** so user projects are never wired to our starter repo; `.gitignore` exists and contains `node_modules` + `.env`; `node_modules` never enters a snapshot; never overwrite a user project's `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md`/`.github/copilot-instructions.md`; apply the mandated `vite.config` (dedupe, optimizeDeps excludes, COOP/COEP headers, media-MIME + gzip plugins, warmup), `tsconfig.app.json` (`skipLibCheck`, non-strict) and `eslint.config` relaxations; **remove React `StrictMode`** (prevents double window init); ES6 packages only (`@babylonjs/*` + `@babylonjs-toolkit/next`).
- **The starter is self-contained — no git submodules (verified against `babylontoolkit/AppTemplate@main`).** `src/babylon` (the React Framework) is vendored directly in the template. This matters because a GitHub zipball NEVER contains submodule content — it records only a gitlink (mode `160000`) — and WebContainers cannot run `git submodule`. The template route still vendors gitlinks generically (resolving `.gitmodules`, pinned to the referenced commit) so a submodule-bearing template is not silently mounted half-empty, but **the starter must never depend on that path**: a template that needs a submodule step is a template that will mount broken. **Kit requirement:** keep `src/babylon` vendored in-repo.
- **`public/babylon.png` + `public/spinner.png` MUST exist in every project (framework requirement — do not "optimize" this away).** They are ALSO ESM-imported by the preloader (`src/chrome/loading.tsx` does `import babylonLogo from '../assets/babylon.png'` — which, with `custom/` at top level since 2026-07-18, resolves to `src/assets/`), so they exist in multiple places on purpose: bundled copies under `src/assets/` and `src/babylon/assets/`, **and** a runtime copy under `public/`. The starter does not ship the `public/` copies, so **project creation copies them into `public/`** (`mount-tree.ts` `withFrameworkPublicAssets` — sources `src/babylon/assets/` first, falling back to any `assets/` copy, so the template reorganisation did not break it) and verifies both are present before declaring the project ready — along with verifying that no imported binary is absent. These two files were the observed casualties of the binary bug and are the canonical smoke test that the file layer is honest.
- **PIN-AND-CACHE — BUILT (Stage 5, 2026-07-16); the divergence below is CLOSED.** New projects now mount a **pinned, SHA-addressed snapshot from object storage**, not live `main`. A snapshot is fetched once and stored immutably at `templates/snapshots/<repo>/<sha>.json`; the pin (`templates/pins/<repo>.json`) records the sha, the ref it came from, when, and by whom. Creation therefore makes **no GitHub call at all** once pinned, and a push to `main` cannot reach a single user until someone promotes it. Pieces: `templates/pin.ts` (keys, pin/snapshot IO, and `decideTemplateSource` — a PURE, exhaustively-tested decision core, `pin.spec.ts`), `templates/fetch.ts` (the fetch lifted out of the route so promotion and bootstrap share it; every fetch now targets an explicit **commit SHA**, which is what makes a snapshot reproducible), `templates/config.ts` (`TEMPLATE_PINNING_ENABLED`, default ON — set false only to develop the starter itself against live `main`), `api.admin.template.ts` (GET pin + rollback menu; POST `promote` / `rollback`, session-`requireAdmin`), and the **Starter template** section of the Admin settings tab (promote-latest + one-click rollback, both confirmed).
  - **The decision rules that must not regress** (each is a silent failure): a **broken mount (`?fallback=1`) outranks a healthy pin** — the server cannot see a runtime-broken WebContainer, so honouring the pin there traps the user on the same bad bytes forever; a pin whose **snapshot object is gone** serves live but **never re-pins** (auto-re-pinning to today's `main` is exactly the unreviewed jump pinning exists to prevent, performed at the moment nobody is watching); an **auto-pin happens only on a clean bootstrap** (pinning on, no pin, not a fallback) and is marked `pinnedBy:'auto'` so an admin can see it was never actually reviewed; **promotion validates BEFORE re-pointing the pin** and leaves the old pin untouched on refusal; **rollback only accepts a snapshot that is still in the store**.
  - **The release-lock footgun is DEFUSED.** `resolveTemplateRef` resolves the **default branch** deliberately and never consults `releases/latest`; publishing a Release on AppTemplate now changes nothing until someone promotes it by name. The pin is the only thing that decides what users mount.
  - **🔴 The model was building the game BLIND — fixed 2026-07-16 (`waitForMountVisible`).** Creation wrote the starter to the WebContainer (awaited; the bytes were on disk) and fired the generation immediately. The model reads `workbenchStore.files`, which a watcher fills asynchronously — measured live, the request went out at **5405ms with 7 files** and the store filled at **5531ms with 78**. So the most expensive generation in the product ran without `src/scripts/<Class>.ts` (the class §4.4b had just scaffolded for it), without `src/babylon/classes/`, without `globals.ts` — silently falsifying §4.2.8's "everything the agent must READ stays fully visible". It reported itself in the product (*"I can't see its source"*) and was read as caution. Nothing threw; the token count went DOWN. Now `startProject` waits for the store to show the exact files §4.4b produced (sentinels, not a count) before generating, degrading after 15s rather than hanging. The wait must stay at the END of `startProject`: the AI SDK sends only committed render state, so waiting inside `createProjectFromRegistry` fixed the files while leaving `projectId: undefined` on every creation. Pinned by `mount-visible.spec.ts`.
  - **🔴 CREATION IS UNCONDITIONAL, AND POST-CREATE MAY NEVER TAKE IT DOWN — owner rule, BUILT 2026-07-22.** New Project is **two phases**, and the second one's failures are bad states of a project that exists, never a lost project:
    - **Phase 1 — CREATE.** Clone the AppTemplate, mount it, verify it on disk, register it with the platform, then `npm install` and `npm run dev` until the stock starter page is live. **No model is contacted**, so nothing a model does can decide whether the user ends up with a project. **Exactly TWO things may fail a creation**, because without either there is nothing at all: the starter fetch, and the mount (`mountTemplate`'s `package.json` sentinel — a mount that did not land must stay LOUD). Since 2026-07-29 there is a third, deliberately different, refusal: the `PROJECT_CREATE_CREDITS` charge, which is taken **before anything is provisioned** and therefore leaves nothing half-made (§4.4a, §4.6).
    - **Phase 2 — POST-CREATE. ⚠️ Superseded 2026-07-29 (§4.4a): phase 2 is no longer part of creation — it is the FIRST BUILD TURN, and the user sends it.** The words below still describe the work and still bind, but the trigger changed: creation stops with the starter running and the user's prompt carried into the textbox; their send is what runs the brief. The AI carries out the brief: the §4.4c landing page and chrome (now conditional and model-decided), any §4.16 generated art, and the user's actual request. Every failure there — a dead image render, a generation that errors mid-stream, a model that writes nonsense — is **one prompt away from fixed**, which is now literally true rather than a design goal: the project already exists and already runs, so a failed build turn is a turn the user retries. (Historically `startProject` fired this generation itself and wrapped it in its own boundary so a post-create failure **still reported success**; that boundary is what the split replaced.)
    - **Everything decorative DEGRADES rather than refusing.** Project hygiene and the §4.4b copy-rename-register are a head start, not the project: each is a pure transform over an in-memory file list, so a throw mounts the template without it. A registry row naming a class the template no longer ships used to cost the user the entire project; it now costs them a scaffolded GameMode.
    - **🔴 VISIBLE IS NOT SETTLED — the build waits for the mount to go quiet (2026-07-27, `registry/settle.ts`).** The hard rule is that a project is created FIRST and built SECOND, and `waitForMountVisible` only guarantees the first half's sentinels: it returns the moment those files appear, while the watcher is still delivering the remaining ~70 behind them (on a server sandbox each is a round trip, not a memory write). `settleAfterCreation` closes the gap by waiting for the file map to STOP CHANGING — **quiescence, never a blind `setTimeout`** (too short on a cold VM, pure dead time on a warm one) — inside a **floor** (a watcher that has not started yet is trivially quiet, so without a minimum the check passes before any work has happened — the same shape as the bug above) and a **ceiling** (a tree that never stabilises must not hang the New Project button; §1.3 principle 0, and reaching it is normal and silent). The wait is narrated by its own phase, `creating-settle` → *"Project created / Preparing to build your frontend…"*, because that is the moment the project exists and the UI had been implying it was still being assembled. Pinned by `settle.spec.ts` against an injected clock; mutation-verified (removing the floor fails three tests, removing the ceiling hangs the suite).
    - **⚠️ The degraded path is only safe because the BRIEF DESCRIBES THE DISK.** Mounting while the brief still claims the mode is "already copied, renamed and registered" trades a loud failure for a silent one — the model builds against a class that does not exist and the project compiles, boots, and dead-ends at a blank `/play`, the exact failure §4.4b exists to prevent. The degraded brief instead tells the model to author AND register the mode itself, and `mustBeVisible` drops the file that was never written (waiting on it would burn the full 15s `waitForMountVisible` timeout and then log a false alarm about a partial context). Pinned by `create-project.spec.ts`, mutation-verified.
    - **A fatal creation says WHICH step broke and what to do (`creation-errors.ts`, pure + tested).** The message it replaced — *"Could not fetch the starter template (401)"* — named the template for what is almost always an **expired session**, sending the user to retry a button that cannot work until they sign in. Classified now: 401/403 → session, `isRetryable: false`; 404 → mid-deploy; 5xx → points at **Settings → Admin → Starter template**, because a 500 is only reachable after the pin AND the last-known-good snapshot both came up empty (i.e. nothing is pinned) and an admin promote is the only actual fix; a rejected `fetch` → "could not reach the server" (the request never arrived — offline, server restarted); an empty 200 → "came back empty"; a mount throw → attributed to the WRITE, with `mountTemplate`'s specific message (which file) preserved. The server's own `details` string survives verbatim so a screenshot is debuggable without a server log. Surfaced as BOTH a toast and the persistent error panel — a faded toast is indistinguishable from a New Project button that silently did nothing.
    - **⚠️ `/api/starter-template` does NOT call GitHub** (the route name is inherited and misleading). Since pin-and-cache it serves a SHA-addressed snapshot from object storage, so this is browser → our own server → storage: its realistic failures are SESSION and SERVER failures. Only an un-pinned repo with no last-known-good snapshot falls through to a live GitHub fetch.
    - **The toast does NOT move.** "🎮 Your game is ready" belongs to FINAL COMPLETE (`onFinish`, once the brief has actually been carried out). Phase 1 is a guarantee, not a milestone worth announcing.
    - **What this replaced:** ONE `catch` around both phases, which told a user whose project had mounted perfectly that we *"could not create the project from the starter template"* because something later failed. A failed creation TURN is a failed post-create — by the time any generation can error, the project exists — so the error alert now says so (`handleError`, gated on `creationTurnStore`). Wrong message on a recoverable situation is how a working project reads as a total loss.
  - **Safety net — BUILT (2026-07-14):** a **last-known-good template snapshot** (`app/lib/.server/templates/last-known-good.ts`, tested). Every successful, structurally-valid fetch is persisted to object storage (the same S3/local-FS layer as §4.5.5); a later fetch that fails outright **or** returns an unmountable result (missing `package.json`, or missing vendored `src/babylon/**` — the exact §4.4 breakages) serves the most recent good snapshot instead of failing project creation. A `?fallback=1` query param lets the client force the snapshot when it detects a broken mount at runtime. **It is still load-bearing under pinning** — it is the ONLY signal that bytes which pass every structural check are broken at runtime, and it deliberately outranks the pin. The response carries `X-Template-Source: pinned | live | last-known-good` (plus `X-Template-Sha`) for diagnostics.
  - **`toolkit_version` (registry) is still metadata, not the pin.** The pin lives in object storage per repo, not in the registry row: a genre is a row that names a demo class to copy (§4.4), and every genre mounts the SAME starter, so pinning per row would let two genres silently diverge. If `toolkit_version` should ever gate anything, it must read the pin — not the other way round.
- New Project: mount the starter snapshot into the WebContainer → apply the chosen registry entry (wire gameMode/sceneUrl) → `npm install` → `npm run dev`. **Binary-asset requirement (principle 10 — see `spec/binary-files.md` for the implemented contract):** upstream bolt.diy's file layer is text-oriented and destroys binary bytes at ingest. ALL project-file paths (template mount, git/folder import, snapshots, GitHub sync, restore, share builds, deploys) MUST handle binaries as bytes: fetch as base64/ArrayBuffer, write to the WebContainer via `fs.writeFile` with `Uint8Array`, keep binary content OUT of LLM context and the text editor map (store `isBinary` + size), carry binaries through snapshots as base64 entries in the JSON envelope (`spec/hosting.md` — supersedes the original tar design). Extend upstream's stores additively (§2.1a). Cold-start mitigations: pre-bundled dependency snapshot if WebContainer install times hurt (measure in Phase 1; the Babylon dependency set is heavy).
- **Template dependency pinning (kit requirement; blocks Share/publish §4.8 when violated).** `@babylonjs-toolkit/next` pins its `@babylonjs/*` peers to an EXACT version (e.g. `9.15.0`). Any `@babylonjs/*` package the starter lists with a caret that is NOT in that peer list will float to a newer minor and break the build: `@babylonjs/serializers@9.16.1` imports `__esDecorate`/`__runInitializers`/`__setFunctionName` from `@babylonjs/core@9.15.0`'s `tslib.es6.js`, which does not export them → `MISSING_EXPORT`, `npm run build` fails (dev still boots, so this hides until Share). **Kit requirements:** (a) every `@babylonjs/*` dependency pinned exactly to the toolkit's peer version, (b) `@babylonjs/serializers` added to `@babylonjs-toolkit/next`'s `peerDependencies`, (c) **the starter ships a committed lockfile** so resolution cannot drift on the next upstream publish.
- **🔴 CORRECTED 2026-07-31 — cross-origin isolation is NOT required for Havok, and this line used to say it was.** The claim was: *"Cross-origin isolation is MANDATORY (Havok/SharedArrayBuffer) … our shared-build `/play` origin MUST serve the same headers or published games with physics break."* **It is false, and it had been contradicted by our own shipped code for months** — `entry.server.tsx` drops COEP entirely on CodeSandbox builds (`*.csb.app` sends no CORP header, so `require-corp` blocked the preview iframe outright), and previews with physics work fine. Nobody reconciled the two; `spec/sandbox-codesandbox.md` and `spec/sandbox-seam.md` recorded the COEP drop as a pure *win* and never noticed it falsified a MANDATORY in §4.4.
  - **Measured against the shipping artifacts** (not the docs): `HavokPhysics_umd.js` (67,605 B) contains **zero** occurrences of `SharedArrayBuffer`, `pthread`, `new Worker`, or `crossOriginIsolated`; `HavokPhysics.wasm` is 2,094,566 B — byte-for-byte the size of the `havok.wasm` this repo uses as its binary-fidelity fixture — with no pthread symbols. An Emscripten pthreads build cannot omit that glue. The **Babylon Playground serves no COOP and no COEP**, so `SharedArrayBuffer` is undefined there, and every Havok physics demo on it runs. That is the existence proof.
  - **What Havok actually requires:** **WebAssembly SIMD** (excludes iOS < 16.4), the correct **`application/wasm`** MIME type, and correct **`Content-Encoding: gzip`** for `.gz.*` assets. Those are real and the `/play` origin must still serve them (CloudFront response-headers policy) — the documented real-world Havok load failures are all MIME/bundler/missing-file causes, never isolation. `share.spec.ts` already pins `contentTypeFor('havok.wasm') === 'application/wasm'`.
  - **It is WEBCONTAINER, not Havok, that needs SharedArrayBuffer** — that runtime cannot exist without it, which is the only reason `entry.server.tsx` sets `require-corp` on non-CodeSandbox builds. Read the header as a property of the sandbox provider, never of the game.
  - ⚠️ **And isolation is actively HOSTILE to us where it is on.** Under `require-corp` a cross-origin subresource without a `Cross-Origin-Resource-Policy` header is refused outright, which puts the hosted scene CDN in the next bullet (`repo.babylontoolkit.com`) at risk on any isolated build — a live, unmeasured exposure on WebContainer builds today, and a hard requirement on any provider whose preview origin forces isolation (BrowserPod's does; §8). Babylon has its own recorded case of `require-corp` *breaking* WebGPU by blocking its CDN fetches.
  - **There is no runtime capability check anywhere** — zero occurrences of `crossOriginIsolated` in the repo, no SIMD probe, no physics-capability warning. If physics ever does fail, the user sees a raw exception, not a diagnosis. Net-new code if wanted; nothing exists to extend.
- **Hosted scene dependency:** referenced scenes (e.g., `repo.babylontoolkit.com/...gz.gltf`) are fetched cross-origin by previews (WebContainer origin) and shared games (play domain) — the scene CDN MUST serve permissive CORS and correct content-encoding for pre-compressed `.gz.gltf`, and must be on CDN-class infrastructure since it sits in the serving path of every preview/play that references it (open question).
- The starter repo also carries the multi-platform agent-config files for the "Toolkit Everywhere" reach play (maintained there, not here).
### 4.4b Project Scaffolding — Copy-From-Source Rule (`src/babylon/classes/` is READ-ONLY)

AppTemplate ships `src/babylon/classes/` as a **read-only library of reference/demo sources** (PlayerControllerDemo, VehicleControllerDemo, PlaygroundDemoScene, DefaultGameMode, …). These are **templates to copy from — never files to edit**. Project code is authored exclusively in **`src/scripts/`**.

**Creation algorithm (all New Project paths — §4.4a A/B/C):**
1. Resolve the `game_registry` entry → its `source_class` (e.g. `VehicleControllerDemo.ts`; Blank Canvas → `DefaultGameMode.ts`) and optional `scene_url`.
2. **Copy** that source file from `src/babylon/classes/` → **`src/scripts/<ProjectClassName>.ts`**.
3. **Rename** the class inside the copy to `<ProjectClassName>`, update its `TOOLKIT.SceneManager.RegisterClass` registration string to match, and fix internal self-references. **Update any imports/paths that shift due to the new location — this is a recurring build break, not a footnote.** The demo classes sit in `src/babylon/classes/`, so they import the game manager as `import GameManager from '../globals'`; once copied into `src/scripts/` that path resolves to the nonexistent `src/globals` and Vite fails with `Failed to resolve import "../globals"`. It MUST become `import GameManager from '../babylon/globals'` (and any other `'../…'` path that assumed the `classes/` location is corrected the same way). The system prompt's hard-constraints call this out explicitly.
4. Wire the app's navigation `gameMode` to `<ProjectClassName>` (+ `sceneUrl` if the entry defines one).
5. `src/babylon/classes/` remains **pristine** — a permanent, clean source library for every future copy, and useful read-only context for the agent ("here is how a working vehicle mode looks").

**Naming convention (deterministic; defined here):**
- Take the project title → strip non-alphanumerics → PascalCase each word → append `Mode` if it doesn't already end in `Mode`/`GameMode`.
  - "Shopping Cart Racer" → `ShoppingCartRacerMode`
  - "my racer!" → `MyRacerMode`
  - "Neon Drift GameMode" → `NeonDriftGameMode` (suffix preserved, not doubled)
- Leading digits or empty results → prefix `Game` (`"3D Test"` → `Game3DTestMode`).
- Collision within the project (`src/scripts/<Name>.ts` exists) → append `2`, `3`, …
- Class name, file name, and the `RegisterClass` string MUST always agree.

**Script Components** authored by the agent (per the two-tier architecture, §4.4) also live in `src/scripts/` — same rule: copy patterns from `classes/`, author in `scripts/`. Per `react-framework.md`, `src/scripts/` is the mandated root for new scripts; sub-folder organization within it is at the agent's discretion.

**Hard agent constraint (baked into every system prompt, §4.2):**
- **NEVER create, edit, or delete files under `src/babylon/classes/**` or `src/babylon/system/**`** — these are the read-only demo library and framework internals respectively. Read them freely for reference; modify never.
- **ALL project game code (GameModes + Script Components) is authored under `src/scripts/`.**
- If a user explicitly asks to change a demo class, copy it into `src/scripts/` first and modify the copy; explain that the library is read-only.

### 4.4a New Project — Entry Points & Routing

🔴 **CREATION IS A CLONE, AND THE BUILD IS A SEPARATE TURN THE USER SENDS (owner decision, 2026-07-29 — BUILT).** New Project fetches the pinned starter, mounts it, scaffolds the §4.4b class, runs `npm install` and `npm run dev`, and stops with the stock starter home page live in the preview. **It contacts no model at all**, so nothing a model does can decide whether the user ends up with a project — *"nothing else should be able to stop the project from getting created."* The user's prompt is then carried onto the **handoff card** — byte-exact — for them to send, edit or put aside. Sending it is the **first build turn**, and that turn carries the user's own words plus the machine-written brief appended **hidden** (play contract, scaffolded class, images on disk, the landing/chrome instruction).

Consequences, each of which is a rule rather than an observation:

- **Two charges, two shapes.** Creation carries a flat `PROJECT_CREATE_CREDITS` (default 150) at registration, ledger reason `project_create`, refused **before** anything is provisioned (§4.6, `spec/billing.md`). The first build turn bills cost-derived like any other turn; flat creation pricing on the generation path is RETIRED and `CREATION_FLAT_CREDITS` is refused if set.
- **"Creation turn" is now `isFirstBuildTurn`, a BEHAVIOURAL fact with no pricing in it.** The marker still drives ten protections (the §4.6.1a paid-rung lock, `bt-landing`+`bt-design` preload, sticky-skill suppression, discuss suppression, the bounded media-only tool loop, `offerLoadSkill: false`, `requiresAction`, media-note suppression, the liveness copy, and the client `ModelTierPill`). Detaching billing also closed a latent exploit: a user who typed the marker verbatim used to buy flat pricing on an arbitrary turn.
- **🔴 THE CREATION BRIEF IS RETIRED — THE FIRST BUILD TURN IS AN ORDINARY TURN (owner, 2026-08-08).** During the reliability debugging the hidden machine-written brief was disabled outright and the plain-prompt builds proved MORE reliable: the baked system prompt (`20-hard-constraints.md` — play contract, landing/chrome rewrite rules, Layout law) plus the per-turn file context already carry what the brief restated, and the model designed the landing page and chrome, wired the play button, and generated images without the forced skill preload or the dual-message send. So the whole brief layer is DELETED, not dormant: `buildCreationBrief`/`CreatedProject.userMessage`/`composeNewProjectTurn` (`new-project-send.ts`) are gone, `NewProjectMode` and `projects.creation_handoff` carry `{userPrompt, plan}` only (old rows/clients still sending `brief` have it silently dropped), and `sendMessage` posts every turn — including the first build — as one ordinary `append`. **Consequences:** nothing sends `CREATION_BRIEF_MARKER`, so the server's marker-keyed first-build machinery (`isFirstBuildTurn` preloads, media-only creation policy, `carriesCreationBrief` outcome rules, the §4.4e phase messages) is INERT — retained but unreachable until an owner decision revives or removes it; the landing/chrome instruction's one home is the baked prompt, whose "Landing-page rewrites"/"Chrome rewrites" sections are now explicitly scoped to **the first build-out (or an explicitly requested redesign)** with a stated leave-it-alone rule for narrow edits; the wizard's compiled selections ride as the CARRIED PROMPT itself (shown on the card, editable) since the hidden channel no longer exists; New Project mode survives as the "created, never built" tracker driving the handoff card and the premium lock; and the skill budget was raised (`MAX_SKILL_LOADS` 2→6, `MAX_TOOL_ROUNDS` 6→7) so the model can choose `bt-landing`/`bt-design` and friends itself. Do not reintroduce a hidden machine message on the send path — that is the layer this entry removes. Superseded by this entry wherever the two bullets below (and §4.4c/§4.7 prose) describe a brief riding the first build turn; they are kept as the record of the shape that was removed.

- **🔴 THE FLAG WENT DEAD WITH THE BRIEF, AND TOOK TEN PROTECTIONS AND THE REFUND WITH IT — FIXED 2026-08-14 (`projectOwesBuild`, `isFirstBuildTurnFor`).** The entry above retired the brief deliberately and correctly. What nobody re-pointed was the SERVER's definition of a first build turn: `carriesCreationBrief(messages0)` searches the last user message for `CREATION_BRIEF_MARKER`, and from `dc58da2` onward **nothing sent it**. `isFirstBuildTurn` was therefore `false` on every creation for six days, and it is not a label — it gates the bounded creation tool policy, the skill preload, the completeness pass, `statusKind`, and `owesFiles = isFirstBuildTurn && !discussNote`, which is what makes `isFailedBuildTurn` able to fire. **Measured live, `gen_msswm3qx_u4u2bt`: 416s, 10 steps, 9 tool rounds of `read_file`/`generate_image` under the ORDINARY policy (`maxSteps` 16) with `skillsLoaded: []`, 82 characters of text, ZERO files, `finishReason: 'unknown'` on a final step that burned 195s and returned nothing — settled `completed` and charged 117 credits, `statusKind: "edit"` on a project four minutes old.** Two refund guards existed and neither could fire: `!producedText` was false (the model emitted one sentence), and `isFailedBuildTurn` needs `requiresAction`, which is `owesFiles`. **The fix is that the signal is now a FACT ABOUT A ROW, not a substring of a message** — `projects.creation_handoff` has meant "created, never built" since migration 0016, the agent route already loads the project for its ownership check (so it is free), and it cannot silently stop being sent the way a client-composed marker can. It is also unforgeable, which matters now that the flag decides a refund: everything else on the proxy's request interface that arrives from a browser resolves DOWN when unrecognised, and this one cannot resolve at all. The marker check is KEPT and OR'd, never ANDed — it is the only signal for the saved transcripts of projects built before the retirement, whose rows were cleared long ago. ⚠️ **How it hid is the whole lesson, and it is the cache warmer's again:** `first-build-turn.spec.ts` pins all ten consumers, each twice, mutation-verified, with the flag true AND false — and every assertion is *"given the flag, does X happen?"*. Not one asked whether the flag is ever TRUE in production, because the derivation was a bare `includes()` with no seam. **A test can only assert the behaviour someone wrote down; it cannot notice that the behaviour stopped being reachable.** Now pinned three ways: `project-owes-build.spec.ts` asserts the LIFECYCLE (created → building → built) rather than the signal, the source scan asserts the derivation reads the row AND that the route resolves it, and reverting the derivation to message-only fails the suite.

- **🔴 AND PHASED CREATION IS LIVE — the client half that was never built (§4.4e, 2026-08-14).** `creation-plan.ts` shipped complete on 2026-08-08 with the server plumbing, the monotonic PATCH, migration 0020 and full specs, to fix the measured monolithic failure (`gen_mskc4r0y`: 90,288 chars in one response, the 64,000-token ceiling hit mid-file, **14 `<boltAction>` opens against 4 closes**, no game on disk, 1,162 credits, `completed`). **It had no caller.** `creationPhaseMessage` was referenced by nothing outside its own spec, so every creation kept running as the single turn the phases exist to prevent — the file's own header says so (*"the LIVE path while this plan is inert"*), a comment describing a feature that was built and unreachable. Now: the send starts a plan and the runner posts one turn per phase (`front end → art → game → verify`) via `decideNextCreationTurn`, a PURE decider in the `decideAutoRepair` shape because a `run` spends credits with no user action. **Never regress, each silent:** the phase TASK rides in the server's VOLATILE SYSTEM TAIL (`creationPhaseNote`, placed with `discussNote` past the last cache breakpoint), never in a message — an earlier draft appended it to the user's words, which is visible rather than hidden and therefore not literally the retired brief, but the same idea wearing a better hat, and the history is uncached so it would be re-sent at full rate forever; the latch is ONE comparison (`armedIndex === plan.next`) and a separate null guard was DELETED after mutation testing proved it unreachable, because a redundant guard is worse than none — the next reader tests it instead of the comparison that does the work; a turn only advances the plan if it WAS a phase turn (`phaseTurnRef`), or an ordinary message typed mid-build marches the plan forward and skips a phase the user paid for; the celebration fires on PLAN completion and from inside the settle promise (a synchronous check after an async advance never fires at all — on any build, ever); **auto-repair is disarmed while phases remain but NEVER disarmed as a watch** (`creationPlanActive`, trap 2 — the front-end phase imports art the art phase has not rendered, so Vite is legitimately red between every pair, and repairing it bills the user to fix what the next phase fixes; but the LAST phase's output has nothing after it and is exactly what self-healing is for); the row is the source and the local mode a cache, so the row is written FIRST and a rejected write PAUSES rather than advancing locally; a pause is TERMINAL and is always REPORTED (a plan that stops silently strands a half-built project, which is the failure this replaces); and the plan RESUMES from `plan.next` rather than restarting, on any device, because it lives on the row. **The unregistered-project fallback deliberately keeps the old single turn** — with no project id the server never loads a row, so `owesBuild` is false and `creationPhase` would be ignored: a plan there is four turns billed as four ordinary edits with none of the protections. ⚠️ **NOT yet driven against a live model** — the pure pieces are exhaustively tested and mutation-verified and the wiring is exercised end-to-end in the React harness, but this is the same "correct by construction" state the §4.14 MCP relay was in before live testing found three defects in it. ⚠️ Still owed from `_specs/phased-creation_plan.md`: the plan-level verdict (trap 1 — `describeTurnOutcome` is defined against the shape of the monolithic failure and will approach zero under phases, which is the `wastedOutput` mistake exactly), the derived per-phase output ceiling (trap 3), the progress UI (Stage 5), and the cache measurement for a per-phase toolset (trap 4 — **measure it, do not reason about it from the shape of the API**, which is the `MAX_MEDIA_ROUNDS` mistake).
- **🔴 THE GAME SPLIT WAS BUILT AND REVERTED THE SAME DAY — a step is a BOUND *and* a FAILURE POINT (owner, live 2026-08-14).** Built on the theory that `game` had inherited the monolith's unbounded-output failure (`gen_mstgbuqo_pkhkhi`: 34,192 output tokens, a **5.4-minute step emitting no bytes**, then nothing, `promptTokens: 0` on a ~300k-prefix turn — a provider-side truncation, intermittent, three such steps in 25 generations), it split `game` into a bounded core plus an unbounded systems step. **Watched live, the premise was false:** the front-end and art phases had already taken the two FIXED-SIZE bodies of work out of the reply, so what was left FIT — owner: *"it is working, everything just is in the Game mode stage… and that is kool… can we just keep that little stage thing 3 modes… JUST MAKE SURE IT FINISHES — that is the whole point, unreliable finishes."* Reverted to **`Front end → Art work → Game code`**. ⚠️ **The reasoning error is worth more than the code was:** the split's argument counted only what a phase boundary BUYS (a bound on one response) and never what it COSTS — every boundary is a place the plan can pause and need a person, plus a turn's warm-prefix read and history re-send. Splitting work that already fits trades a bound nobody needed for a stall nobody wants, and the owner's stated priority is FINISHING. `DEFAULT_CREATION_PHASES.length` is now pinned at 3 so a fourth step must be a deliberate act. 🔴 **What made the revert safe is `RETIRED_PHASES`** — the mechanism built hours earlier for `verify` (*"it says step 3 of 4, what happened to step 4 of 4?"*): `game-systems` is retired, not deleted, so the plans written during the hour it was live — **including one mid-build** — collapse back to three steps at the PARSE rather than parking on a step that no longer runs. It stays DECLARED, because `plan.done` keeps the record of what actually ran and `phaseById` must resolve one to render it. **That mechanism is what makes the phase list changeable at all**; without it every edit to the default list strands whatever is in flight. ⚠️ The migration that ADDED the step on the way in (`withSplitGamePhase`) is deleted rather than left dormant — it existed to teach old plans a new shape, and the new shape is gone.
- **THE STEP LABELS ARE UNIFORM, AND THE COUNTER CARRIES NO TOTAL (owner, 2026-08-14).** *"make the step… say `Art work` not just `Art` so things display uniform"* and *"Dont say `Step 2 of 3 - art work` but instead `Step 2 - art work`… no need `of 3` part."* Every row now names a body of work in two words AND SHARES NO WORD WITH ANOTHER (Front end · Art work · Game code — owner: *"they are all different"*); the `id` stays `game-systems` because it is the STORED key and renaming it would make `parseCreationPhaseId` refuse every plan already carrying it, dropping the step silently for any plan whose `game` phase has already run; a one-word row read as a different KIND of thing in the same list, and the line it feeds (`Step 2 — art.`) read as a truncation. The total belongs to the CARD, which draws every row — and it is the half that can be WRONG, because the denominator is the plan's OWN length and a resumed plan legitimately has a different one, so two messages in one transcript could honestly disagree about how long a build is. The ordinal cannot. `creationPhaseMessage` and `describeCreationPlan().step` say it the same way, asserted over the whole card rather than the counter alone — the total was on screen twice, and fixing only the string a test happens to name is how half a rename ships.

- **🔴 A RESCUE PASS REPLAYED THE PROVIDER'S OWN THINKING BLOCKS, AND BEDROCK REFUSED THEM (found live 2026-08-14, `stripReplayedReasoning`).** Reported mid-creation: `ValidationException: ***.***.content.0: Invalid \`signature\` in \`thinking\` block` — a hard 400 before a token, so the build could not start at all. Three passes start a SECOND stream inside one generation (the forced continuation, the unproductive rescue, the creation completeness pass) and each replayed `(await first.response).messages` VERBATIM. Those are the provider's assistant messages, and on a thinking model they open with a `reasoning` block carrying the signature THAT backend issued. Replaying it is safe only if the same backend validates it, and the platform is a gateway CHAIN (Comet → Bedrock) where the second request need not land on the same one. ⚠️ **It surfaced only now because the completeness pass is gated on `isFirstBuildTurn`, which had been dead for six days** — the entry above restored it, and the first thing it did was run this path for the first time since the gateway moved. **A fix that revives dormant code revives its bugs with it, on the most expensive generation in the product.** ⚠️ **The sibling was already right, which is exactly why this looked covered:** `compactHistory`'s `stripReasoning` has stripped prior-turn thinking out of the CLIENT's history since 2026-07-16 (that bug was `signature: Field required` — absent; this one is `Invalid signature` — present and rejected). It operates on `Message`/`parts[]`; the replay path is `CoreMessage`/`content[]`. **One rule, two message shapes, and every fixture in `history.spec.ts` held the shape that already worked** — so the failing shape did not exist anywhere in 6,600 tests. Tool calls and tool results are deliberately left ALONE (a continuation exists because the model was mid-tool-loop; one that cannot see what its tools returned re-runs them, which on the media tools means paying twice), and a message that was ONLY thinking is DROPPED rather than emptied, since `content: []` is itself a 400. Pinned behaviourally with `CoreMessage`-shaped fixtures plus a **default-deny source scan with two controls** asserting that no `(await first.response).messages` anywhere in `proxy.ts` is read unwrapped — because a fourth rescue pass will be written on a bad day by someone copying the one above it. Mutation-verified four ways.
- **🔴 THE HANDOFF IS A CARD WITH A BUTTON, NOT A PREFILLED BOX (owner, 2026-07-29).** The first version prefilled the chat box with the user's words and left a banner describing the situation; the owner's reading was *"it kind of feels disconnected to the initial project creation process."* Text arriving in a box nobody typed into reads as leftover state rather than as the next step, and a panel that only DESCRIBES gives the flow no forward edge. `CreationHandoffCard` replaces the banner (two panels explaining one moment is the stacking problem `NewChatIntro` is already gated against), renders in-flow in the chat column so the workbench and the running preview stay visible behind it, **shows the brief** (Build must never send something invisible), and offers **Build my game** / **Edit my brief** / **X**. It encodes the asymmetry the owner named — *"if it craps during project creation the whole thing is fucked; if something goes wrong with the brief we can easily fix it with a prompt"* — so the heavy step and the cheap one are separated by a deliberate press. Never regress, each silent: **Build goes through the ordinary `sendMessage`** (ten protections hang off that function recognising a first build turn; a private send path has every one of them absent); **`dismissCreationHandoff` is not `exitNewProjectMode`** — X hides the panel while the hidden brief still rides on the next message, and only a SEND clears the mode; **no Build without words** (a card-path creation offers *Describe your game*, which just focuses the box — a Build there would post an empty turn or invent a brief); the words are **persisted on the mode** (`userPrompt`), never in the `cachedPrompt` cookie, which used to leak one project's prompt onto the next visit to the landing page; and **X fills the box unfocused** while Edit focuses it with the caret at the end. The mode is still per-project and cleared **on send, not on success** — a failed build is one the user retries, and the retry must still carry the brief.
- **🔴 THE CARD'S THIRD ACTION IS PLAN, NOT SAVE (owner, 2026-08-09 — replacing the baseline save below).** *"I think I have enough save to GitHub buttons… change that button to `Plan my brief` that does the same thing as `Edit my brief` EXCEPT prefix the prompt with `/bt-plan`. Then I can also edit further or hit enter and it would start creating the PLAN to build the game instead of trying to build the whole thing in one shot."* **Plan is Edit with a prefix** — `planCommandFor` (pure, in `creation-handoff.ts`) composes `/bt-plan <brief>`, the same `onEdit` handler dismisses the card and fills the box focused, and **nothing is sent**: the user reads the composed command, edits it if they want, and presses enter. That is the whole reason it is a prefix rather than a second send path — a card button that spends credits without a send is what Build is for. Two details, each silently wrong the other way: the brief is **TRIMMED here and nowhere else** (`decideCreationHandoff` preserves the exact bytes because that text is what Build sends and what the card quotes, but this is a COMMAND LINE, and a carried prompt starting with a newline — routine out of the landing-page box — puts `/bt-plan` alone on the first line, identical on the wire and reading as a command that lost its argument); and the composition is asserted against the **real `parseSlashInvocation`**, never a string shape, because a command the parser does not recognise falls through as ordinary prose and builds the game exactly as if the button had not been pressed. The Plan button exists only on the `build` branch — there is nothing to plan without words — and the row's SIZE is pinned (`the action row` spec) for §4.1a's reason: a row grows one individually-reasonable control at a time, and nothing in a code review shows you the row. 🔴 **AND IT SWITCHES THE CHAT INTO PLAN MODE** (owner, same day — *"WE NEED To ALSO SWITCH TO PLAN MODE. That is the whole point as well"*): the prefix asks the skill to plan, and §4.2.9's Plan mode (`chatMode:'discuss'`) is what makes the turn READ-ONLY — the server's Plan note, the `skills-only` toolset (no media debits, no MCP writes) and the `NO_REPLAY` mark that routes the reply through the render-only parser, with `_specs/**` as the one write door so the plan file itself lands. **Prefix without mode is a request the pipeline is free to ignore**, so Plan is its own `onPlan` callback rather than `onEdit` with different text — routing it through `onEdit` composes the identical command and leaves the chat in Build, i.e. a turn free to rewrite the project while the user believes they asked for a plan, with nothing on screen disagreeing. Setting the mode is safe HERE and would not be on Build: this sends nothing, so React has committed the state and `useChat` has refreshed its request body long before the user presses enter (`Messages.client.tsx`'s "Build & Apply" needs a per-request `body` override for the mirror-image reason). 🔴 **AND THE FIRST-BUILD EXEMPTION IS GONE, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES IT REAL (owner, 2026-08-09).** `discussModeNote` used to take `isFirstBuildTurn` and return `null` on a creation turn — *the user asked for a game, not an essay, and the whole creation context has already been billed*. Sound when creation was one hidden-brief turn the model was told to build; wrong now, because planning the build BEFORE writing it is something the user can explicitly choose on turn one, and dropping the note there composes a `/bt-plan` command and then runs it with full write access — the read-only half missing, nothing on screen disagreeing. It was also **already inert** (nothing has sent `CREATION_BRIEF_MARKER` since the brief was retired), i.e. dormant code that would have woken up and broken this button the moment the brief or the §4.4e phases returned. Removing it is a no-op today and the difference between a working feature and a silent one later. The concern it was written for moved to where it can be answered: Plan is a mode the user SETS on a toggle labelled with its current state, and `owesFiles` already excuses a discuss turn from producing files rather than failing it. Pinned as an ABSENCE in `first-build-turn.spec.ts` (whose four-consumer wiring scan is now three plus a not-wired assertion) — re-adding the flag is a one-word change that reads like a fix. 🔴 **AND THE LOOP CLOSES — "Build this plan" (owner, same day, `decidePlanFollowUp`).** A `bt-plan` turn leaves NOTHING unapplied (its `_specs/` write is the one write Plan mode performs), so `shouldOfferBuildAndApply` is correctly silent and the flow used to END there: plan on disk, toggle still reading Plan, user needing to know both to flip it and to type a slash command. The reply now offers **Build this plan** → flips to Build and posts **`/bt-execute <plan> ALL`** — the skill's own two-position grammar, not a sentence. The first draft wrote prose naming the file, reasoning that guessing at an argument grammar this repo does not own was the risky side; it is the opposite, because `bt-execute`'s SKILL.md says a missing task id must make it *"list the task ids and ask the user what to run — DO NOT guess"*, so prose does not degrade to "run everything", it degrades to a round trip asking the question this button exists to have already answered (owner corrected it; asserted whole, never by `toContain`, since the string is PARSED). Never regress, each silent: **`_plan.md` only** (a `bt-spec` turn's `_spec.md` is fed to bt-plan next, and offering to BUILD it skips the planning step and spends a build's worth of credits doing it); **ONE button per reply**, decided by one pure function rather than two `&&`s in JSX, with **apply winning** when a turn both proposed a write and wrote a plan (the narrower, cheaper action, and it keeps every pre-existing plan turn behaving identically); and the `append` MUST carry **`body: { chatMode: 'build' }`** — `setChatMode` lands on the next committed render while `append` fires now, so without it the button posts another read-only turn that reads the plan and cannot act on it, billed in full. That override had been load-bearing since "Build & Apply" shipped with **no test at all** — only a comment — and is now pinned for both buttons by `plan-followup-wiring.spec.ts` (mutation-verified: dropping it fails). **What it replaced (kept, because it explains the shape): THE CARD CARRIED THE BASELINE SAVE (owner, 2026-07-29)** — *"we should put some sort of Save project button that at least saves the core project to GitHub, so we can easily reset from"* it — through `useSaveProject`, the header chip's own hook and single `requestSave` writer, never a second thing called saving. That reasoning was sound and the button was still a duplicate of a control one press away in the header; the thing this moment was actually missing was a way to NOT one-shot the build (the §4.4e monolithic-creation failure). Saving is unchanged and unmoved.
- **Layout, all four owner calls in one place:** the three actions share the row and each takes an equal share of the brief box's width (`flex-1`) rather than being as wide as its own label; their captions sit on one line UNDERNEATH, behind a separator, because side-by-side a useful sentence wraps and knocks the buttons out of alignment; the primary is the ACCENT fill (the pale house primary read softer than the bordered secondary next to it, inverting the hierarchy); and the card is FIRST in the chat column, whose top padding is now `--panel-top-gap` — the same variable that places the fixed workbench panel, so the two columns start on one line instead of on two hand-picked numbers that were only correct relative to each other.
- **🔴 THE HANDOFF LIVES ON THE PROJECT ROW, AND THE CARD RETURNS UNTIL THERE IS A BUILD (owner, 2026-07-29 — migration 0016).** Both halves shipped in `localStorage`, which made them facts about a DEVICE rather than about the project: an unbuilt project opened on a second machine showed no card **and sent its first build turn with no creation brief at all** — no play contract, no scaffolded class name, no list of the images on disk. The build still ran and was simply worse, with nothing reporting why (§4.2.8's silent failure mode, where the token count goes DOWN). Now `projects.creation_handoff` (jsonb `{brief, userPrompt}`) is the source and `localStorage` is a synchronous cache read FIRST so the common case still paints without a round trip; the fallback re-checks the project id on arrival, because an SPA navigate can land elsewhere while the request is in flight. **`NULL` is the end state, written when the first build turn is SENT** — never on success, since a failed build is retried and the retry must still carry the brief.
