The brief arrives in a BROWSER BODY and reaches the model, so it is capped on the way in (24k brief / 8k prompt, the `MAX_INSTRUCTIONS_CHARS` reasoning) and anything malformed CLEARS rather than throws — a corrupt handoff is exactly a project that should stop offering to build itself. **And the dismissal is NOT persisted:** until the first build this card IS the state of the project (one action outstanding, nothing else on screen saying so), so `X` means "hide it for now" and the card comes back on reload and on any device. Persisting a dismissal is right for a NAG; this is not one, because it ends by itself the moment the user builds. Verified live: `localStorage` wiped, reload, card back with its brief from the row.
- **🔴 CREATION CHECKPOINTS ONCE, BECAUSE NOTHING ELSE WILL (found live, 2026-07-29).** The server copy of a conversation is written by `checkpointProject` at the END of a generation, and creation no longer runs one — so a created-but-not-yet-built project uploaded NOTHING: `/api/chats` returned `[]`, the sidebar read "No previous conversations" beside the open chat, and a project made on a laptop simply did not exist on the desktop until its first build landed (§4.5.6 says the sidebar lists the ACCOUNT's chats). The old flow hid this: creation ended by firing a generation, and that generation's checkpoint uploaded the transcript as a side effect — a dependency nobody had written down. Fire-and-forget, like the post-generation call: a safety net must never take down the thing it protects.
- **Creation is not "done" until the starter is RUNNING.** The boot splash covers install and dev-server start (`creating-install` → `creating-serve`), bounded and degrading: a ceiling reached is normal and silent, and a slow install never hangs the New Project button (§1.3 principle 0).
- **🔴 THE SPLASH COVERS EVERY DOOR THAT PUTS FILES IN THE WORKSPACE, NOT JUST CREATION (owner, 2026-07-31).** *"Whenever the workspace is actually loading files into the project workspace should be the splash screen."* Creation, import, remix, resume and open all reveal the workbench only once the file map has stopped changing — so a user never watches ~88 files assemble themselves in the file tree, which is the entire purpose of the surface. Three mechanisms, all in `boot-progress.ts` / `settle.ts` / `useChatHistory.ts`: the mount chains `settleAfterCreation` (`MOUNT_SETTLE_OPTIONS`, whose `minCount` is what makes it safe on the branch that restores nothing and leaves the watcher as the map's only writer); an IMPORT is covered by the overlay rather than by `ready` (see the amended reason below); and the import tail lives in its own flag (`importTailActive`), not in the single phase slot it would otherwise strobe against. **The overlay gate is "every phase except `idle` and `failed`", asserted over the declared phase union** — it was twice written as an enumeration of the known doors and a third door walked past both, so a test written against the same enumeration was structurally unable to catch it. Mounts are also deduped per page load (`mountedThisLoad`): the mount effect fires more than once per load and the whole mount was running twice, ~9s of redundant re-scan that only became visible once the splash started narrating it honestly. Live-verified on the real provider.
- **Two moments, two honest sentences — and only ONE of them is a toast.** Creation says *"Your project is ready"* as the handoff card's heading and toasts nothing at all: the card is the surface for that moment, and a toast repeating it puts the same sentence on screen twice while giving the transient copy to the one that disappears. The first build turn says *"🎮 Your game is ready"* — a toast, and only after its queued actions settle (long-lived `start` actions excluded, since a dev server is `running` forever by design).

- **⚠️ THE OVERLAY-NOT-`ready` REASON IS AMENDED FOR THE GIT DOOR (2026-08-02).** The justification recorded above — *"its files replay through the message parser and can only land AFTER the chat renders, so a `ready` gate would deadlock the replay it waits for"* — was TRUE of the artifact-replay import and is **no longer true of a git clone**, whose files now land by direct write before the hand-off (§4.13a). The behaviour is unchanged and still correct, but for a different reason, and the difference matters to anyone editing this: a clone is started from a page where `ready` is **already true** — the user pressed a button in a workspace they were looking at — so there is no full-page `BootScreen` to render into and the cover must come from `WorkspaceSplash`. **Folder import still has the original deadlock reason.** Two doors, one treatment, two independent justifications; neither is load-bearing for the other. This is exactly the kind of recorded reason that quietly stops matching its code, so it is amended rather than relied upon.
- **The clone door has its own phase, and it narrates three stages, not one.** `cloning` ("Reading your repository") → `files` (driven by `restoreFiles`' `onProgress`, no new plumbing) → `settling` (`IMPORT_SETTLE_OPTIONS`, never a re-derived copy or a blind `setTimeout`). The name deliberately does NOT start with `creating-`: that prefix is the `isCreationPhase` family test, and an import is emphatically not a New Project — no §4.4b scaffolding, no creation handoff card. Measured live at 50ms: `coverToggles: 2`, zero uncovered file growth, on both the clone and the subsequent mount.

Three paths by user intent, plus a fourth door that bypasses intent entirely. **The wizard NEVER interrupts a user who typed a specific prompt** — that is a bug, not a feature.

**Path D — Import an existing repository (§4.13a):** the "Clone a repo" button, `/git?url=<repo>`, or a `StarterTemplates` link (which is the second). It answers the same three questions the other paths do, differently: **credits** — it charges `PROJECT_CREATE_CREDITS` like every other door, on **both** sandbox providers, because every one of them provisions a workspace the platform pays for (the one exemption is an import into an already-open project, which would bill twice for one VM); **handoff card** — none, because there is no §4.4b brief and no scaffolded class, so `creation_handoff` is null and the "no Build without words" branch applies; **scaffolding** — §4.4b copy-rename-register does NOT run, since a foreign repository is not AppTemplate-shaped by assumption and scaffolding a GameMode into it would be destructive.

**Path A — Typed a prompt (Bolt-native; most common for returning users):**
1. User types on the New Project screen: *"make me a kart racer where the cars are shopping carts"*.
2. **🔴 NO GENRE INFERENCE (owner directive, 2026-08-04 — this supersedes the keyword-scoring design below).** Every specific typed prompt seeds the **Blank Canvas** fallback entry (generic shell; §4.4b still scaffolds `<Title>Mode` from `DefaultGameMode.ts`, so the play contract stays wired), and the MODEL decides what to build from the request on the first build turn — with the brief pointing it at the `src/babylon/classes/` demos, the Agent Reference, and the pinned asset library (§4.4d) for material. The retired mechanism ranked `match_keywords[]` and shipped a real wrong-starter on its own single-word threshold: *"top down twin stick shooter"* matched `shooter` (owned by First-Person Explorer) and mounted a free-look FPS camera. It was the third keyword-table failure in this codebase (after the skills router and the landing-pass rule §4.4c already bans), and the owner's rule is now uniform: **the prompt is the prompt — don't interfere with or restrict it.** `rankEntries` survives exported for the seed-chip runner-up affordance; it decides nothing.
3. The project is created IMMEDIATELY — **no wizard, no interstitial, no "pick a template" prompt after they already told us what they want** — and their prompt is carried onto the handoff card for them to send (see the project-first rule above). What runs immediately is the CLONE; what the user sends is the build.
4. The genre demos remain reachable through **explicit choice only**: the registry cards (Path B) and the wizard (Path C), which pass their entry directly and never consult `decideSeed`.

**Path B — Clicked a registry card:** project created from that entry → straight into the builder. No wizard. **With words already in the box, BOTH are honoured** (fixed 2026-07-29, reported live): the card picks the entry — better than keyword seeding could — and the typed words are the brief AND the project title. `handleSelectEntry` used to ignore the box entirely, so "type your idea, then click the genre you meant" silently deleted the words, named the project after the CARD, and left the banner telling the user to edit a prompt that had just been deleted. Nothing threw; the only signal was the user's own report. With an EMPTY box nothing is carried (inventing some would put the machine's phrasing in the user's mouth) and the handoff card offers *Describe your game* instead of a Build button.

**Path C — Guided Tour (§4.7):** entered by (i) explicitly clicking **"Not sure what to build? Take the guided tour"** on the New Project screen, or (ii) typing something too vague to act on (see threshold below). Only in these cases does the wizard appear.

**Vagueness threshold (the ONLY automatic route to the wizard):** a prompt that names no genre, no mechanic, and no subject — e.g. *"I want to make a game"*, *"help"*, *"something fun"*. Implementation: no registry match AND no actionable nouns/verbs. Even then, the wizard is **offered, not forced**: *"Want a guided setup, or just start from a blank scene?"* Anything with a discernible subject ("a game about a robot in a maze") is Path A — seed and run.

**Precedence rule (never violate):** explicit user input > inference > guidance. A typed prompt always beats a suggested template; a picked card always beats a matched keyword; the wizard only ever appears when asked for or when there is genuinely nothing to act on.

**Registry data:** `game_registry` rows still carry `match_keywords text[]`, but since 2026-08-04 they power NOTHING on Path A (`decideSeed` never ranks them — see the 🔴 rule above). They survive as data for the seed-chip runner-up affordance (`rankEntries`), hide-don't-delete.

### 4.4c The Frontend & the Play Contract (landing page → full game UI)

AppTemplate ships the landing page pre-extracted at **`src/pages/Home.tsx`** (+ its own `Home.css`); `app.tsx` is a lean routing shell (BrowserRouter + NavAdapter + lazy `/play`). The platform therefore never performs surgery on the router — it rewrites one already-isolated page.

**The shipped `Home.tsx` is also the agent's CORRECT-USAGE EXAMPLE:** it imports `useUnifiedNavigation` (never `GameManager`), keeps Babylon out of the main bundle, and calls the play contract properly. Imitate its navigation pattern; replace its content.

🔴 **WHEN THE REWRITE HAPPENS, AND WHO DECIDES (revised 2026-07-29, §4.4a).** It happens on the **first build turn**, not at creation — creation now ends with the stock starter page live, and the design pass rides on the message the user sends. And it is **conditional**: the hidden brief states the situation (a fresh stock starter, nothing designed yet), a DEFAULT (a game/experience brief gets the full `bt-landing` pass — landing page + `src/chrome/**` chrome — plus the game) and an EXCEPTION (a narrow or non-game request gets only what was asked, leaving the frontend alone). **The MODEL resolves that from the request; there is NO keyword table in this repo, and one must never be added.** That constraint is not stylistic: the hardcoded skills router matched `'ui'` inside "b**ui**ld" and inlined a 24KB design skill on a debugging question while three synced skills were unroutable entirely, and `effort-policy.ts` states the same rule for spend ("decide by turn KIND, never by reading the prompt — a prose classifier puts the model in charge of the bill"). A comment-stripped **source-scan spec with controls** (`no-prompt-classifier.spec.ts`, taint-aware for aliased identifiers) fails any keyword/regex classification of the user's prompt introduced on the creation path. (`match.ts`'s genre seeding was the one written exemption until 2026-08-04, when the owner retired it too — `decideSeed` no longer ranks keywords at all, see §4.4a Path A — so the rule now holds without exemptions; only the vagueness check reads the prompt, and it decides whether to OFFER the wizard, never what gets built.) Everything below applies unchanged **once the rewrite is in scope**.

🔴 **BUILD ORDER — THE FRONT END COMES FIRST, THE GAME LAST (owner decision, 2026-08-08; `20-hard-constraints.md` "BUILD ORDER").** On a first build-out the model designs and renders the frontend shell — art, then `Home.tsx`/`Home.css`, then `src/chrome/**` — **before** it writes a line of gameplay. The rule is about which body of work is BOUNDED, not about what looks impressive first: the front end is one page plus three chrome files, much the same size whatever the game is (measured at 33% of the monolithic run's characters), while the game is open-ended (45% on that run) and scales with the request in a way nothing can predict before the turn starts. **Whatever is written LAST is what a length-truncated reply mangles** — mid-file, closing `</boltAction>` missing, several files welded into one (the `gen_mskc4r0y` failure) — so the fixed cost goes first, where it is certain to fit and certain to land intact, and the unbounded work takes the room that is left. It also degrades better: a finished front end with a short game is one prompt from done with everything intact on disk, whereas a truncated landing page leaves a file that does not compile on top of a game nobody can reach. **The order is only safe because §4.4b scaffolds a registered GameMode at creation** — the class is on disk before any model turn, so the landing page can wire `navigate('/play', …)` against its real name (read off the file, never invented) with no gameplay written yet; the front end then becomes the *specification* the game step must deliver. **This reverses the phase order recorded in `creation-plan.ts`** (`game → frontend → art`, argued as "what survives a failure" — reasoning that treated a cut-off run as terminal when a plan resumes); that constant is reordered to match, but note it is INERT per §4.4a (nothing sends `CREATION_BRIEF_MARKER`), so the **baked prompt is the live path** and the two must never disagree. Pinned by `creation-plan.spec.ts` (order, the art↔frontend adjacency, the fence in each direction). ⚠️ **A prompt-section edit does nothing until doc-sync rebuilds and an admin PROMOTES the new version** (Settings → Admin) — the sections are `?raw` imports assembled into an immutable `prompt_versions` row, and activation is a separate step.

**FULL REDESIGN — TOTAL REPLACEMENT, NOT AN EDIT.** `Home.tsx` and `Home.css` are OVERWRITTEN with a landing page designed from scratch for THIS game. **Nothing from the starter page survives** — no hero/logo montage, no "React + Vite + BabylonJS" heading, no demo buttons, no Documentation/Asset-Library links, no Vite-community "Connect with us" section, **no footer, and NO Babylon Toolkit / BabylonJS / Vite / React attribution or branding of any kind.** The starter page is scaffolding to be thrown away; the user's game gets its own front door (theme per §4.4a; honor any landing-page instructions in the prompt). The ONLY thing carried forward is the navigation PATTERN (`useUnifiedNavigation` → play contract, §4.4c), not any markup, styling, copy, or link.

**LAYOUT LAW — full-bleed by default, ALWAYS responsive (non-negotiable, applies to EVERY UI surface).** This binds the landing page, `src/components`, and the game chrome in `src/chrome/**` alike; a violation is a generation-quality **defect**, not a taste call, and is enforced in the hard-constraints section of the baked prompt (`20-hard-constraints.md` "Layout law"). Two rules: **(1) FULL-PAGE-WIDTH BY DEFAULT** — the design fills the viewport edge to edge; do not wrap the page in a centered fixed-width column (`max-width: …px; margin: 0 auto`, a `.container`, a hard `width: 960px`). Root/section containers are `width: 100%`; backgrounds/heroes/nav are full-bleed. Inner *content* may still cap its own line length for readability **inside** a full-bleed section. A boxed/fixed-width layout is built **only when the user explicitly asks for one**; absent that instruction, full-bleed wins. **(2) ALWAYS RESPONSIVE** — every layout adapts cleanly from ≈320px phones to ≈2560px desktops with **no horizontal scroll at any width** and nothing clipped or overlapping: fluid units (`%`/`vw`/`dvh`/`rem`/`fr`/`clamp()`), fl*ex*/grid that reflows (`flex-wrap`, `auto-fit`/`minmax`), `@media` breakpoints that restack multi-column → single-column on mobile, `max-width: 100%` on `img`/`video`/`canvas`, and the starter's `<meta viewport>` kept intact. Responsiveness ships in the SAME generation as the design — never a deferred polish pass.

**ASSET-IMPORT HYGIENE (prevents the exact "Failed to resolve import" crash seen in Phase 1):**
- The starter's landing-page images live in **`src/assets/`** (`hero.png`, `player.png`, `react.svg`, `vite.svg`) — these are what the shipped `Home.tsx` imports. **Use whichever ones the new design genuinely calls for — and simply DON'T IMPORT the rest.**
- **NEVER DELETE image files from disk.** Unused assets stay in `src/assets/` (and `public/`); they cost nothing and may be wanted by a later redesign. **`public/babylon.png` + `public/spinner.png` are framework-required** (§4.4), as are their sources in the read-only framework zone (`src/babylon/assets/`, ESM-imported by the preloader): never delete either copy. Removing an import is not permission to remove a file.
- **Only import images that provably exist** — files already in the project, or ones generated this session (e.g. via the project's `.mcp.json` image servers, §4.14). NEVER import an asset path you did not verify or create.
- After rewriting, **zero unresolved imports** may remain — Vite fails hard and the preview goes blank.

**The play contract (the ONE invariant):**
Launching gameplay = calling the framework's unified navigation:
```ts
navigate('/play', { gameMode: '<RegisteredModeClass>', sceneUrl?: '<optional scene>' })
```
This call is an **API the frontend invokes**, not a button the frontend must contain. The agent may restructure the entire frontend freely — the contract is only that gameplay is entered through a valid `navigate('/play', …)` with a **registered** GameMode class (§4.4b) and, where used, a resolvable `sceneUrl`. Never break, stub, or bypass it; never route to `/play` with an unregistered mode.

**After-action questions (mandated by `project-installer.md`):** after project creation the agent offers next-step suggestions ("Create a new game mode?", "Design a user interface?"). The platform renders these as clickable chat suggestions — natural onboarding for non-developers.

**First-build-turn landing page (scaffolding, not the final product — renamed from "creation-time" 2026-07-29, §4.4a: creation ends with the STOCK starter page live and this pass rides on the first message the user sends):**
- Overwrite `src/pages/Home.tsx` + `Home.css` with a landing page designed from scratch for this game — nothing from the starter page survives (no hero montage, no demo buttons, no Vite/React/Docs links, no footer, no Toolkit/BabylonJS attribution or branding). Styled with the Toolkit UI design system (`ui-design-system.md`, already in the prompt bake).
- Wire ONE primary play action to the project's own GameMode + the registry entry's `scene_url` — **but the page MAY define N play actions** (e.g. "Race" / "Free Roam" → different registered modes), and a design MAY have **no play button at all** if the flow reaches `/play` from deeper UI.
- Theming per §4.4a routing: card-created → registry vibe defaults; prompt-created → themed to the game's subject; **landing-page instructions present in the prompt → honored explicitly** (the system prompt directs the agent to LOOK for frontend/landing-page intent, not only gameplay intent — e.g. "dark minimal landing that just says ENTER").

**First-build-turn chrome — splash, preloader, overlay (scaffolding, like the landing page; same rename, same reason):**
**The full landing-page + chrome redesign PROCEDURE lives in the `bt-landing` skill (2026-07-18):** the brief delegates to it (with a fallback to the baked "Layout law"/"Chrome rewrites" prompt sections while the skill is absent from the synced repo), the **first build turn** preloads `bt-landing` + `bt-design` (`preloadSkills(slashSkill, isFirstBuildTurn)` — the pair is the constant `CREATION_SKILLS`, keyed off the brief marker, so it rides the turn the user sends rather than creation), and the user can re-run `/bt-landing <new brief>` any number of times to redesign the whole frontend shell until they like it — each run replaces the design; the invariants in this section bind every run.

The landing page is not the only starter surface that ships Babylon-branded. On a new project the agent also redesigns the game's **chrome** in `src/chrome/**` to the SAME design — the starter's default splash, preloader, and overlay carry the Babylon logo/spinner and placeholder copy, and §2.3 forbids that branding reaching the user's game exactly as it does on the landing page. This is a **restyle that preserves wiring**, not a free-for-all — each surface is functional:
- **Preloader** — `src/chrome/loading.tsx`. Shown before the app mounts. It **re-exports `babylonLogo`/`spinnerLogo`** that `splash.tsx` imports — a rewrite keeps those exports (or updates `splash.tsx`'s import). `public/babylon.png` + `public/spinner.png` stay (framework-required, §4.4), whatever the new design displays.
- **Splash / loading screen** — `src/chrome/splash.tsx` + `splash.css`. Shown while the 3D scene loads. **Keeps the `GameManager.EventBus` `"OnLoadProgress"` subscription and its status text** — real load progress, not decoration.
- **Initial overlay** — `src/chrome/overlay.tsx` + `overlay.css`. The in-game HUD layer, shipped as a placeholder. Gets a minimal, themed starting point (a title/brand corner, a frame); the full HUD grows later with the gameplay features (§4.4c growth path). **Keeps `pointer-events: none` on the container** so input reaches the canvas — only genuinely interactive elements go `pointer-events: auto`.
- **Bundle-integrity nuance:** unlike `src/pages`/`src/components` (which must stay Babylon-free — see below), `src/chrome/**` renders in the viewer context, so it MAY import `GameManager`/`EventBus` for game data and `useUnifiedNavigation` for navigation (`splash.tsx` already imports `GameManager`). The read-only list below does NOT include `custom/**` — it is a write zone.

**Growth path — the frontend is a first-class generative target:**
Landing page → full game frontend is expected and supported. The launch MECHANISM never changes; what changes is WHO computes its arguments — a hardcoded button at first, a menu flow later. A racing project may evolve into: title screen → track select → car select → race-type/options → **Start Race**, where the button computes `gameMode` (e.g. `FreeDriveMode` vs `TimedRaceMode`) and `sceneUrl` (the chosen track) **from the player's selections** and then calls the play contract. The agent authors these screens in the write zone (`src/pages/`, `src/components/`; game logic in `src/scripts/`).

**Passing richer configuration to the GameMode (chosen car, difficulty, lap count, …) — the channel EXISTS:**
`navigate(path, state)` carries a generic **NavigationState** object; the host nav adapters persist it to `sessionStorage` under `NAV_STATE_STORE_KEY`, and `BabylonSceneViewer` reads it back via `readNavStateStore()`. `gameMode`, `sceneUrl`, and `reloadPage` are simply its known keys — **additional selection data travels in the same state object**, read by the GameMode on start. This is the sanctioned channel: the agent NEVER invents side channels (no `window` globals, no query-string smuggling of game config, no ad-hoc localStorage). Note the framework's deliberate security property: nav state is sessionStorage-scoped, NOT in the URL, so users cannot craft links with a spoofed `gameMode`/`sceneUrl` — do not "improve" this by moving state into the URL.

**BUNDLE-INTEGRITY RULE (framework-mandated; an AI will violate this by default):**
- **React UI code** (pages/components — landing page, menus, track/car select) MUST navigate with **`useUnifiedNavigation`** and MUST NOT import `GameManager` (or any Babylon module). Importing `GameManager` into UI pulls the entire Babylon runtime into the main bundle and destroys initial load performance — the whole reason `/play` is a lazy chunk.
- **Game code** (GameModes, Script Components in `src/scripts/`) uses **`GameManager.NavigateTo(path, state)`**.
- Keep all Babylon imports inside the lazy `/play` route chunk.

**Read-only (never edit — §4.4b zones extended):** `app.tsx` routing shell, `src/routing/**`, `src/babylon/system/**`, `src/babylon/classes/**` (demo sources; the shipped `Home.tsx` content is likewise a *source* to rewrite, its router wiring untouched).

### 4.4d The Synty Prototype Asset Library (pinned manifest — BUILT 2026-08-04, awaiting first export)

> **Status: machinery BUILT + tested; no manifest exists yet** (`repo.babylontoolkit.com/assets.json` → 403 as of 2026-08-04 — the owner generates it from Unity Export). Until one is promoted, generations run without a library and the prompt emits NO block. `_specs/asset-library_plan.md` records the build.

**What it is (owner directive, 2026-08-04):** the owner exports Unity Synty prototype packs — complete levels (military maps, biomes) and character/prop packs (military, street racer, city, apocalypse, …) — as interactive glTF with Toolkit metadata, hosted at `repo.babylontoolkit.com`, described by a master `assets.json`. The model is made AWARE of all of it so a game build defaults to real prototype models: **when the user names no models, source from the Synty library; primitives only when nothing suitable exists there or in a user-named pack.** Awareness, never restriction — *"we don't want to hinder the model's creativeness, but we do want the model to be aware of all the Synty prototype models available."*

**The mechanism (never regress, each fails silently):**
- **PINNED like skills and the starter template** (`assets/library-store.ts`): immutable versions + pointer in the ObjectStore, admin fetch→review→promote in Settings → Admin → **Asset library** (`api.admin.asset-library.ts`, `requireAdmin`), validate-before-write with ALL errors reported, rollback only onto bytes that still validate, unpin deletes the pointer. `fetch-master` is for the operator's EYES and is never machine-applied (auto-applying whatever the bucket serves would hand anyone who can write that object the model's context); its URL is host-locked to `…babylontoolkit.com`. **Generations never fetch the live URL** (§4.3's GitHub rule applied to the bucket).
- **The "Use Asset Library" toggle is PER-USER (2026-08-07, Control Panel → Features, first card, default ON — it was briefly built as an admin platform switch the same day and reworked on the owner's correction: pinning stays admin; whether a given user's games use the library is THEIR choice).** The preference lives with the other feature toggles (`useAssetLibraryStore`, localStorage `useAssetLibrary`, `updateUseAssetLibrary`), rides in every `/api/agent` body (`useAssetLibrary`, beside `chatMode`), and is honored server-side by the pure `assetLibraryIndexForRequest(pref, manifest)` — the ONE call the proxy makes for the block. ON means the library is ALWAYS PREFERRED: the prompt block and the creation brief both instruct that when the user supplies or names no assets — even a bare "make me a kart racing game" — the FIRST step for every model/character/prop/level is to search the library, and primitives are the LAST resort after that search fails. **OFF (an explicit `false` on the wire) means the pinned library behaves as if it never existed for that user's generation — no block, and since the brief's sourcing rule is conditional on the block's presence, nothing about the library can leak into their project.** Absent/undefined/non-boolean reads as ON (an older client that never sends the field keeps the shipped default; the costly direction here is silently degrading a user's game, not serving a free index). The unused inherited FeaturesTab toggles stay inert-and-hidden as before — this card is REAL, which is exactly why its copy may claim to govern generations (§2.3's honest-copy rule). Pinned + mutation-verified in `library-manifest.spec.ts` (`assetLibraryIndexForRequest` describe block — removing the gate fails the explicit-false test); the preference can never invent a library when nothing is pinned (ON + no manifest → no block).
- **NO baked fallback — the degraded state is "no library", and the prompt emits NO block** (`activeAssetLibrary()` → undefined → nothing pushed). Telling the model about a library it cannot enumerate is how invented asset paths ship; degrading to "off" is honest, degrading to "on" invents a capability. The creation brief states both branches conditionally on the block's presence, so the client never needs to know the pin state.
- **Validation refuses** (all errors at once): missing/duplicate pack ids, empty packs/manifest, non-https `baseUrl`, and any asset `path` that is absolute, carries a scheme, or contains `..` — the path-traversal wall, because the model ships listed paths verbatim into game code. **Unknown exporter fields pass through untouched** — the exporter will grow detail (per-object script components, bounds) this code predates.
- **CONTEXT IS THE MONEY PATH (§4.2.8):** only `buildAssetLibraryIndex` reaches the prompt — every pack with kind/count/description plus capped asset-name lists, inside a HARD char budget (`ASSET_INDEX_CHAR_BUDGET`, ~1.5k tokens), deterministic (sorted by pack id — byte-identity across processes, the sorted-skills-index rule). The full manifest NEVER rides in context wholesale. Placement: its own system entry after the starter-files block, before the per-conversation doc blocks (sharedness ordering — it is identical for every user/project/conversation), with **NO breakpoint of its own** (covered by whichever follows; `MAX_CACHE_BREAKPOINTS` untouched). It changes only on PROMOTE — the same accepted one-time warmup cost as a prompt promotion.
- **🔴 OFF MEANS "AUTHOR IT YOURSELF", NEVER "USE WHATEVER ASSET URL IS NEAREST" (owner, 2026-08-08).** The no-library branch used to read *"build with the starter's own content and primitives"* — and **"the starter's own content" IS the demo library**, whose reference documents load `riggedmustang.gltf` and `openterrain.gltf` from the shared playground repo. So the branch, read correctly, authorised reaching for a demo fixture, and a "mario kart clone" duly shipped a Mustang on a grey test map. The toggle chooses between **library** and **build it yourself**; no state of it makes a demo asset the right answer, and OFF is not a degraded mode. The branch now says AUTHOR THE CONTENT YOURSELF (composed primitives, procedural geometry, materials you write), names the four fixtures that must never be substituted, and states that a specific asset URL is loaded only when the user named it. ⚠️ It also states the accurate capability — 3D models are BUILT in code, never generated (the media tools are image/video, and are off on this turn) — because a brief implying otherwise has the model announce assets it cannot produce. Pinned by `creation-brief.spec.ts` (8 tests, mutation-verified: restoring the old sentence fails 5) with two CONTROLS — the old phrasing is banned outright, and the author-it-yourself rule must not suppress the library branch.
- **The brief's sourcing rule is conditional on the BLOCK, not on client knowledge:** "if a Prototype Asset Library block is present in your context → default to it, exact listed paths only; if not → starter content and primitives, never reference a library you cannot see" (`buildCreationBrief`). The Admin panel renders the exact block the model sees ("Show the prompt block").

**Schema v1** (the Unity exporter's target — tolerant by design): `{ version, baseUrl, generated?, packs: [{ id, title, kind?, description?, assets: [{ path, name, tags?, components?, sceneUrl? }] }] }` — `path` relative to `baseUrl`, `components` opaque exporter detail. Tests: `library-manifest.spec.ts` (validation + index budget/determinism), `library-store.spec.ts` (promote/rollback/unpin/degrade).

**Deferred, deliberately:** per-object component detail in context (an on-demand lever if placements need it — measured first, §4.2.8); any `search_assets` tool (a tool round re-processes the prefix; the index makes it unnecessary at current scale).

### 4.4e The "Toolkit systems" preference (built 2026-08-08)

**Why it exists.** An identical *"mario kart clone"* prompt produced, on Fable 5, a Ford Mustang on an open-terrain test map driven by simulation raycast vehicle physics; on Opus 5, hand-written movement that ignored the Toolkit's racing stack entirely. The owner could steer neither: *"there are times when I do want it to use the included interactive glTF script components and then there are times when I need it to be creative itself and make its architecture."* That is a request for a CONTROL — the prompt can state a default, but it cannot know which of those two moods the user is in, and no rewording makes it guess right every time.

**Two root causes, fixed separately** (they are independent, and fixing only one leaves the other):

1. **A genre→class MAPPING in the prompt.** The batteries-included rule listed *"Driving / racing → the RacingSystem (e.g. `StandardCarController`)"* under a heading declaring that reimplementing one of these is *"a generation-quality bug, not a stylistic choice"* — so departing from it read as a defect, on a genre where the handling model IS the game. Now SPLIT: **infrastructure** (physics, animation, navmesh, cameras, input, audio, netcode) keeps that framing because it is plumbing; the **higher-level controllers** are restated as *"a menu, not a mapping"*, with the RacingSystem's simulation DEFAULTS named explicitly (and its tunability — the reference now documents an arcade/kart tuning pass, so the question is "does its feel, as configured, match the request?", never "is this the racing class?"), plus the rule that **the request decides how the game FEELS and the Toolkit decides what it runs ON** — using a built-in, building on one, or authoring movement over `RigidbodyPhysics` are all legitimate, and *picking a controller that fights the requested feel is the real defect*. A companion section states that **a demo's assets are an EXAMPLE, never a default**, naming `riggedmustang`/`openterrain`/`samplescene` directly, because the two reference docs that teach them are authored in `babylontoolkit/agent` and are not ours to edit.

2. **🔴 A MANDATORY RULE THAT DEPENDS ON A DISCRETIONARY, BUDGETED TOOL CALL IS INCONSISTENT BY CONSTRUCTION.** The rule sits in the cached prefix and is read by every model on every turn — but it is *unusable on its own*: it names `StandardCarController` and teaches no API, so using it costs one of `MAX_REFERENCE_LOADS = 3`. "Did the model follow the batteries-included rule?" therefore resolved to "did it spend one of three loads on the right doc?", decided **before it had designed anything**, from a one-line index description, against `react-training`/`game-patterns`/`scene-manager`/`rigidbody-physics`. Measured on the reported creation: `blocksLoaded = ["racing-system", "demo-vehicle-scene", "input-controller"]` — the whole budget on the vehicle path, one of it spent on the doc that supplied the Mustang. Neither model was ignoring the rule; both obeyed as far as a 3-item budget allowed. **Fixed by stating the fallback at the point of use rather than by raising the budget** (a wider budget pulls 5–20k-token docs on every creation, racing or not): the rule now says to load the reference FIRST because the API cannot be written from memory, and that **if the load cannot be afforded, author that part yourself and say which system was skipped** — converting a silent coin-flip into a deliberate, reported choice.

**The control** mirrors §4.4d end to end — per-user, localStorage, in the `/api/agent` body, honored by ONE pure function (`toolkitSystemsNoteForRequest`, `app/lib/agent/toolkit-systems.ts`). Three settings: **`prefer`** (reach for the built-in controllers and the interactive glTF script components), **`auto`** (default — the model judges from the request), **`own`** (author movement, rules and structure from scratch). UI: Control Panel → Features → **Game architecture**, placed after Core Features so §4.4d's owner-chosen first card is not displaced.

**Never regress, each silent:**
- **`auto` emits NO BLOCK.** The baked rule already states the balanced position, so an `auto` note would restate the cached prompt at full rate on every turn forever (§4.2.8). The default must cost zero.
- **An unrecognised value resolves DOWN to `auto`**, never to a stronger setting — the `parseUserEffort` rule. This arrives in a browser body; inventing a preference the user did not choose silently changes what their game is made of.
- **`prefer` carries the load-the-reference-first fallback**, or it instructs the model to write an API it may have no documentation for — which our own prompt correctly calls worse than not using the system.
- **`own` must NOT forbid the infrastructure systems.** Read literally it would mandate writing a physics engine; the block says to keep using Havok/`AnimationState`/`NavigationAgent`/`DefaultCameraSystem`/`InputController`/`AudioSource` and build game logic on top.
- **The parser is shared client+server** (`app/lib/agent/toolkit-systems.ts`, outside `~/lib/.server/**` beside `turn-outcome.ts`) so the two cannot disagree about what a value means — deliberately NOT the `MODEL_TIER_IDS` shape, which declares the same union twice.

Pinned by `toolkit-systems.spec.ts` (13 tests, 3 mutations verified: dropping the value guard, making `auto` emit a block, collapsing the two override branches), with two CONTROLS — the branches are distinct, and exactly one of the three settings is free.

⚠️ **The prompt-section half is INERT until an admin re-syncs and promotes** (the `src/custom` → `src/chrome` trap). The toggle works immediately; the reworded batteries-included rule does not.

### 4.5 Identity, Accounts & Persistence (Supabase)

> **Status: IMPLEMENTED (Stage 3, 2026-07).** Sub-spec: `spec/billing.md` (which also records four deliberate divergences from the original billing design — read it before assuming §4.6 describes the code).

bolt.diy ships **no** user system (local, single-user, browser-persisted). This entire layer is net-new; Supabase Auth is the identity provider so we never store passwords or build token handling ourselves.

**Local mode (Supabase unconfigured) is a REAL, supported mode — not a stub.** The platform resolves to a single verified local developer with filesystem persistence, a working append-only ledger, real ownership checks, and byte-faithful snapshots. This is what made Stage 3 buildable and testable before a Supabase project, an S3 bucket, a Stripe account, or the license service existed (§1.3 principle 0) — and it is the single-user posture bolt.diy shipped with, so local dev behaves as it always did. It can never reach production by accident: `assertNotLocalInProduction` **refuses to boot** into the anonymous local user when `NODE_ENV=production`, because that fallback treats every caller as a verified admin.

⚠️ **The platform Supabase is NOT the one in §4.15 (Game Backends).** That one is the *user's own* project, reached through upstream's connector with `VITE_SUPABASE_*` — values that are public by design. Ours holds accounts, projects and the credit ledger, and its service-role key **bypasses RLS**. Platform secrets are therefore never `VITE_`-prefixed: Vite inlines every `VITE_*` variable into the client bundle, so doing so would hand every visitor every row in the database.

#### 4.5.1 Authentication

- **Methods (launch):** email + password, and OAuth via Google and GitHub (GitHub doubles as groundwork for Phase 4 repo export). Magic-link email sign-in optional flag. All through Supabase Auth — password hashing, token issuance/refresh, OAuth handshakes, reset flows are theirs, not ours.
- **Sessions:** Supabase-managed JWTs with refresh; server-side session validation on every authenticated route (`@supabase/ssr` pattern in the Remix server). Tokens in secure, httpOnly-where-possible cookies per the library's Remix guidance; never in localStorage beyond what the library requires.
- **Email verification:** required before the free credit grant (§4.5.4) and before Share/publish. Unverified accounts may open the builder and look around (reduces sign-up friction) but cannot generate.
- **Flows owned by Supabase, skinned by us:** password reset, email change (re-verification), OAuth account linking (same verified email = same account; conflicting links surfaced, not silently merged).
- **Anonymous visitors:** may browse `/gallery` and play `/play/[shareId]` with no account (the zero-cost funnel). Any creation intent — New Project, wizard, **Remix** — hits the sign-up gate, and the intended action is preserved through auth (post-signup redirect completes the remix/new-project the visitor started).
- **Sign-out everywhere** and session revocation via Supabase; account **deletion** (self-serve, Settings) — **BUILT 2026-08-02** (`app/lib/.server/account/delete-account.ts`, `POST /api/account`, Settings → Profile → Danger zone). Deletion requirements vary by jurisdiction — legal review before public launch (open question).
  - **Immediate purge, NOT soft-delete-then-schedule.** This line used to say "projects, snapshots, messages soft-deleted then purged on schedule", which was written when the platform stored a snapshot history of everyone's projects. Under repo-primary persistence (§4.5.4b) we hold a project row, a conversation, one recovery buffer and any published build — small enough to reap synchronously, and a scheduled purger is machinery that must exist, run, and be monitored, whose failure mode is indistinguishable from success.
  - 🔴 **`requireUser`, not `requireVerifiedUser`.** Verification gates SPENDING; a user who signed up, never confirmed, and wants their address off our systems must be able to leave. Holding out for more of someone's data is least defensible on exactly this request.
  - 🔴 **The identity goes LAST, and a failed purge ABORTS before it.** The user id is the only handle on everything else — `projects.user_id` finds the projects, each project id finds its bytes. Delete the identity first, or carry on past a failed purge, and whatever survives is orphaned: unreachable by the user, unreachable by support, and still ours after they were told it was gone. That is `delete-leaves-nothing` at account scale, and here it is unrecoverable rather than embarrassing. Every step is idempotent, so a retry after a partial failure completes.
  - 🔴 **The confirmation is the user's own email, typed, and the SERVER checks it against the session** (`decideAccountDeletion`, pure + exhaustively tested, the `auto-repair`/`restore-target` rule for anything irreversible). Comparing a caller-supplied confirmation against a caller-supplied email is not a check. It matches case-insensitively and trims — an address pasted from a password manager is not a different person, and refusing it teaches the user to retype rather than to reconsider.
  - 🔴 **Migration 0017: ledger retention was a CLAIM, not a behaviour.** `credit_ledger.user_id` and `generations.user_id` were both `references auth.users(id) ON DELETE CASCADE` (0001), so the first `auth.admin.deleteUser()` would have deleted the user's entire financial record — including `purchase` rows backing real Stripe payments — and shrunk every historical §4.10 margin report by whatever that user had spent. Nothing throws; the numbers just get smaller and stay confident. The FKs are dropped (not `set null` — the column is `not null` and is what every balance read and per-user report groups by; a ledger whose owner column empties is not a retained record). The uuid survives and no longer resolves to a person: that IS "disassociated from PII". `FsLedger` has no foreign keys at all, so this was invisible to every TypeScript test — pinned in `ledger-sql.spec.ts` against real Postgres, **with a control that the person's own rows (profile, projects, `git_tokens`) still cascade**, or dropping every FK in the schema would pass.
  - **`git_tokens` goes with the account** — an encrypted OAuth token for somebody's GitHub, held by a platform they have just left, is the one survival here that is a security problem rather than an untidiness one.
  - **No creation refunds during an account delete** (`purgeProject({refundCreation: false})`): a refund credits a ledger belonging to a user about to stop existing, and appends fresh rows to the financial record at the moment we are closing it.
  - 🔴 **It found a pre-existing orphan on the ORDINARY project-delete path: the published build.** `unpublish` was reachable only from the Share dialog, so deleting a published project removed the row that resolves `/play/:shareId` and left the built game — every asset — in object storage under a share-id prefix no surviving record mentions. Fixed in the shared reaper, so both paths get it. The reaper itself was **extracted** (`projects/purge.ts`) rather than copied: the list is long, every entry on it exists because it was once missing, and a divergence leaves bytes behind while the UI reports success.

#### 4.5.2 Profiles

- `profiles` row auto-created on first sign-in via Supabase trigger: `id (= auth.users.id), display_name, avatar_url, created_at, updated_at`.
- Defaults: display_name from OAuth name or email local-part; avatar from OAuth or generated placeholder. Editable in Settings.
- **Public surface is minimal by design:** display_name + avatar appear only on gallery entries the user chose to publish. Email is never public. No public profile pages at launch (Phase 4+ consideration alongside the community features).
- Display names sanitized (length, charset, reserved words) since they render on public gallery pages.

🔴 **The chrome names you from the ACCOUNT, and it used to name you from `localStorage` (BUILT 2026-08-02, `app/lib/identity.ts`).** The sidebar header and the settings avatar dropdown both rendered `profile?.username || 'Guest User'` — upstream bolt.diy's per-browser `bolt_profile` blob, which is coherent in a product that has no accounts and is a second, unrelated identity here. With Supabase configured, a signed-in user would be labelled **"Guest User" beside their own projects**, with nothing anywhere in the product saying which account they were in; and the Profile tab's `localStorage` fields sat right next to it, so the natural conclusion was that the name typed there *was* the account name. It survived because local mode has no accounts, so the wrong branch is the only branch anyone ever sees. Now one pure `resolveDisplayIdentity` (`useDisplayIdentity`), tested including a **control that no combination of inputs can still produce the string** — the tests name the cases somebody thought of, and the defect was a case nobody had.

- 🔴 **An account is named by the account, and the local profile may NEVER override it — not even as a fallback.** `bolt_profile` is per-BROWSER, so on a shared machine it holds whatever the last person typed; a fallback is exactly how their name and face reappear next to this session. The avatar follows the same rule, which is why `avatarUrl` now rides on the session (`profiles.avatar_url` → `/api/me`) instead of being read from the browser.
- **In LOCAL mode the local profile DOES win**, and that is not an inconsistency: there is no account to contradict it and nobody else's name can be in it (§4.5 — local mode is a real mode).
- **While `/api/me` is in flight, assert nothing.** Printing 'Guest User' — or the local username — for the ~100ms before the session answers is the same false statement, briefly. The Profile tab's local fields are kept (hide-don't-delete) but relabelled "saved in this browser only" once there is an account, because a field that no longer drives anything must say so.
- 🔴 **WHERE the identity renders is a stability decision, not taste (owner + measured, 2026-08-02): the sidebar's top strip (`SidebarIdentity`), name left / avatar right — NEVER the header.** It spent a day in the header's `header-brand` block, counter-translated to hover over the sidebar column, and flashed white-on-white on every dock toggle and hover slide-out ("it keeps slipping back in") — content pinned over the seam between two animation engines (framer-motion sliding the sidebar, a CSS transition padding the body) has to win a per-frame race forever, and two patches at the seam both failed (an easing match narrowed it; a `body::before` gradient band **never painted a pixel** — an opaque in-flow app-shell wrapper sat above its `z-index: -1`, so the guard could not fail, which is why the bug kept returning). In the sidebar the identity moves WITH the sidebar; there is no second edge. The same rule made the **wordmark** an ordinary in-flow child of the header (no counter-translate, no `z-logo` float, no plaque — the header's own gradient is behind it in every frame; the hover drawer slides OVER it, which is drawer behaviour, not a glitch), and put the **date/time** in the sidebar footer's centre slot. Pinned by `app/styles/chrome-band.spec.ts` (structural, comment-stripped scans with controls).

#### 4.5.3 Authorization

- **RLS on every user-facing table** (`user_id = auth.uid()` ownership policies; gallery/play reads via explicit public-read policies on published artifacts only).
- **Server-route middleware — the second wall:** every server route that touches a project (agent proxy, snapshot read/write, share/build, export) validates session **and project ownership**, not merely "logged in." RLS is the backstop; the middleware is the front door. This is a code-review checklist item from day one.
- Admin routes gated by an `is_admin` claim/allowlist, entirely separate from user auth paths.
- The agent proxy additionally enforces per-user rate limits and the credit gate (§4.6) after ownership passes.

##### 4.5.3a The third wall: browser-local records are scoped to an account too (BUILT 2026-08-02)

**🔴 Both server walls were correct and two people sharing a computer still read each other's conversations.** `/api/projects` and `/api/chats` have always gone through `requireUser` → `listByUser(user.id)`, so the Projects dashboard and the sidebar's *server* half never leaked. But IndexedDB (`boltHistory`) and `localStorage` are per-BROWSER-PROFILE, they carried no owner, and sign-out clears neither — it drops the session cookie and reloads. Ownership was a property of the server's data and of nothing in the browser, so **five independent read paths each leaked, and each was written by someone reasonably assuming the local store belonged to whoever was looking at it**:

1. **The sidebar.** `mergeChatList` drops a local chat whose `serverChatId` is absent from the server list, but keeps every chat that has *no* `serverChatId` — correct about SAVEDNESS, silent about ownership. A chat only gets a `serverChatId` at the END of a generation (`ensureServerChatId` ← `checkpointProject`) while `storeMessageHistory` writes IndexedDB on every message, so every stopped, failed, or closed-tab generation left one behind permanently.
2. **`/chat/:id` for a non-UUID id.** `isServerChatId` routes UUIDs to `openFromServer` (which searches the caller's own list — 404-not-403 applied to a URL); a *local* id skipped that entirely and read `getMessages` + `getSnapshot` straight out of the shared database. The whole conversation and the snapshot's files, to whoever typed the URL.
3. **The offline fallback.** `localChatList` filters nothing by design — blanking the sidebar on a network blip is its own bug — and an expired session reaches it as an ordinary thrown 401, so a signed-out browser listed every chat anyone had made on that machine.
4. **Settings → Data.** "Export all your chats to a JSON file" read the store unfiltered, twice (`getAllChats`, plus a second direct read in `useDataOperations` written for "more reliable access").
5. **`localStorage` identity caches.** `github_connection` / `netlify_connection` / `vercel_connection` survive sign-out, so the next account opens Settings and finds the previous person's GitHub account sitting there connected. Display only — the tokens are server-held (`git_tokens`) and `token` is written as `''` — so this is disclosure, not credential theft.

**The rules, each of which fails silently in one direction or the other:**

- **Scope reads; do not delete.** A local chat is the write-ahead buffer for work the server has not seen (`storeMessageHistory` writes per message; the server is written per generation). Wiping IndexedDB on sign-out trades a privacy bug for a data-loss one — anyone who signs out and back in loses an in-flight conversation. Filtering hides the record and keeps the bytes; the rightful owner signs in and sees it again. Live-verified: after an account switch all 23 records were still on disk, including the other account's.
- **An UNSTAMPED record belongs to nobody, never to everybody.** The permissive reading of that one line is precisely the behaviour being removed, and it is the one a future reader will reach for the first time a legacy chat goes missing. Legacy records are rescued by attribution, not by making the predicate generous.
- **Unknown fails CLOSED.** Everywhere else in this codebase "could not ask" degrades toward showing more (`mount-source.ts`'s `null` vs `undefined`; `localChatList` itself) because the cost is a user losing sight of their own work. Here the cost of guessing is showing someone else's conversation. Safe only because nothing is deleted. ⚠️ `viewerFromSession` must check `loading` FIRST and alone: `EMPTY_SESSION` is byte-identical to a resolved local-mode session, so a rule reading `accountsEnabled` first hands every booting page the local single-user id and shows it everything on the machine.
- **The mount effect WAITS for the session rather than deciding without it.** `/api/me` resolves after first paint, so the `mixedId` branch necessarily runs once with the viewer unknown — and an unknown viewer owns nothing. Deciding there would bounce every user off their own chat URL on every cold load, and the re-run would not undo the navigation. The viewer is therefore an effect dependency: **the guard without the dependency turns a race into a hang.**
- **Attribution is by EVIDENCE, not by whoever arrives first.** A legacy chat whose `serverChatId` appears in the viewer's own `/api/chats` list is provably theirs and is stamped; one whose id is absent belongs to another account and is left alone forever. Only chats with no `serverChatId` have no evidence available, and those are adopted **once per browser** behind a `bt_local_owner` marker. A record that already has an owner is **never re-stamped** — re-attributing on a later sign-in is the original leak with an audit trail.
- **An unanswered server is not an empty one.** A failed `/api/chats` yields an empty owned set, shaped exactly like "you own no chats"; acting on it would stamp nothing while burning the one-shot marker, orphaning every legacy chat on the machine permanently and unrecoverably.
- **An imported record is claimed for the importer, and whatever `ownerId` the file carried is discarded.** An export `.json` is user-supplied, so the id inside it is not evidence — and an unstamped write is invisible to the person who just made it, so the import would report success and show nothing.

`app/lib/persistence/local-owner.ts` (pure) + `local-owner-sync.ts` (the one module-level session subscription — ownership is a privacy property and must not depend on which screen is mounted). Pinned by `local-owner.spec.ts` (17), `local-owner-db.spec.ts` (8) and a **default-deny source scan with five controls** (`local-owner-scan.spec.ts`) that fails any new file listing the browser's chats without ever mentioning ownership — because fixing the five doors I found fixes exactly the five doors I found, and this repo has already paid for that lesson in the outbound-auth sweep (five routes named after subsystems left anonymous behind 21 green assertions) and the boot-splash gate (a third door that walked past two enumerations). ⚠️ The scan's granularity is the FILE, not the read site, and the sub-spec says so rather than letting a reader assume more. **Live-verified 2026-08-02** in the real browser: another account's chat absent from the sidebar while the viewer's own control chat rendered; `/chat/<their-id>` bounced to `/` with neither title nor message body on screen; `/chat/<my-id>` opened normally with its message; the identity caches cleared on an account switch with zero records deleted.

#### 4.5.4 How identity ties to credits

Credits are meaningless without identity — the ledger is keyed on `user_id`, and every enforcement point resolves the authenticated user first:

1. **Grant at verification, not signup:** the free starter grant (§4.6) is written to `credit_ledger` (`reason='grant'`) only when email verification completes — one grant per user, enforced by a partial unique index (`user_id, reason='grant'`), so re-verification, OAuth-relinking, or races can never double-grant.
2. **Every debit is attributable:** generation completes → ledger row (`reason='generation'`, `generation_id` FK) for the authenticated owner of the project. Balance = latest `balance_after` for that `user_id`; append-only.
3. **Purchases bind Stripe to the user:** Stripe Checkout sessions are created server-side with `user_id` in metadata and the `stripe_customer_id` stored on the profile; the webhook writes the `purchase` ledger row against that user (idempotent on `stripe_ref`). A payment can never credit the wrong account because the linkage is server-asserted, not client-supplied.
4. **The credit gate is an auth-context check:** agent proxy resolves session → user → **BYOK-entitled? else balance** before any platform-key Anthropic call; unverified users and zero-balance users are blocked at this single choke point. A user with an active Pro entitlement AND BYOK enabled bypasses the credit charge (their own key pays); everyone else is on the ledger.
5. **Abuse containment is per-identity:** rate limits, device heuristics, and the grant kill-switch (§4.6) key on `user_id` (+ verified email domain heuristics); one identity = one grant = bounded free exposure.
6. **The choke point is only a choke point if nothing routes around it (added 2026-07-13).** Upstream bolt.diy ships `/api/chat` and `/api/llmcall`, which resolve a provider from the request body and read the key from the server env with **no session check, no credit gate, and no ledger row** — on a deployed instance with `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` set, either one is an unauthenticated, unattributable bill on the platform key. Nothing in this product calls them (the client posts to `/api/agent`), so they now **fail closed with a 404** — not a 403, which would confirm to a prober that the endpoint exists — behind `UPSTREAM_LLM_ROUTES_ENABLED` (`app/lib/.server/llm/upstream-routes.ts`; never set it in a deployed environment). Upstream's `/api/enhancer` was the same hole but *live* — the prompt-enhance button called it anonymously, unmetered, and took its **model from the request body** — so it now runs the full path: `requireVerifiedUser` → server-resolved BYOK → credit gate → settlement, with the model fixed server-side and input length-capped. **Any new route that can reach a provider key must go through the gate, or it is not a route — it is a hole.**
   > 🔴 **And one route did not merely spend the key — it GAVE IT AWAY.** Upstream's `/api/export-api-keys` was an **unauthenticated GET loader** that walked every provider, read its key from `process.env` / the CF env / `llmManager.env`, and returned the values as JSON: `curl /api/export-api-keys` → `{"Anthropic":"sk-ant-..."}`. This is a strictly worse failure than an unmetered endpoint — an unmetered endpoint bills us only while it is reachable, whereas **a leaked key keeps working off-platform forever**, with no gate, no rate limit, and no way to attribute the spend. The route now requires a verified user and **never reads the server environment at all** — not even as a fallback when the cookie is empty; there is no ordering of precedence that makes returning a platform secret acceptable. Its legitimate purpose is narrow: a BYOK user exporting the keys *they themselves entered*, which live in their own cookie (echoing a caller their own cookie is not a disclosure). The "is a key configured?" question is answered by `/api/check-env-key`, which returns a **boolean, never the value**. Regression-tested in `upstream-routes.spec.ts` with a platform key deliberately present in the environment. **Standing rule: a server route may act on a platform secret; it may never emit one.**
7. **Every debit's `generations` row is written BEFORE the debit (added 2026-07-13).** `credit_ledger.generation_id` is a foreign key to `generations(id)`, and `settleGeneration` may never throw (§4.6) — so a missing row is a `23503` that gets caught, logged, and swallowed, and **the generation bills zero**. Silently, on every generation, forever. The row is therefore anchored inside `settleGeneration` itself (`app/lib/.server/billing/generations.ts`), not left to a caller's ordering, so every settlement path gets it for free. Pinned by `billing.spec.ts` with a ledger that enforces the same foreign key Postgres does — `FsLedger` has none, which is exactly how this survived to production.
6. **BYOK is Pro-gated:** the `byok_enabled` profile flag is honored ONLY while an active Pro Tools entitlement exists (checked server-side per generation — a lapsed subscription silently falls back to credits with a friendly notice). Key stored client-side only; generations are still recorded (tokens, `credits_charged=0`) and rate limits still enforced.

#### 4.5.4b Persistence model — REPO-PRIMARY (BUILT 2026-07-16)

> **Status: BUILT + LIVE-VERIFIED ON GITHUB (2026-07-18).** The user's game code lives in their own GitHub/GitLab repo; the platform holds the project record + chat and no files. §4.13's GitHub Sync is no longer an optional bridge — it IS the storage backbone.
>
> **Live verification (2026-07-18), driven through the real UI against real github.com (`MackeyK24`, private repo `blank-canvas`) — the entire GitHub flow PASSES, no defects; only GitLab remains:** Save → **private** repo created (CREATE-not-adopt); binary **byte-identity** on github.com incl. a **2 MB `havok.wasm`** (sha256-identical, lossless base64 blob round-trip); `.env` **secret-excluded** (404 + `.gitignore`); **wipe ALL browser storage → reopen → mounts from the repo** (server `Fetched 66 files`) → `npm install` → **playable** preview; **revoked-token → 401 → LOUD reconnect** (no false success); **VS Code commit → Pull round-trip** (real external clone committed `0d5925f`, `Sync from GitHub` pulled it — `Fetched 67 files`, sha advanced, safety checkpoint created first per §4.13); **network-kill mid-push → LOUD failure** (fault-injected `unavailable` in `fastForwardPush`: badge stayed "Not saved" with the error surfaced + a "Try again" affordance, no false success; recovered on retry once the fault was removed). **One step remains owner-side: repeat the whole flow on GitLab** (after the one-click GitLab OAuth connect). Caveat: the network-kill was **fault-injected, not a real OS-level socket cut** (no passwordless sudo; the host's wifi is also the agent's uplink) — the LOUD-failure UI path is exercised end-to-end, the physical-cut variant is not.
>
> **Ten deviations from the design above, each with a reason — read them before changing this area** (10 is the newest, added when import landed):
>
> 1. **`buildCommit` is not a seam method.** The design listed it alongside `fastForwardPush`; making it one would have forced GitLab to fake GitHub's four-call blob/tree/commit/ref dance (GitLab commits atomically, one call with `actions[]`). The seam is `fastForwardPush` — the *outcome*. A seam that encodes one vendor's call shape is not an abstraction, it is GitHub with a second implementation bolted on.
> 2. **`ensureRepo` takes a required `adoptExisting`, and Save passes `false`.** Save DERIVES the repo name from the project title, so `deriveRepoName("My Game")` → `my-game` → a user with an unrelated `my-game` gets 422 → the original code ADOPTED it → their repo's HEAD silently becomes this game. Save now walks to `my-game-2`. Linking a repo the user *named* still adopts, which is the correct behaviour for that verb — hence a required flag rather than a default.
> 3. **Local checkpoints are ordered by a monotonic `seq`, never `createdAt`.** Two checkpoints in one millisecond tie on a clock, and the tiebreak decides which one undo restores and which one the 20-checkpoint trim discards. This is migration 0003's ledger bug (`order by seq desc`, never `created_at`) in a second place — clocks tie and clocks go backwards, so never order anything that matters by one.
> 4. **`remoteHead: undefined` (could not ask) is not `null` (branch is empty).** `selectMountSource` keeps them distinct: collapsing them lets a reload on a flaky connection decide the browser is authoritative and push over a repo it never read.
> 5. **The link is a TUPLE — `provider` + `linked_repo` + `linked_branch` are all-or-nothing** (migration 0006 constraint). It caught two live half-link writers on the way in: the sync route's `link` op never set `provider`, and `PATCH /api/projects/:id` let any caller set `linkedRepo` alone.
> 6. **A remix SEED is deposited at publish time** — the one thing the platform still stores of a user's source, and only for a game they deliberately made public. Removing server snapshots quietly broke §4.8 remix: the source pointer became `undefined` for every ordinary project, so a stranger's remix cloned nothing and produced an empty editor, silently. The owner's repo cannot fill the gap (it is private and it is theirs). `buildRemixSeed` strips the `.env` family — sharper than the push path, since a seed is handed to strangers — reusing `isSecretPath` rather than writing a second copy of "what counts as a secret".
>    - **It is stored as what it is (2026-07-16):** one object per project at `seeds/{projectId}.json`, key **derived** from the project id (`share/seed-store.ts`) — not a `Snapshot` row wearing the clothes of a deleted version history. It is a **read-only** surface (`GET /api/projects/:id/seed`; the route has no `action` — a write here would be server-side project storage under a new name), and **`unpublish` deletes it**: "make my game private again" has to mean the platform stops holding the source, or §4.5.4b is a claim rather than a behaviour. A clone gets its **own** seed under its own id, so the source being unpublished or deleted never empties someone else's remix. Binary byte-identity on this path is pinned by `seed-store.spec.ts` (the hostile-bytes tests ported from the deleted `snapshots.spec.ts` — the store went, the invariant did not).
> 7. **A restore DELETES what the incoming version dropped — it was an overlay, and an overlay is not a restore.** `restoreFiles` wrote the incoming map and removed nothing, so undo (§4.12) left the file it was undoing and "use the version from my repository" (§4.13) produced a third version that was neither. Both silent. `planRestore` is pure and exhaustively tested because it is the code that destroys the user's files: it normalises both sides through the SAME `toRepoRelativePath` the push uses (compare `/home/project/src/main.ts` against a repo's `src/main.ts` raw and nothing matches → every file is "missing" → the project is wiped), it takes a REQUIRED `protect` (a repo's map has no `.env`, so treating it as the whole truth deletes the user's keys), and an empty incoming map deletes nothing. Writing that test found a real latent bug in the shared path rule — `/home/project` with no trailing slash normalised to `home/project`, so the workdir itself was a deletion candidate.
> 8. **The chat is restored from the server on a new device — as a TRANSCRIPT, marked never-replay.** `saveMessages` was uploading every conversation and `loadMessages` had zero call sites, so a project opened elsewhere came back with its files and no history. The fix is not `setInitialMessages(serverMessages)`: parsing an assistant message RUNS its actions (that is how upstream rebuilds a project with no snapshot), so a replayed history writes stale file bodies over the ones just fetched from the user's repo. Restored messages are marked `NO_REPLAY` and routed to a second parser that renders them and executes nothing.
> 9. **Self-remix (Duplicate) sends its files from the browser.** There is no server copy of an unshared project to clone. The caller owns both sides (`requireOwnedProject`), so their own bytes are authoritative. `body.files` is IGNORED on the `shareId` path: a visitor's files are not the owner's game.
>
> 10. **An IMPORTED project is born LINKED (2026-08-02) — a fourth entry point into the lifecycle.** The documented lifecycle is UNLINKED → SAVE = LINK → LINKED, which assumed every project starts life existing only in a browser. An import does not: its code came out of a repository, so the honest initial state is LINKED, and the alternative would prompt the user to "save" a game that is already saved. Written as the complete tuple through `linkProjectToRepo` (§4.13a), **after** the clone and the checkpoint and **before** the hand-off navigation, and best-effort at the CALL SITE — wrapped in its own try/catch rather than trusting the callee's never-throws doc comment, because the enclosing catch rolls the project back and a link failure must never delete a project whose files are already correctly on disk. Live-verified: an imported repo reads back `{linked: true, provider: 'github', repo: 'octocat/Spoon-Knife', branch: 'main'}`.
>

**The model (settled):** the user's game code is permanently **owned** by them and lives in **their own GitHub or GitLab repository**. Our servers hold the lightweight **project record** (name, thumbnail, chat/messages, registry seed, `provider`, `linked_repo`, `linked_branch`, `last_synced_commit_sha`, `auto_push`), plus — as of §4.5.4c — **one working copy per project, purely for crash recovery**. The live working copy still lives **in the browser** (WebContainer + inherited IndexedDB).

#### 4.5.4c Durability — the server WORKING COPY (DECIDED 2026-07-22, owner decision)

> **Status: DECIDED, NOT YET BUILT.** This amends §4.5.4b. Repo-primary stays the **ownership** model; it stops being the **durability** model.

**The failure that forced this, measured live (2026-07-22).** A `/bt-landing` redesign ran to completion on an unlinked project — `finish=stop`, 20,364 output tokens, 8 files rewritten, four generated images — and settled at **427 credits**. The browser tab then died. Re-opening the project showed the state from *before* the run: no redesign, no assets, and no mention in the conversation that it had ever happened. The server log knew it succeeded and the ledger knew it had been paid for; everything else was gone.

That is not repo-primary working as intended. Repo-primary says the user's code is *theirs* and belongs in *their* repo. It never said a completed, paid-for generation should be one tab-crash from oblivion — and §4.12 explicitly sells checkpoints and undo as **"the single most important safety net for non-developers"**, i.e. for exactly the users who do not have a git remote to fall back on. That net currently lives in IndexedDB and dies with the browser profile.

**The decision.** The platform keeps **one working copy per project** — a *recovery buffer*, not a backup product and not a version history.

**Invariants — each one is the line that keeps this from becoming the thing migration 0007 deleted:**

1. 🔴 **EXACTLY ONE OBJECT PER PROJECT, overwritten in place.** `working/{projectId}.json`. **Never a history** — no per-generation retention, no "keep the last few", no rows. Retention is what made the old `snapshots` table unbounded and expensive; "just keep N" is precisely how it grows back, so the count is one and the spec says so.
2. 🔴 **The key is DERIVED from the project id, never caller-supplied.** Same shape as the remix seed (§4.5.4b deviation 6, `share/seed-store.ts`). This is a security property, not tidiness: the deleted snapshot route took a caller-supplied id, which is exactly why it needed `assertSnapshotBelongsTo` to stop project A's owner reading project B's files. With a derived key there is no id to supply, so `requireOwnedProject` alone is sufficient — **and that stays true only while the key remains a pure function of the project id.**
3. **Two walls, as ever** (§4.5.3): verified session **and** project ownership, 404-not-403.
4. **Written on the same trigger as a local checkpoint.** One concept, one moment. A second, independent "when do we save" rule is how two writers end up disagreeing.
5. **Present whether or not the project is linked.** ⚠️ **`linked` ≠ `pushed`** — deleting the copy when a repo is linked re-opens the whole hole for anyone who links early and pushes late, which is the common case. This is why "store it until GitHub sync, then clean up" was **considered and rejected**: it makes existence conditional, and the failure mode of getting that condition wrong is deleting somebody's only copy.
6. **Deleted with the project**, by the same prefix sweep that already reaps chats (§4.5.6) — bytes must never outlive the record that named them.
7. **Binary byte-identity applies**, as on every other file path (`spec/binary-files.md`).

**What this does NOT change:** ownership (the user's code is theirs and belongs in their repo); the push/pull/divergence rules (§4.13); `.env` exclusion (`isSecretPath`); the remix seed; and the absence of a **snapshot history**. `POST /api/projects/:id/snapshots` stays non-existent.

**Save becomes SYNC.** The button is renamed because the current label conflates *durability* with *publishing to GitHub* — and that conflation is the reason a non-developer loses work: "Not saved — browser only" reads as a nag rather than as "the only copy of this is one tab-crash from gone". After this change durability is automatic and **Sync to repo** means what it says.

**Costs accepted, explicitly:**

- **Storage scales per project.** The Arcade Racing project measured **~30 MB** of generated PNGs, or ~3 MB once §4.16's `output_format: "jpg"` guidance lands. That guidance stops being cosmetic and becomes load-bearing for this decision. **BUILT 2026-07-23:** jpg is now the *default* for photographic art (not just guidance) via `resolveImageDelivery` (explicit choice wins; transparency-needing art renders as jpg and is cut out into an RGBA png — §4.16's cut-out pass) — and it had to be, because the working-copy save serializing large PNGs as base64 + `JSON.stringify` on the main thread **froze the tab at 8 GB** during a media-heavy `/bt-landing` run. The save now (1) **defers past the stream** and **size-gates** (a project over the client budget keeps its local checkpoint and skips only the server copy), and (2) runs the base64 + stringify + PUT in a **Web Worker** with transferable zero-copy bytes, so the encode can never pin the UI. See CLAUDE.md §4.16 "CONSUMING GENERATED MEDIA FROZE THE TAB".
- **The platform now holds user code.** The claim "we store no project files" ends; privacy copy must change with it.
- **A route that writes whole projects returns.** It ships with the two walls and the derived key from day one, never "added later".
- **`no-server-storage.spec.ts` stays exactly as it is, and it did NOT need changing** — a first draft of this section claimed it "pins the opposite" and would have to be rewritten. That was wrong, and checking it produced a more useful finding. It pins the absence of the **snapshot HISTORY specifically**: snapshot routes, `SnapshotStore`, `currentSnapshotId`, and any client module referencing a snapshots URL. §4.5.4c reintroduces none of those, so the whole suite went green with a working-copy store added — **which means that guard would never have caught this coming back under a different name.** A second guard is therefore required, asserting the NEW rule (`one-working-copy.spec.ts`): exactly one object per project, no retention, a key derived from the project id, and no caller-supplied storage id anywhere on the path. Two guards, two distinct rules — the old one keeps history dead, the new one keeps the buffer bounded.

**Lifecycle:**
- **UNLINKED (browser-only):** every new project starts here — newbies run a prompt or two and see a working game with ZERO friction (no repo, no OAuth). Honest consequence, stated in the UI: clear browser data / switch devices before linking = the code is gone (the chat record survives; the files do not).
- **SAVE = LINK:** "Save your project" connects GitHub or GitLab (OAuth), creates a private repo in *their* account (or links an existing one), pushes. The repo is now the project's permanent home.
- **LINKED:** a **commit + push** of the browser working copy happens **only when the user presses "Commit changes"** (owner decision, 2026-07-23 — auto-push on checkpoint is RETIRED; the post-checkpoint hook is a status READ, so the chip can go amber "Changes not synced"). Writing to a user's own account is the one action that leaves the platform and lands somewhere with their name on it — it takes a person. Commit messages still default to the generation summary. **LINKED ≠ SYNCED, and the copy says so:** the chip reads "Linked to GitHub" (a permanent fact about the project), never "Synced to GitHub" (a claim about the repository's current contents that nothing verified once nothing pushes automatically). `autoPush` remains a stored field that nothing reads; reviving it as a toggle is an owner decision, not a code change.
- **RELOAD (any device / lost browser):** open project → server record → fetch linked branch head → mount fresh WebContainer → `npm install` → resume. Local IndexedDB newer than `last_synced_commit_sha` → prefer local, prompt to push. Remote ahead (VS Code commits) → pull per §4.13 (checkpoint first; asset introspection re-runs; agent gets the externally-changed-files note). **This is the round-trip: create here → iterate in VS Code with their own AI → commits flow back.**

**UI surface (required):** LINKED/UNLINKED indicator on project header + dashboard cards (UNLINKED = amber "Not linked to GitHub"; LINKED = provider icon + repo@branch, quiet, amber "Changes not synced" when the browser holds work the repo does not).
