Unsaved-work nudges — **milestone-based, never timed nags, never blocking generation**: a **long, loud save-reminder toast after EVERY new project's first creation** (per PROJECT, not once per user — every unlinked game is one cleared-cache or device-switch from lost, so each one earns the reminder; `saving.introToastAutoCloseMs`, default 60s, or `false` to require a dismissal — keyed `bt_saving_intro_shown:<projectId>`); dismissible banner every N generations (default 5) while unlinked; `beforeunload` warning with unsynced work; Commit always one click in the header. Help page `/help/saving-projects` in plain non-developer language (what GitHub/GitLab are, why the game lives in THEIR account, the VS Code round-trip).

**Provider abstraction (required):** a `GitProvider` seam — oauth/connect, ensureRepo(private), getBranchHead, fetchTree, buildCommit (base64 binary blobs), fastForwardPush — with **GitHub** (the §4.13 machinery generalized) and **GitLab** (REST commits API) implementations. **Binary byte-identity extends to provider round-trips** (`binary-files.spec.ts` discipline): a PNG/GLB committed and re-fetched MUST be sha256-identical.

**What stays on our infrastructure:** project record + messages (Postgres), play builds (S3/CloudFront), skill resources, template snapshots/pins (§4.4), and **remix seeds** — a published game's source (§4.8), the one deliberate exception. **What leaves:** user project files as a server-held permanent store. In-session checkpoints (§4.12) are local, with commits as the durable history for linked projects. Remix clones files into a NEW unlinked project — the link never travels.

> **⚠️ This paragraph used to end "the `ObjectStore` snapshot seam REMAINS dormant as documented fallback". It does not — it was DELETED (migration 0007, 2026-07-16).** Keeping the machinery dormant meant a `snapshots` table with a live RLS policy, both store backends, and a route that stored whole projects and merely declined to. That is not a fallback, it is the old model one call site away from returning, and the §4.5.5 note above records what came out. The **ObjectStore itself remains** and is load-bearing (play builds, template pins, skill resources, seeds) — it is the *snapshot* layer on top of it that is gone.

**Honest risks, owned:** OAuth tokens expire → refresh + loud re-connect prompt (a lapsed token must never silently drop saves); provider rate limits → queued, retried, visible push status (a failed save is LOUD); asset-heavy repos → shallow fetches + repo-size cap; Git-LFS explicitly OUT of scope v1 (documented limitation). Completes §4.13's remaining hardening: server-side OAuth app tokens, not the inherited connector cookie.

#### 4.5.5 Data model

RLS on all user-facing tables.

```sql
-- auth.users                     (Supabase-managed: credentials, identities, verification)
profiles         -- id (= auth.users.id), display_name, avatar_url,
                 -- stripe_customer_id?, byok_enabled bool, is_admin bool,
                 -- created_at, updated_at
projects         -- id, user_id, name, template_id, share_id (nullable unique),
                 -- remix_seed_at? (§4.8: a HINT that this published game has a seed —
                 --   the seed's key is derived from the project id, so this is never
                 --   an address. Replaced current_snapshot_id, dropped in 0007.)
                 -- provider?, linked_repo?, linked_branch?,   -- all-or-nothing (0006 constraint)
                 -- auto_push (not null, default true),
                 -- last_synced_commit_sha?, github_installation_ref?, created_at, updated_at
git_tokens       -- user_id, provider, access_token_encrypted, refresh_token_encrypted?,
                 -- expires_at?, provider_login, created_at, updated_at
                 -- pk (user_id, provider). RLS ENABLED WITH NO POLICY, deliberately:
                 -- service-role only. A user's own session must never read these rows.
templates        -- (see 4.4)
-- snapshots     -- DROPPED (0007). The platform stores no project files (§4.5.4b).
--                  Checkpoints are local (IndexedDB); the one server-held copy of a
--                  user's source is a published game's remix seed, and it is an OBJECT
--                  at seeds/{projectId}.json, not a row. See §4.5.5.
-- messages      -- NOT a table. A project's conversations are OBJECTS at
--                  messages/{projectId}/{serverChatId}.json — written whole every turn,
--                  read whole on resume, never queried by row, and megabytes. MANY per
--                  project (§4.5.6). The table can be added beside them if per-message
--                  queries (admin search, analytics) are ever actually needed.
generations      -- id, project_id, message_id, model, prompt_version_id,
                 -- input_tokens, cached_input_tokens, output_tokens,
                 -- skills_loaded text[], credits_charged, status, error, created_at
prompt_versions  -- (see 4.3)
skills           -- id, name (spec-valid), description, is_active, created_at (see 4.11)
skill_versions   -- id, skill_id, body, resources_manifest jsonb, storage_prefix,
                 -- source_commit_sha, content_hash, is_active, created_at
credit_ledger    -- id, user_id, delta, reason (purchase|generation|grant|refund|promo),
                 -- generation_id?, payment_provider?, payment_ref?,
                 -- balance_after, created_at
                 -- partial unique (user_id) where reason='grant'
credit_packs     -- id, name, credits, price_cents, provider_price_ref, is_active
entitlements     -- id, user_id, source ('protools_subscription'), tier
                 -- (indie|small_business|enterprise), status (active|lapsed),
                 -- subscriber_email, last_validated_at, expires_at?, created_at
```

- **Snapshot policy — the machinery is DELETED, not dormant (§4.5.4b; migration 0007, 2026-07-16).** The platform no longer snapshots a user's project at all: checkpoints are **local** (IndexedDB, `local-snapshots.ts`, 20 per project, ordered by monotonic `seq`), and durable history is the commits in the user's own repo. Resume = `selectMountSource` → local / repo / seed / diverged (`npm install` only when `node_modules` is absent or the lockfile moved).
  - **What went, and why "dormant" was the wrong answer.** §4.5.4b originally kept the `SnapshotStore` (both backends), the `snapshots` table, and a 405-refusing write route — the behaviour removed, the machinery left standing as a "documented fallback". That is a door with a lock on it: an uncalled route that stores a whole project re-opens by being called, and a live RLS policy on a `snapshots` table is a standing invitation to write to it. Migration 0006 removed the writes and left the entire schema; **0007 drops the table, its index, its policy, and `projects.current_snapshot_id`**, and the interface, both backends, both routes, `buildManifest`, `snapshotKey` and `assertSnapshotBelongsTo` are gone with them. `POST /api/projects/:id/snapshots` does not refuse — **there is no route**.
  - **`current_snapshot_id` → `remix_seed_at`, and the rename is the point.** The field kept a real meaning on published projects (it pointed at a remix seed) under a name describing the per-generation history that no longer existed. A field named for a deleted system is how the deleted system comes back. The seed is now one object at a key **derived** from the project id (`share/seed-store.ts`) — so there is no id to keep in sync, and no caller-supplied id to cross-check, which is why `assertSnapshotBelongsTo` needed no replacement.
  - Pinned by `no-server-storage.spec.ts` (routes absent, store absent, no client helper, a comment-stripped source scan **with a control proving the scanner works**) and by `ledger-sql.spec.ts` against the real migrations under PGlite (no `snapshots` table, no `current_snapshot_id`, `remix_seed_at` present).
- Balance = latest `balance_after`; ledger append-only, never a mutable counter.

#### 4.5.6 Many chats per project — "New chat, same game" (BUILT 2026-07-16)

**A project holds many conversations.** A user can start a fresh context on a game they are already
building: the files stay exactly where they are, the history does not come along. The existing chats are
untouched — this is another conversation, not a replacement.

**Why.** A project used to hold exactly ONE chat. That was never a decision — it was upstream's model
showing through, where the chat *was* the project and 1:1 was a tautology. Two things make it wrong here:

- **Game projects are long and phase-shaped.** The `bt-spec` → `bt-plan` → `bt-execute` loop has natural
  context boundaries, and each phase is grounded by artefacts on disk (`SPEC.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, the plan)
  rather than by the talking that produced them.
- **The conversation is the one thing we re-send at full rate, forever.** It is UNCACHED (§4.2.8, all
  four breakpoints are on the system blocks) and grows without bound, so by the execute phase every turn
  pays to re-send the spec discussion. `HISTORY_WINDOW_TURNS` bounds that bill, but it does so by
  *forgetting* — on a mature project you pay to re-send a conversation that has been silently truncated
  anyway. Starting a new chat is the honest version of the same saving.

**Nothing is lost, because the conversation was never the grounding.** Every turn the agent is sent the
project's files fresh from the WebContainer FS, the project's `CLAUDE.md` as its own instructions block,
and the skills index. A new chat sees the whole game; it just does not see the talking.

**`/clear` is this feature's chat-command spelling (2026-07-18, `app/lib/chat/client-commands.ts`).**
Typing `/clear` (aliases `/new`, `/newchat`) in the chat box takes the same path as the header's ⋯ "New
chat" — intercepted in the BROWSER before anything is posted, so it costs zero credits and never reaches
the model. The parser is pure + tested and matches the bare command ONLY (exact after trim,
case-insensitive): `/clear the obstacles` is a message for the agent, and a prefix match would silently
swallow it. With no project yet it falls back to a full-page load of `/` (the sidebar's "Start new
chat"), because an SPA navigate without the baton inherits the old chat's identity.

**🔴 With a project open, the reset happens IN PLACE — it must NEVER re-mount the project (amended
2026-07-25, `app/lib/stores/chat-reset.ts`).** Both entry points originally hand-balled to the mount
baton (`setPendingOpenProject(pid, 'fresh')` + `navigate('/')`), reusing the path a dashboard Open takes.
Sound as reuse, wrong as behaviour: the baton exists to mount a project that is NOT open, and here it
re-mounted one that never left — `mountProjectFiles` ran again, the route remounted, and the workbench
tore down and slid back in. The user asked for the left-hand column to be emptied and watched the whole
workspace reload ("it makes the workflow look broken"). Clearing a conversation is not a project event.
So the already-mounted case resets only what belongs to the CONVERSATION — the message array, the chat
identity (via the same pure `identityForMount({ freshChat: true })` the mount path uses, so an inherited
`serverChatId` can never make the new chat save over the old chat's server transcript), the parsed-message
cache (keyed by INDEX — leaving it standing renders the previous chat's message 0 in the new chat), the
context stats, the repair chain, and the URL (`replaceState` to `/`, never `navigate`, which would re-run
the mount effect). It touches NOTHING of the project: not the WebContainer, not the file tree, not the
preview, not `showWorkbench`, not `chatStore.started`, not `chatStarted` (the open-state effect keys on
`activeProjectId`, which has not changed — flipping it back replays the landing intro over an open game).
The two entry points meet at a monotonic signal store (`requestChatReset()`), because the conversation is
`ChatImpl` component state and the ⋯ menu is outside it; listeners compare against the value they last
saw, seeded from the CURRENT value at mount (the store is module-level and survives an SPA navigate, so
seeding from 0 would replay a reset on every remount). **The baton path stays correct where the
DASHBOARD uses it** (`ProjectsDashboard`, `'fresh'`) — there the project genuinely does need mounting; do
not collapse the two. Pinned by `chat-reset.spec.ts` (mutation-verified: restoring the baton fails it).

**`/context` (alias `/usage`) + the context health dot (2026-07-18)** answer "when should I clear?".
The proxy measures the re-sent conversation AS IT WENT ON THE WIRE — post-compaction, post-window
(`historySize` in `llm/history.ts`, threaded through the generation handle into the `agentMeta`
annotation) — and the client (`app/lib/stores/context-stats.ts`, pure `contextHealth` + tested) folds
it with the `usage`/`credits` annotations into a green/amber/red dot on the chat box
(`ContextIndicator.tsx`); `/context` or clicking the dot opens the breakdown (history msgs/tokens vs
window, cached-read 0.1x, uncached input, cache writes 2x, output, last-turn credits, model). The
client NEVER estimates history from its own messages — it holds the un-compacted copy, which is
exactly the number that does not matter. Amber = half the `HISTORY_WINDOW_TURNS` window; red = the
window is about to start silently dropping the oldest turns, i.e. `/clear` now costs nothing that was
going to be kept anyway. Stats are seeded on reload from the last assistant message's persisted
annotations, and RESET before seeding — "New chat, same game" mounts with no messages, and inherited
stats would report the context the user just cleared.

**`/effort` (alias `/thinking`) — the user's base thinking effort, MEDIUM or HIGH only (2026-07-27).**
Thinking bills as OUTPUT at the full rate (`spec/anthropic-models.md` §3.5), so effort is the one dial a
user can move that changes the price of every turn without changing what gets built. The user picks the
**floor** for their session; `effort-policy.ts` still escalates above it on evidence (repair → `high`,
2nd repair → `xhigh`, `/slash` → `high`), so **the floor never caps and never lowers anything**. Session
state (`app/lib/stores/effort.ts`), **deliberately NOT persisted**: it resets to `medium` on every reload,
because a raised floor spends silently on every subsequent turn and the failure mode of persisting it is a
user who raises it once for one hard problem and quietly pays more for months. Sent on the request body,
**validated server-side with `parseUserEffort`** — a browser value that could name `xhigh`/`max` would let a
tampered client buy the escalation-only levels on the platform's credit pool, so anything outside the two
levels resolves to "no choice" (the operator default) and never clamps upward. `xhigh`/`max` are
deliberately unofferable: they are what the ladder spends on *evidence*, and a chosen ceiling is a floor
that makes every ordinary edit start where a twice-failed build ends. `low` remains unrepresentable (§4.2a).

The control is `/effort` (a picker panel, not an argument — `/effort high` is NOT a command, so a message
meant for the agent is never swallowed), or the clickable **Thinking effort** row in the `/context` report.
It adds **no button to the chat row** (§4.1a's crowding lesson): the panel renders nothing until opened, and
the current value is readable in the model pill's tooltip and in `/context`, so a raised floor is visible
even though its control is not. Pinned by `effort-policy.spec.ts` (mutation-verified: making the floor a cap
fails 3 tests), `client-commands.spec.ts`, and the `parseUserEffort` block in `anthropic.spec.ts`.

**Never regress these — each fails silently:**

- 🔴 **The chat's server id is a minted UUID, NEVER the browser's chat id.** `getNextId` is
  `max(local keys) + 1` — a per-browser counter that hands out `"1"`, `"2"`, `"3"`. Keying the server
  transcript by it makes this laptop's chat "1" and that desktop's chat "1" **the same object**: two
  devices, one project, one silently destroys the other. Under §4.5.4b the conversation is the only
  thing we still store for the user, so that overwrite is data loss. The id lives in
  `IChatMetadata.serverChatId`, is minted at FIRST SAVE (an abandoned empty chat costs nothing), and
  `messagesKey` accepts a UUID and nothing else — a whitelist, so `"1"` and `../../seeds/{other}` are
  both refused by default. Upstream keeps `getNextId` and the URL scheme; we add ours beside it (§2.1a).
- 🔴 **Deleting a project sweeps a PREFIX, not a key.** `deleteMessages` deleted one object because
  there only was one; left alone it would delete a key that no longer exists, report success, and strand
  every real transcript with nothing left that can name them. It sweeps `messages/{projectId}/` and the
  legacy key.
- 🔴 **Deleting a chat deletes it on the SERVER too, or it resurrects.** The sidebar's delete was
  upstream's: it removed the IndexedDB record and stopped. Since `restoreTranscript` reads the server
  copy on open, the chat the user deleted **came back on the next open** — the delete did not delete it,
  it hid it until the next mount. Server first, while the local record still holds the ids that address
  it; the project and its other chats are deliberately untouched.
- **A restore keeps the chat's server id.** Minting a new one on a device switch uploads the same
  conversation again under a second id, so the user's chat list grows by one every time they open the
  project elsewhere.
- **The mount baton's chat slots are always written, never merely set.** A leftover id from a previous
  open silently restores the WRONG conversation — which reads as data loss while everything is in fact
  still there. A remix never inherits one: it is a new project, and that id names a conversation
  belonging to the project it was cloned FROM.
- **Pre-§4.5.6 transcripts are adopted on READ**, at a synthetic id derived from the project id and
  shaped so no minted v4 UUID can equal it. Continuing one migrates it to its own object and drops the
  old key (`putChat`) — otherwise the same conversation lists twice, forever. `listChats` prefers the
  real object, so a failed migrating delete never becomes visible.

#### The sidebar is the ACCOUNT's chats (2026-07-16)

The chat list follows the user between devices, the way claude.ai's does. The browser is a **local
staging area**: the platform holds the project record and the conversation, the user's code lives in
their own repo (§4.5.4b), and the sidebar is a view of the account rather than of one profile.

It was `getAll(indexedDb)` filtered by `urlId && description` — so a chat started on a laptop did not
exist on a desktop, and clearing site data destroyed the list, *while the transcripts sat on the server
the whole time with nothing listing them*. That was never a decision: upstream's chat WAS the project
and lived in IndexedDB, and the server copy was added underneath without the list ever learning of it.

- **`chats` (migration 0008) is a metadata INDEX; the objects remain the truth about existence.** A
  global list cannot read every transcript body on the platform to render a row of titles, so titles
  move into a queryable table — but an index that is authoritative about existence turns a failed row
  write into a conversation that is silently gone. So: an object with no row is still listed and its row
  is **backfilled** (the index heals by being used); a row with no object is not listed; `putChat`
  writes the object first and a failed index write never fails the save. A ghost row is left rather than
  pruned — a transient `list` failure must not delete a healthy account's sidebar.
- **No `user_id` column.** Ownership is inherited from the project (the `snapshots` pattern from 0001),
  and `/api/chats` resolves the caller's projects first, so the chat query can only ask about ids that
  ownership has already cleared.
- 🔴 **A chat's URL is its ID.** Upstream routed `/chat/<slug-of-the-title>`, de-duplicated against ONE
  browser's IndexedDB (`getUrlId` appends `-2`). That cannot address a chat once there is more than one
  user: `/chat/start-dev-server` is not unique, the de-duplication cannot see other accounts, and the
  same conversation got a different URL on every device. It also leaks the conversation's title into the
  URL bar and every proxy log. The URL is the `serverChatId` — a v4 UUID, minted in `mintUrlId` (free),
  kept by `restoreTranscript`, and resolved by `openFromServer` when the browser has no record, so a
  chat link works on a cold device. Someone else's id simply is not in their list, which is 404-not-403
  (§4.5.3) applied to a URL.
- 🔴 **`mergeChatList([], local)` is not the offline fallback.** It drops every synced chat — correct
  when the server has genuinely spoken (a delete from another device must stick), catastrophic when it
  merely failed to answer. `localChatList` is the fallback. Same distinction as `mount-source.ts`'s
  `remoteHead: null` (branch empty) vs `undefined` (could not ask).
- 🔴 **Sidebar delete takes the ITEM, not an id.** It read the addressing ids back out of IndexedDB,
  which finds nothing for a chat from another device — so the server delete was skipped *silently* and
  the chat returned on the next load.

**Two more, found only by driving the real UI (2026-07-16). Both were reported by the owner, and the
1,109 passing tests saw neither:**

- 🔴 **"Do we need to CREATE a project?" is never "is the chat empty?"** `sendMessage` branched on
  `!chatStarted`, which is seeded from `initialMessages.length > 0`. An empty chat on an EXISTING
  project satisfies that, so the first message ran the new-project path: it mounted a fresh template
  over the user's game and registered a SECOND project named after the prompt. Measured: opening "Kart
  Racer" and typing "add a boost pad to the track" produced a new project called "Add A Boost Pad". It
  is not specific to §4.5.6 — a dashboard Open of a project whose chat lives on another device had it
  too; the new-chat feature just made it the common path. The branch now asks `!activeProjectId`, and
  `chatStarted` reacts to a mounted project so the builder stops showing the marketing intro for a game
  the user is already in.
- 🔴 **`setUrlId` is a React state setter, and FOUR call sites read the value it does not update.** A
  chat is invisible without BOTH a `urlId` and a `description` — that is what the sidebar filters on —
  and upstream sourced both SOLELY from `firstArtifact`. So any conversation the model never wrote a
  file in never appeared: a question answered in prose, a failed generation, a Stop. Reported as "no
  chats at all show in the left sidebar". The stale-closure reads then made it worse in ways that
  cancelled out and looked plausible: `storeMessageHistory` wrote `urlId: undefined` on a new chat's
  first save; the chat-id block navigated to `/chat/1` over the slug; the next save re-minted the slug,
  collided with the record it had just written, and landed on `…-2`; and `ensureServerChatId` rewrote
  the record with `undefined`, wiping the slug at the exact moment the chat was first saved. Fixed with
  `urlIdRef` (a ref updates synchronously; state does not) plus a title/slug fallback to the user's
  first message. **Any new read of `urlId` inside an async callback is this bug again.**

**Deleting a chat NEVER deletes the game, and the card says how many chats there are.** Reported as a
bug ("I deleted KartRacer from side bar and its still in dashboard"), it is the correct behaviour and
the confusion was ours: the dashboard gave no way to tell "I deleted its only conversation" from "this
is an orphan". A chat delete cannot cascade to the project, because for an UNLINKED project the browser
holds the game's ONLY copy (§4.5.4b) — cascading would let deleting a conversation destroy a game that
was never saved. So `GET /api/projects` returns a server-side `chatCount` (`countChats` — one prefix
listing per project, reads no bodies) and a card reads "2 chats" or "No chats yet". `chatCount:
undefined` renders NOTHING: it means we did not count, and saying "No chats yet" because a listing
failed is a lie about someone's data.

Pinned by `message-store.spec.ts` (the collision, the sweep, legacy adoption + migration, the count —
including that a migrated legacy chat is counted ONCE),
`chat-routes.spec.ts` (both walls, the id whitelist, and that the chat cap never refuses to save a chat
the user is IN), `pending-remix.spec.ts` (the stale-slot cases), and `chat-visibility.spec.ts` (the slug
is never empty; four opens leave ONE local chat, not four).

- 🔴 **"Writes nothing" is about the FILESYSTEM, never the screen.** The `transcriptParser` (§4.5.4b) is
  a copy of the live parser with `runAction` removed — correctly, since replaying a stale
  `<boltAction type="file">` over the user's repo is the bug it exists to prevent. But it also dropped
  `showWorkbench.set(true)`, which writes no files and re-runs nothing, so **the project view vanished
  for every restored conversation**: chat and artifact bubbles rendered, file tree / editor / preview
  simply absent, for a project the user was looking straight at. Its test mocked the exact call that
  was missing (`showWorkbench: { set: vi.fn() }`, anonymous and inline), so nothing could assert it.
  Hoisted and pinned on both parsers now, verified to fail without the fix. **When you strip behaviour
  from a copy of a code path, strip only what is unsafe — and assert the rest is still there.**

⚠️ **The lesson, and it is the same one §4.5.4b already recorded.** Every invariant above was tested and
green while the feature was unusable end-to-end: the tests drove the stores and the routes, and every
bug lived in the wiring between them. "Correct by construction" is what the §4.14 MCP relay also was.
**Drive the real UI before claiming a user-facing feature works.**

⚠️ **A spec file must not live in `app/routes/`** — Remix compiles it as a route, so the manifest imports
`vitest` at runtime and *every request 500s*. Route tests live beside the code they exercise.

### 4.6 Credits & Billing

> **Status: IMPLEMENTED (Stage 3, 2026-07) — see `spec/billing.md` for the four divergences from this design.** In short: the charge formula has **four** token classes, not three (cache WRITES bill at **2×** base input, because we use the 1-hour cache tier per §4.2.8 — assuming the 1.25× headline number under-charges every generation and nothing throws); the pre-flight gate is `balance > 0` rather than a p90 cost estimate (a generation's cost is dominated by the tool loop and is not knowable in advance), so a `generation` debit is allowed to drive the balance negative and the *next* gate catches it — exposure bounded by one generation, which is the price of never yanking a game out from under someone mid-build; `balance_after` is derived by an atomic Postgres writer (`append_ledger_entry`) rather than in application code; and `REPAIR_WEIGHT` is not implemented (repair turns settle at full cost; `generations.repairOf` records the data to price them later).

- **Payments: Stripe only.** Stripe Checkout (cards + Apple/Google Pay + Link) is the platform's sole payment processor — for credit packs and premium assets. **No PayPal integration exists anywhere in the platform.** Pro Tools/BYOK is disabled by default and the external license-service validation is retired (§4.6.1); the platform's only subscription signal is the user's live Stripe plan, which drives the §4.18 Unity license tier and nothing else. The ledger keeps provider-agnostic fields (`payment_provider` + `payment_ref`) purely as cheap insurance (e.g., a future Merchant-of-Record move for tax compliance — see open questions); no second integration is planned.
- **Single credit balance:** one bucket, filled by Stripe pack purchases, the signup grant, promos, refunds; never expires. Append-only ledger; balance = latest `balance_after`. Pro Tools subscribers don't receive credit grants — their perk is BYOK (§4.6.1), which bypasses credit charges entirely.
- **Flow:** provider checkout → verified webhook (idempotent on `payment_ref`) → `purchase` ledger row. Failed generations auto-refund.
- **Charge formula:**
  `credits_charged = ceil((in*IN_RATE + cached_in*CACHED_RATE + out*OUT_RATE) / CREDIT_UNIT_COST * MARGIN)`
  Rates per model in config; `MARGIN` targets ≥ 70% gross margin on generation cost. Retail pack pricing is a launch decision; ledger records raw usage so pricing tunes without schema change.
- **Project CREATION is flat-priced; the first BUILD turn is not (2026-07-29, §4.4a, `PROJECT_CREATE_CREDITS` default 150, ledger reason `project_create`, migration 0015).** Creation runs no generation at all, so there is no creation turn left to price: the flat charge moved to `POST /api/projects`, **quoted before the project row exists and debited after it** (the refusal must leave no row behind; the debit's audit note is the project id, which does not exist until the row does), **never allowed to go negative** (enforced + insufficient → 402 naming the price, with no project row and no ledger row written — the `'media'` posture, since it debits before anything is provisioned). `0` disables and writes no row. The new balance rides back on the response so the UI cannot drift below the ledger. Refunded on the server DELETE path when a project is deleted having never had a completed generation. The first build turn settles cost-derived like every other turn.
- **SUPERSEDED — Creation turns were FLAT-priced (2026-07-28 → 2026-07-29, `CREATION_FLAT_CREDITS` default 500, `spec/billing.md` §"Charge computation").** `CREATION_FLAT_CREDITS` is now **REFUSED** if set (a `NotConfiguredError` naming its replacement, on the retired-KIE-price-var precedent — a price var nothing reads is a mis-bill waiting to be believed); `decideCredits`' `flat`/`maxCredits` levers and the gate's `minimumCredits` wall survive as tested-but-uncalled capability. Kept because it explains the shape of the replacement: The cost formula still computes and RECORDS every creation's true cost, but the user is charged one predictable number: creation cost is dominated by prompt-cache luck (measured 54 credits warm vs 430–633 cold — a 12× spread the user cannot see or influence), and an unanswerable "what does a game cost?" is a product defect, not a billing curiosity. The platform absorbs the variance; a Stop charges `min(consumed, flat)`; a failure refunds in full; the pre-flight gate refuses a creation below the flat price with the price in the message; `0` disables. Companion: the **base-prompt cache warmer** (`prompt/cache-warmer.ts`, `CACHE_WARMER_*`) keeps the one byte-identical-across-users prompt block warm — platform ops spend, no ledger rows — shrinking the true average cost under the flat price. ⚠️ This sentence said "on KIE's per-backend cache" until 2026-08-11, which is the KIE-only framing that made the warmer **inert for months** (CLAUDE.md); it is provider-routed and follows the `AUTO_MODEL_SELECT` ladder, with a fanout defaulted per gateway (KIE 6 · Comet 5 · Anthropic 1) and a break-even of `~3.4 × fanout` cold starts/day. Ships DEFAULT OFF. The per-project prefix cannot be pre-warmed; the measured-first restructure that could is recorded in `spec/context-budget.md` §"Levers that are NOT built".
- **The Marketplace price list (BUILT 2026-07-18, `spec/billing.md` §"The Marketplace price list"):** every price a gateway charges — LLM token rates AND the §4.16 per-task media prices — lives in ONE versioned document PER PROVIDER (this read "every KIE price … ONE versioned document" until 2026-08-11; see the per-provider bullet directly below, which supersedes the singular): a baked fallback in code (`billing/baked-market-prices.ts`, captured from KIE's public pricing feed, which confirmed the measured rates to the cent) overridden by whatever list the admin has PROMOTED from **Settings → Admin → Marketplace prices** (immutable versions + pointer in the ObjectStore, validate-before-write, rollback; the panel can fetch KIE's live feed for comparison, which is never machine-applied). **The env price vars are RETIRED and refused if set** (`KIE_INPUT_DOLLARS`/`KIE_OUTPUT_DOLLARS`/`KIE_CACHED_INPUT`/`KIE_CACHED_WRITES`/`PREMIUM_INPUT_DOLLARS`/`PREMIUM_OUTPUT_DOLLARS`); `KIE_DEFAULT_MODEL` and `PREMIUM_MODEL` survive as selectors, accepted only if the active list prices them in their own right (§4.6.1a); `SUPERMAX_MODEL` joined the retired-and-refused list 2026-08-08. Cache rates are quoted or derived BY FAMILY (extended 2026-08-04, `spec/model-families.md`) — **never for `claude-*`** (they derive 0.1×/2.0× per row and validation refuses the keys, unchanged), **required as an atomic PAIR for `gpt-*`** (KIE publishes both, and its cache WRITE is 1.25× input — the 5-minute tier — not the 2.0× every Claude row derives, so deriving them over-charged the write class by 60%), and **refused for `gemini-*`**, whose cached tokens bill at the full input rate because KIE quotes no cached rate and reports no cached-token counter. The standing rule was EXTENDED, not broken: a half-quoted row is still inexpressible. Media rows have NO most-expensive fallback — an unpriced media request is refused, because media debits happen BEFORE spend.
- **🔴 THE PRICE LIST IS PER PROVIDER (2026-08-10, T4).** Immutable versions + an active pointer **per gateway**, not one document for the platform — the KIE keys are unchanged, so nothing migrated. Two gateways reselling the same models at different prices cannot share one list, and the failure of pretending otherwise is silent: whichever list was promoted last would price the other gateway's turns. `activeMarketPrices(provider)` is the sync read on the billing path; `ensureMarketPrices(provider)` is the async doorway, and it now runs for **every provider the ladder could select** (`providersToPrice`), because a gateway gated and settled from its BAKED table while the operator's promoted list sat unread is exactly the same mis-bill wearing a different coat.
- **Comet's feed quotes OFFICIAL vendor rates; the charged rate is `pricing × ratio`, READ PER ROW (2026-08-10, T5).** The ratio is not a constant to hardcode — three rows carry `1.0`, so a blanket `0.8` would under-charge precisely the newest and most expensive models. 🔴 ⚠️ **OQ1 WAS ANSWERED WRONG, AND COMET'S FEED WAS THE RELIABLE SOURCE (corrected 2026-08-12).** This read: *"`MODEL_RATES['claude-sonnet-5']` was verified and deliberately LEFT at $3/$15: Comet's feed reporting the official $2/$10 confirms the introductory-pricing note in `rates.ts` rather than contradicting it (closes OQ1). Under-charging ourselves for a few weeks is the correct direction to be wrong in."* Anthropic has made **$2/$10 the standard price** and cancelled the 1 September increase, so the feed was reporting the current rate all along and the "confirmation" reading kept a **1.5× over-charge on the platform default model** — passed through to the user, because credits are cost-proportional (`spec/billing.md`). **When a captured source contradicts a hand-maintained table, the burden of proof is on the table.**
- **🔴 THE DISCOUNT IS VISIBLE TO THE USER, AND IT IS REFUSED RATHER THAN GUESSED (2026-08-10, `billing/savings.ts`).** Credits are cost-proportional, so a cheaper gateway is not margin — it is purchasing power, and it was completely invisible: the same turn cost 1,017 credits on Anthropic and ~407 at KIE-shaped rates with nothing on screen explaining the difference. The `/context` panel now shows the serving **provider** and the turn's saving; the credits panel shows a headline total **with the scope it covers** (a page of ledger rows, never a lifetime) and a per-row `saved N`. **`describeSavings` is PURE** — no env, no price store, no billing config, no `context` — and three defects were designed out by getting it there, each of which printed a *flattering* number: it must read `MODEL_RATES` **directly, never `providerRates().Anthropic`** (which injects paid-rung models at marketplace rates, so a model Anthropic never sold would still resolve and invent a discount out of a premium); it must compare a **USD ratio** against the RECORDED `raw_cost_usd`, never two absolute credit figures (the margin appears on both sides of a ratio and cancels — recomputing at today's margin made the 3.34 → 4.0 change retroactively paint every older full-price turn as ~16% discounted); and it must be unable to THROW, because it runs on `/api/credits`, and a 503 on the page that answers "where did my credits go?" is the worst place to put one. Every unanswerable case returns `null` and the UI says **nothing** — a savings figure is a marketing claim printed beside a money number, so silence is the only safe failure mode. A turn served at full price reports `full_price` explicitly rather than being hidden, or absence would imply a discount.
- **A ledger row says what the turn WAS (2026-08-10).** Every LLM turn debits under the single reason `'generation'` — that vocabulary belongs to a SQL `CHECK` constraint and describes the kind of money movement, not the kind of work — so a creation build, a one-line edit, an auto-repair and a plan all rendered as four identical rows reading "Generation", and the panel that exists to answer "where did my credits go?" answered "generations". `generations.status_kind` (migration 0019) has recorded the difference on every turn since it shipped and nothing read it; the credits panel now decorates rows from it at READ time (`billing/ledger-view.ts`). Never stored on the ledger: it is append-only, written by a locked SQL function, and a display label must not become a new way for that path to fail — deriving it also lights up every historical row. An absent or unrecognised kind falls back to "Generation", which is exactly as specific as what we know.
- **Free grant:** small configurable signup grant (`SIGNUP_GRANT_CREDITS`, default **1000 credits on KIE at `CREDIT_MARGIN = 4.0`** — sized for **1 creation + real room to iterate**: a cold KIE creation is ~231 credits at margin 4.0 (12,862 out + 111,659 in tokens, `rates.ts`), and since 2026-07-29 the flat `PROJECT_CREATE_CREDITS` (default 150) comes off the top FIRST, so the grant that reaches the model is 850 ≈ ~3.7× headroom (`grantHeadroom()` subtracts it — a headroom computed from the raw grant overstates it by exactly the creation price, silently, every time that price is raised). On Anthropic a cold build is ~576 credits, so that provider needs ~1,200 — the grant, provider, and margin are ONE decision, guarded by `grantHeadroom()`/`MIN_GRANT_HEADROOM`) gated by email verification + basic device/abuse heuristics; grant size, gating, and even existence are config flags. The credit AMOUNT is the stable fact; "creations' worth" is approximate and moves with model + **cache warmth**.
- **Packs (one-time) + PLANS (monthly) — BOTH BUILT (Stage 5, 2026-07-16; repriced premium 2026-07-18).** Packs: Starter 3,000/$30, Pro 9,500/$90, Studio 25,000/$225 (base $0.01/credit with a shallow volume discount to Studio; premium game-platform positioning, `stripe.ts`). Plans: the same three, per month, auto-granted on every paid invoice. Credits **never expire and never reset** — a monthly plan ACCUMULATES (§4.6: single bucket, no expiry); if you paid for it you keep it.
  - **⚠️ A PACK'S PRICE AND `CREDIT_MARGIN` ARE ONE NUMBER IN TWO FILES.** `ceil(raw / CREDIT_UNIT_COST_USD * CREDIT_MARGIN)` only earns `CREDIT_MARGIN` if a credit RETAILS at `CREDIT_UNIT_COST_USD` ($0.01): **effective margin = CREDIT_MARGIN × (pack $/credit ÷ CREDIT_UNIT_COST_USD)**. The packs shipped at $0.003/credit against a 3.34× setting → **1.01× on the smallest and 0.84× on the largest**, i.e. a ~19% LOSS per generation, worst on the best customers, silently. `packMargin()` + `MIN_PACK_MARGIN` + `billing.spec.ts` now assert it (one test reproduces the shipped 0.835× so the arithmetic is pinned to a fact).
  - **The model does not affect the margin — only the volume.** Credits are charged in proportion to raw cost, so at `CREDIT_MARGIN = 4.0`, 9,500 credits is ~$23.75 of model spend on ANY model. Measured: a $90 Pro subscriber who burns every credit earns **~$63 (73% GM)** on Opus 4.8 *and* on Sonnet 5 — identical. Opus buys many warm creations/month. **Downgrading the model to "save money" earns nothing**; it trades the quality differentiator for volume. (And §4.2's pathology 8: a cheaper tier returns confident WRONG answers whose repair turns cost more than the saving.)
  - **Subscription money path (the shape is where the bugs are):** a renewal has **no checkout session**, so `invoice.paid` is the ONLY grant trigger — and because it also fires for month one, granting on `checkout.session.completed` too would double-credit month one under two different idempotency keys (`session.id` vs `invoice.id`), which the `payment_ref` unique index cannot reconcile. The subscription checkout session is therefore an explicit, tested no-op. The user id rides on `subscription_data.metadata` (a renewal invoice has no session to read). **Cancellation needs no code**: Stripe stops invoicing, we stop granting. Portal/cancel/card/invoices are Stripe-hosted (`api.billing-portal`), resolved via `subscriptions.search` on OUR `userId` — never by email, which breaks the moment a user changes theirs. `subscriptions.spec.ts` drives the real webhook against a stubbed SDK.
- **BYOK (Pro Tools exclusive):** non-Pro users never see key/provider/model controls — only a marketing panel with an upgrade link (§2.3, §4.1). With a server-verified active entitlement, the BYOK panel renders: user-supplied provider key (any supported provider), stored client-side; zero credits charged (a minimal platform fee per generation is a config option, default off). Server re-verifies the entitlement per generation; lapse → controls hidden again + automatic fallback to credits with notice.
- **The turn that promised the work and ended — RESCUE, not refund (2026-07-25, `agent/unproductive.ts`).** A generation that produces *no actions, no tool calls, tiny visible text, and a chars/output-token density far below the code baseline* bought output and delivered nothing (measured: 524 output tokens → 83 characters → 316 credits → `status: 'completed'`). `!producedText` cannot see it, because a promise is text. It now gets **ONE** bounded corrective pass against the already-warm prefix, bounded and mutually exclusive with the tool-cap forced continuation, so no turn ever runs three streams. **Rescue beats refund here:** a refund returns the credits and no spec; a warm second pass costs ~11 credits (`spec/billing.md`) and returns the work the user asked for. The decision is a PURE, exhaustively-tested function like `decidePremium`/`decideAutoRepair` — it spends credits without the user asking — and every signal is mechanical (never a prose classifier, §4.2a). Recorded as `+unproductive-rescue` in `finish_reason`, because the rescue's own `drain` overwrites `finishReason` with a clean `stop` and this failure class is otherwise invisible in every column: it bills a normal amount and completes normally. **If this starts firing often, the cause is upstream of the rescue and the rescue is only paying for it.**
- **Kill-switches & caps:** Anthropic Console spend limits + alerting configured from day one; platform-level daily token budget breaker; per-user rate limits (generations/min, max message length, max concurrent projects).
- **Feature flag:** `BILLING_ENFORCED`. Off = beta mode (grants only, usage fully recorded). On = launch mode. The system is built credit-native from the start; the flag only controls enforcement.

#### 4.6.1 Pro Tools Subscriber Entitlements (BYOK — disabled by default; external ASMX license service RETIRED 2026-07-20)

> **Status: BYOK/Pro is disabled (`PRO_FEATURES_ENABLED=false`) and stays that way. The external ASMX license service (`licenser.asmx`) is RETIRED as of 2026-07-20 — its SOAP client is deleted and no code path performs network subscription validation.** This is a deliberate business decision: the platform is a **credits-based** product (Lovable/Bolt-style, specialized for high-quality Unity-style game development) with no BYOK upsell. The `entitlements` table and `resolveByok` remain in place but **inert** — nothing writes entitlements, `getEntitlement` returns the stored row without revalidating, and BYOK/Pro is a **manual, testing-only** knob from here forward. If Pro gating is ever re-enabled by hand, its authority is the user's **Stripe plan** (via the §4.18 tier resolver), never an external ASMX call.

When BYOK is manually enabled, Pro subscribers get **BYOK unlocked**: bring your own provider API key + model selection; unlimited usage at your own token cost, no credits involved. **That is the ONLY thing Pro would ever gate.** Everyone else — i.e. everyone, by default — uses platform credits with a fixed model and no keys to manage, and every other feature, including **Export and GitHub Sync**, is available to all users without gating.

- **No external subscription-validation authority.** The retired ASMX operation `ValidateSubscription(email) → { active, tier, expiry }` is gone: the SOAP client (`app/lib/.server/licensing/licenser.ts`) is deleted, `refreshEntitlement`/`linkSubscriberEmail` and the `/api/entitlement` route are removed, and the `LICENSE_SERVICE_URL` / `LICENSE_SERVICE_SECRET` env vars are retired (documented as unused in `.env.example`; refused as load-bearing). The only "who is subscribed?" signal the platform now reads is the live Stripe plan, and only §4.18 (Unity Project Licenser) consumes it.
- **`PRO_FEATURES_ENABLED` (config, default `false`):** the master switch for ALL Pro surface area — provider picker, model selector, BYOK key entry. When `false` (the shipping default, Lovable-style), none of it exists in the UI for anyone and every generation uses the `DEFAULT_MODEL` constant (`app/utils/constants.ts`) on platform credits. Setting it `true` is a **manual, testing-only** action; the BYOK UI then renders for local development, and any re-enabled entitlement authority would be the Stripe plan (§4.18), not the retired ASMX service.

> **The bullets below describe the HISTORICAL BYOK entitlement lifecycle. It is INERT today** (see the banner): no revalidation runs, `getEntitlement` returns the stored row as-is, and every "license service" they reference is the retired ASMX service. They are retained only as context for the manual/testing-only re-enable path.

- **Entitlement lifecycle (historical, inert):** on sign-in (and every 24h for active entitlements), the platform validated the user's verified email against the license service → upsert `entitlements` row (`status`, `tier`, `last_validated_at`). Lapse on failed revalidation (grace window: 72h of validation-service unreachability does NOT lapse anyone — our outage must never punish subscribers). This validation path no longer runs.
- **The perk is BYOK, not credits:** an active entitlement unlocks the BYOK setting (§4.6). Platform LLM cost for Pro users: zero (their key pays). Pro users may still buy credit packs and toggle BYOK off if they prefer the managed experience — both paths through the same gate logic.
- **Lapse behavior (historical, inert):** failed revalidation → `status='lapsed'` → BYOK stops being honored server-side; generations fall back to the credit gate with a friendly "your Pro subscription has lapsed" notice. Re-validation restored instantly. No revalidation runs today.
- **Email-mismatch recovery (historical, inert):** the subscription email on file with the license service often ≠ platform email; the manual link flow verified proof (license key / subscription ID / payer email) → stored `subscriber_email`. Removed with the ASMX client.
- **Scope (SUPERSEDED 2026-07-20 by §4.18):** the earlier boundary — "project license-key generation belongs to the Unity Editor Export Tool, not this platform" — no longer holds. The platform now **generates Unity project license files itself** (§4.18 Unity Project Licenser), byte-compatible with the Unity plugin's `licenser.cs` validator, billed as a **flat credit charge per tier** the user selects (replacing the retired PayPal annual Pro Tools subscription). Web projects still do not use or check license keys; the generated `license.json` is for the user's linked Unity project.
- **UX (historical, inert):** entitled users saw a "Pro Tools: BYOK unlocked" badge and the key-setup panel; non-entitled users saw the BYOK setting locked with an upgrade link. Not rendered today (`PRO_FEATURES_ENABLED=false`).

#### 4.6.1a Model Tier Ladder (credits mode) — BUILT 2026-07-17 as a two-model toggle, GENERALIZED to three classes 2026-07-31, down to TWO 2026-08-08, back to THREE 2026-08-10 (`platinum`)

> **Status: IMPLEMENTED.** An ordered ladder of model classes a **credits** user may pick between per-project — distinct from BYOK. This is the ONE user-facing model choice in credits mode, and it deliberately does not reopen the "model is a user input" concern: the user picks a **CLASS**, and the client sends an **enum tier id** the server maps through operator-configured, operator-priced env selectors — never a free-form model string, never a provider picker. The rungs:
>
> | Tier id | Label | Default model | Selector | Threshold |
> |---|---|---|---|---|
> | `standard` | Standard | `claude-sonnet-5` (`DEFAULT_MODEL`) | `LLM_MODEL` | none — always usable |
> | `premium` | Premium | `claude-opus-5` | `PREMIUM_MODEL` | `PREMIUM_MINIMUM_CREDITS` (in-code 1200; `.env.example` ships 1500) |
> | `platinum` | Platinum | `claude-fable-5` | `PLATINUM_MODEL` | `PLATINUM_MINIMUM_CREDITS` (2000) |
>
> Master switch: **`ENABLE_EXTENDED_MODELS`** (default ON; `false` serves Standard alone), with `ENABLE_PLATINUM_MODEL` gating the top rung on its own. The wire field is `tier: ModelTierId`; the legacy `premium: boolean` is still accepted as an alias (`true` → `'premium'`) so a client mid-deploy is not silently downgraded. Works on **all three `LLM_PROVIDER` values** (KIE, Comet, Anthropic).
>
> 🔴 **THE FLAG'S NAME HAS FLIPPED TWICE, AND THE REFUSED NAME IS ALWAYS WHICHEVER ONE IS NOT CURRENTLY READ — take the polarity from `premium-model-flag.ts`, never from memory or from this document's history.** `ENABLE_EXTENDED_MODELS` (two paid rungs) → **2026-08-08** `ENABLE_PREMIUM_MODEL` when SuperMax was retired and the plural stopped being true → **2026-08-10** back to `ENABLE_EXTENDED_MODELS` when Platinum restored the second paid rung. **`ENABLE_PREMIUM_MODEL` and the `SUPERMAX_*` names are the REFUSED ones today.** ⚠️ **This section asserted the INVERSE until 2026-08-11** — it described the third rung as retired and named the refused variable as the live one, on the switch that decides who is served a paid model, in the document `CLAUDE.md` tells every session to read first. It was found by T14's write-back verifier AFTER the same error had been corrected in `CLAUDE.md`, which is the lesson: **a polarity fix applied to one document is half a fix.** The hazard is unchanged by the rename — the flag defaults ON, so a deploy carrying a stale `=false` under the name that is no longer read falls through to the default and starts serving a paid rung to everyone, the costly direction, throwing nothing. That is exactly why the not-currently-read name is REFUSED rather than ignored.
>
> ⚠️ **HISTORY — THE THIRD RUNG WAS RETIRED 2026-08-08 AND RESTORED 2026-08-10 AS `platinum` (see the table above; this paragraph is kept because the refusal machinery it describes is unchanged).** `supermax` / `SUPERMAX_MODEL` / `SUPERMAX_MINIMUM_CREDITS` existed 2026-07-31 → 2026-08-08 and are gone — Platinum is a NEW rung with its own env keys, not a rename of that one. **The retired names are REFUSED if set** (`billing/premium-model-flag.ts` `refuseRetiredModelTierEnv`, `NotConfiguredError` naming the replacement), and the flag's rename is why: it defaults ON, so a deploy carrying `ENABLE_EXTENDED_MODELS=false` in SSM would silently fall through to the default and start serving the expensive rung to everyone — the costly direction, throwing nothing. The refusal sits inside `getModelTier`, which `getModelTiers` wraps per rung, so the MONEY path throws and the READ path (`/api/me`) degrades to a locked row carrying the message: never a throw on the session endpoint. A request body still naming `'supermax'` resolves DOWN to `standard` with reason `standard_requested` — it is now simply a string that is not a tier id — and the client's `isModelTierId` coerces a stored `'supermax'` the same way.
>
> ⚠️ **SUPERSEDED (kept because it explains the shape of the replacement): this section shipped 2026-07-17 as "a boolean toggle between exactly two operator-configured, operator-priced models".** Generalizing it to an ordered LIST rather than adding a copied branch is what keeps every rule below stated once — the threshold, the first-build lock and the decline-to-Standard fallback are per-rung data, not per-rung code — and it is why removing a rung again in 2026-08 touched the table and none of the rules.
>
> ⚠️ **COVERAGE LOST WITH THE THIRD RUNG, MADE WRITABLE AGAIN BY ITS RETURN (2026-08-10) — AND ONLY TWO OF THE FOUR ARE ACTUALLY WRITTEN (audited 2026-08-11).** Restored: never-step-down-one-rung (`premium.spec.ts`, `BETWEEN_PREMIUM_AND_PLATINUM`) and one-broken-selector-leaves-its-sibling-serveable (`model-tiers.spec.ts`). **NOT restored:** `getTierModel` naming its OWN env var (`model-tier-config.spec.ts` still has only the structural scan) and the panel's unserveable-beside-serveable copy (`ModelTierPanel.spec.tsx`'s ladder fixture is still standard+premium). ⚠️ **An earlier draft of this line claimed all four "WERE restored" — an over-claim caught by review, and the worst kind, because a spec asserting coverage that does not exist is how the gap stops being looked for.** Both un-restored properties are flagged IN THEIR OWN SPEC FILES in the present tense ("comes back the moment a second paid rung does" / "wrong the moment a second rung returns") — the condition they name has been met and nothing pointed at them. The mechanism is the lesson: a property needing two paid rungs is unwritable with one, and writing the loss down is what makes restoring it a checklist rather than an act of memory — **provided somebody re-reads the checklist when the condition changes.** **Original entry:** ⚠️ **COVERAGE LOST WITH THE THIRD RUNG, stated rather than quietly dropped.** Several properties could only be expressed against two PAID rungs and are now unwritable: "a balance clearing Premium but missing the rung above declines to STANDARD, never one rung down"; "one broken selector leaves its healthy sibling rung priced/available"; "`getTierModel` names the requesting rung's OWN env var and not another's"; and the panel's rendered "unavailable, never a threshold" copy (with one paid rung, an unserveable Premium means `hasModelChoice` is false and there is no picker to inspect). Where a structural equivalent existed it replaced the behavioural one (a source scan that the selector is read from `paidModelTierDefinition(id)`, `lockReasonFor` exercised directly, per-rung try/catch pinned in `model-tiers.spec.ts`); where none did, the loss is recorded in the spec file's own header. If a rung returns, restore them.

- **🔴 A TIER ID IS NEVER CLAMPED UPWARD.** An unrecognised, unpriceable or unaffordable rung resolves DOWN to `standard` — never to an adjacent paid rung, never to the rung above. This mirrors `parseUserEffort` and it is the only safe direction: the value arrives in a BROWSER BODY and spends the platform's credit pool, so inventing a more expensive class than the user asked for is the costly failure. A declined rung falls all the way back to Standard, and `decideModelTier` is never an error.
- **🔴 THE "COST LADDER IS NOT MONOTONIC" FINDING WAS A MIS-BILL, NOT A MEASUREMENT (retracted 2026-08-12).** This bullet read: *"Rungs order capability, not price. On Anthropic a SuperMax turn settled **814 credits** against Premium's **1,017**, because Anthropic prices Opus 5 ($5/$25) above the fable-5 list row ($4/$20)."* Anthropic **does** sell Fable 5, at **$10/$50** — `MODEL_RATES` simply had no row for it, so `providerRates`' gap-fill priced the rung from KIE's $4/$20 and the platform ate ~60% of every Anthropic-served Platinum turn. **814/1017 is exactly 4/5, which is the tell.** At the true rate that turn is ~2,035 credits and **the ladder is cost-monotonic on every gateway** (Anthropic $2/$5/$10 · Comet $1.60/$4/$8 · KIE $0.85/$2/$4).
  - **The rule it was written to defend still stands and must not be relaxed:** rungs order **capability**, not price. Do not reorder them by cost, and do not derive the order from price — the agreement between the two orders is a coincidence to re-check whenever a rung or a gateway changes, never a design. "The top rung" and "the most expensive row" remain two different facts; read the rung from the `agentMeta` annotation, never from the model string.
  - ⚠️ **The lesson is the one this document keeps re-learning, one level up: a rule justified by a measurement inherits that measurement's bugs.** The finding was quoted in four files as settled evidence, and each citation made it harder to question. It was retracted only because a vendor price table was read against the code — not by anything failing.

- **Config (`billing/model-tiers.ts` + `billing/rates.ts` `getModelTier`/`getModelTiers`, `.env.example`) — the price side lives in the Marketplace price list (2026-07-18):** each paid rung's `*_MODEL` is a **SELECTOR**, accepted only if the ACTIVE price list prices it in its own right; `PREMIUM_INPUT_DOLLARS`/`PREMIUM_OUTPUT_DOLLARS` are **retired and refused if set** (cache rates are family policy, not a per-deploy knob — derived 0.1×/2.0× for `claude-*`, an atomic quoted pair for `gpt-*`, full input rate for `gemini-*` — so a half-repriced rung cannot be expressed on any family; `spec/billing.md`). Each `*_MINIMUM_CREDITS` stays env (`envNumber` — a threshold, not a price). `model-tiers.ts` is deliberately **zero-import** (`MODEL_TIER_IDS`, `PAID_MODEL_TIERS`, `STANDARD_TIER_LABEL`) so the ladder's shape can be read by both the billing math and the client without dragging either into the other; `getModelTiers` takes the standard model as a PARAMETER because `rates.ts` may not import `agent/config.ts` (the cycle documented at `mostExpensive`). A rung whose selector cannot be priced is reported, never thrown.
- **🔴 EVERY RUNG IS INJECTED INTO EVERY PROVIDER'S RATE TABLE, FILL-A-GAP AND NEVER OVERWRITE (`providerRates`).** This is the single most load-bearing line in the feature, and it only became load-bearing when Premium moved to `claude-opus-5` — a model **Anthropic prices natively**. Before that, `PREMIUM_MODEL` was `claude-fable-5`, which **`MODEL_RATES` carried no row for** — wrongly, as it turned out (see the correction below): Anthropic does price it. While the row was missing, filling and overwriting were the same operation and an unconditional overwrite would have looked correct forever. **Measured live 2026-07-31:** a Premium turn on Anthropic billed **$2.5414 = the native $5/$25 exactly**; had the KIE-shaped $2/$10 row overwritten Anthropic's own, the same turn would have billed $1.0166 → **407 credits instead of 1,017**. Per-rung failures are caught individually so one broken selector cannot drop another rung's row, and the whole injection is non-throwing so a promoted list that unprices a rung refuses NEW requests loudly (`getTierModel`) without taking in-flight settlement down.

  🔴 ⚠️ **This bullet closed with "It is also what makes `claude-fable-5` (no `MODEL_RATES` row) billable on Anthropic at all — proven live in the same drive." That sentence described the DEFECT (corrected 2026-08-12).** Anthropic prices Fable 5 at $10/$50; the live drive it cites was billing it at KIE's $4/$20 and the "proof" was a 2.5× under-charge on a live rung. Both shipped Claude rungs are natively priced on every gateway now, so **the gap-fill fires for no current configuration** — it stays for a non-Claude rung selector (`PREMIUM_MODEL=gpt-5-6-sol` was a real deploy), which no Anthropic table can ever carry. The guard's correctness never depended on which model a rung names, which is exactly why it is a CONDITION and not a choice of model: whether a provider prices a model itself is a question about the table, not about our beliefs.
- **The minimum-credits gate PROTECTS the free grant.** The signup grant (1000) sits below the shipped threshold (1500), so a brand-new account **cannot** burn its grant on a paid rung out the gate — it must buy a credit pack or subscribe (crossing the threshold) first. There is deliberately **no separate subscription check**: holding the credits IS the proof of intent, and gating on a subscription would wrongly punish a credit-pack buyer who never subscribed. The threshold stays an env var per rung, so a re-added rung can be separated without a code change. **A threshold binds regardless of `BILLING_ENFORCED` (2026-07-18):** the old "enforcement off → premium freely usable, nobody is charged" bypass rested on a false premise — settlement debits the ledger either way (enforcement only lets the gate refuse at zero), so an under-threshold user could switch on an expensive rung and ride the balance negative with nothing objecting (observed live at 320 credits). The balance is always being debited, so the balance is always the eligibility fact; a deploy that wants a free paid rung states it explicitly with that rung's `*_MINIMUM_CREDITS=0`.
- **EVERY paid rung is locked on the FIRST BUILD TURN — a per-rung `firstBuildLocked` flag, not a premium-only rule.** The evidence is Fable 5 (2026-07-18, observed live): KIE serves it with a BUFFERED answer (accepted for edit-sized replies), and a creation-sized artifact (~25k output tokens, 4–7 min of decode) cannot flush before KIE's gateway timeout — measured: step 2 ran 307.8s streaming 17,635 chars of reasoning and died at `finish=error` with the artifact never arriving, 449s total. The lock was **generalized to the whole ladder rather than kept on the rung that produced it**, because the first build is the largest artifact in the product and the safe direction is to assume the next expensive model has the same problem until a live drive says otherwise. It is expressed as data (`firstBuildLocked`) so relaxing it later for a streaming model is config, not surgery — and ⚠️ **it HAS been relaxed: every rung ships `firstBuildLocked: false` since 2026-08-03 (owner decision), so no rung is first-build locked today.** This paragraph said the default was `true` until 2026-08-11 while a line further down already said the lock is "asserted absent" — one section stating both. The history above is why the flag exists and is data rather than a branch; it is not a description of current behaviour. `decideModelTier` takes `isFirstBuildTurn` (renamed from `isCreationTurn` 2026-07-29, §4.4a — the flag drives ten BEHAVIOURAL decisions and no longer any pricing one) and returns `reason: 'creation_turn'`; creations run the platform default, and every paid rung unlocks from the first edit turn. Silent by design — the creation is not degraded.
- **The decision is a PURE function (`billing/premium.ts` `decideModelTier`), exhaustively tested** — same category as the auto-repair loop and restore-target selection: it spends credits at a higher rate without a second confirmation, so it is never inlined into the proxy. Decision order is **standard → unavailable → creation lock → threshold**; `unavailable` is checked BEFORE the first-build lock deliberately, because it is the only OPERATOR-fault reason and burying it behind a per-turn condition hides a broken selector on exactly the turns most likely to request the rung. The server re-derives eligibility on **every** generation from the balance the credit gate already read; a tampered client store only ever reveals a rung the server declines (with a soft `tierDeclinedNotice` — "<Tier> needs N credits — used the standard model" — never a block). The resolved rung and its reason ride the log line and the `agentMeta` annotation, so a paid turn is identifiable in the generation log; the model string alone stopped being sufficient the moment a rung and the platform default could name one model — which they can whenever a rung's selector and the platform default resolve to one model — so read the rung from the annotation, never from the model string. ⚠️ This cited `PREMIUM_MODEL=claude-fable-5` / `LLM_MODEL=claude-opus-5` as the live example until 2026-08-11; the deploy now reads `LLM_MODEL=claude-sonnet-5`, `PREMIUM_MODEL=claude-opus-5`, `PLATINUM_MODEL=claude-fable-5`. **A spec should state the RULE and cite config as dated evidence — a live config quoted in the present tense goes stale silently.**
- **`modelTiersSessionHint` degrades a rung to OFF, never to ON, and never throws** — `/api/me` runs on every page load, and the 2026-07-25 incident was exactly one unguarded tier lookup taking the whole app down for every user. Every rung is an independent way to be mid-move, so each lookup is individually try/caught and a rung whose config threw is reported with its baked fallback and locked. **`available` and `serveable` are two fields on purpose:** `available` is a page-load SNAPSHOT (can this user have it right now?) while `serveable` is an operator fact (is this rung configured at all?). Folding them forces one of two silent failures — a user who buys credits mid-session stays locked on the screen they just paid on, or a misconfigured rung renders enabled and hard-fails on use. The client therefore reads `serveable` from the server and recomputes affordability LIVE from `balance` (`canUseTier`), so `applySettlement` re-locks a rung mid-session.
- **A rung may name a model from ANY of the four families, on any gateway (2026-08-04, extended 2026-08-10; §4.2a + `spec/model-families.md`).** `LLM_MODEL` / `PREMIUM_MODEL` are still pure SELECTORS and every existing refusal rule holds unchanged — unpriced → `NotConfiguredError`; unaffordable → resolve DOWN to Standard; `serveable` vs `available` stay separate fields; the provider must LIST what we price. The only thing that changed is that the selector's *family* (and therefore its wire, its effort dial, its cache economics and its delivery mode) is now derived from the model id rather than assumed to be Claude. Cross-family is safe by construction on the conversation side too: the history strip is keyed on message ROLE and is **never** family-conditional, precisely because a tier decline or an `LLM_MODEL` change means turn N and turn N+1 of one chat can run different families. ⚠️ **No rung has been reassigned across families** — all three still name Claude models, and which models earn rungs is an operator decision after a manual eval (output quality on `gpt-*`/`gemini-*` is unevaluated; there are no output-quality evals at all).
- **UI (`components/chat/ModelTierPill.tsx` + `ModelTierPanel.tsx`):** a credits-mode composer **pill** (NOT behind `byokUnlocked`) that always names the model actually in use, plus a **popover picker** cloned from the `/effort` panel. Clicking the pill opens the picker rather than toggling — a two-state control cannot express an N-state choice, and cycling rungs per click would make the most expensive one reachable by an accidental double-click (kept as-is through the drop to two rungs and the return to three: the ladder's length is DATA, and each time a toggle would have had to be un-built — the picker needed no change in either direction, which is the argument for it). The pill shows the **EFFECTIVE** rung, never the stored preference: a user who chose Premium and spent below its threshold sees Standard, because Standard is what their next build will run. A locked row **EXPLAINS and is never HTML-`disabled`** (§4.1a's dead-end rule), and the lock reasons are told apart because they need different actions — below-threshold (add credits, and the row names the number) and unserveable (no amount of credits helps, so the row must NOT quote a threshold); the retired first-build lock is asserted absent. The preference persists client-side under `modelTier` (`modelTierStore`, migrating a legacy `premiumModelEnabled === true` to `'premium'`); `/api/me` surfaces `credits.modelTiers = { standardModel, tiers: [{ id, label, model, minimumCredits, available, serveable }] }` as a rendering hint only. Rung LABELS come from the server (they are facts about the operator's config); the credit-consequence DESCRIPTIONS are client-side in one place so the picker rows cannot drift into two versions of the same sentence. **Every rung shows its RESOLVED MODEL NAME ACROSS ALL FOUR FAMILIES** (owner decision, 2026-08-04; the pill and picker already rendered `parseModel(tier.model)` — what changed is that the PARSER can now read non-Claude ids, which previously fell through to the raw string) — pill tooltip and picker row, e.g. a Premium rung reading "GPT 5.6 Sol" beside its label — sourced from `modelTiersSessionHint`'s per-tier `model`, **never a client-side model table**: `parseModel` DERIVES the display name from the id's shape (a run of numeric segments is one version — `gpt-5-6-sol` → "GPT 5.6 Sol"; a vowel-less word segment is an initialism; Claude keeps its vendor-word-dropping branch because the pill's width is the toolbar's scarcest resource), and an id it cannot read is rendered **verbatim, never blanked**. A lookup table would have to be edited every time KIE ships a model, and would silently mis-render or blank every id it had not been taught — which is the same reason `LLM_MODEL` is allowed to name a model this build has never heard of (§4.2a).
- **Verified live 2026-07-31** — three generations at three distinct, exactly-recomputed rates with the tier named in a persisted `agentMeta`, and a first build with a paid rung selected that ran Sonnet 5 to completion and was not billed at the paid rate. ⚠️ **The 2026-08-08 two-rung change is NOT live-verified** — it is typecheck-, test- and mutation-verified (seven mutations) with no generation driven through it. ⚠️ **Owed and NOT fixed:** `ContextIndicator.tsx` renders the RAW model id (`claude-sonnet-5`) where the pill and picker render "Sonnet 5" through the shared `parseModel`, and it ignores the `tier`/`tierReason` now on `agentMeta` — correct, but a third surface answering one label question with no shared source. Also `ModelTierPill.tsx` and `Chat.client.tsx` narrow the selected rung DIFFERENTLY (the pill applies `!creationTurn && canUseTier(…)`, the wire `canUseTier(…)` alone), so on a first build the pill shows Standard while the body says the selected paid rung; the end state is correct because the server declines to `standard(creation_turn)`, but that equality is held by reasoning across two files and one server rule with nothing asserting the two derivations agree.

### 4.7 Guided Tour Wizard ("Prompt Magic")

Non-developer front door at `/new/tour`, reached ONLY per §4.4a Path C (explicit "guided tour" click, or an offer when a prompt is too vague to act on). It never interrupts a user who typed a specific prompt. Four steps → compiled structured first message (hidden behind a friendly summary):

1. **Game type** — one card per active `game_registry` entry (GameMode preset + optional base scene).
2. **Vibe** — art/lighting presets (Sunset Arcade, Neon Night, Low-Poly Daylight, Moody Fog…) mapping to known skybox/lighting/post-process configs from the training examples.
3. **Mechanics** — genre-appropriate checkboxes, each mapping to a prompt fragment referencing a known Toolkit pattern (e.g., for a racing genre: lap counter, boost pads, drift via the built-in RacingSystem; for a platformer: double-jump, collectibles, moving platforms — defined per genre in wizard config), plus cross-genre toggles the Toolkit makes one-fragment cheap: **mobile touch controls** (MobileInputController — makes the shared game phone-playable) and **split-screen local multiplayer** (DefaultCameraSystem, 1–4 views).
4. **Your twist** — one optional free-text sentence.

Wizard creates the project from the template and drops the user into the builder with the **stock starter running** and a prompt waiting in the textbox — it obeys the §4.4a project-first rule like every other path (revised 2026-07-29; it previously landed the user in the builder *with generation already streaming*).

**The box holds the SUMMARY; the compiled prompt rides HIDDEN.** `startProject` seeds both strings (`prompt` = `compileWizardPrompt(selection)`, `visiblePrompt` = `summarizeSelection(selection)`) and `draftTextForSeed` prefers `visiblePrompt` — correct on Path A, where the visible string is what the user actually typed, and on Path C it means the box holds *"Racing · Neon Night · Drifting, Lap timing · 'shopping carts'"*, which is right — a prompt the user is about to edit should be one they can read. The compiled fragments are not lost: they are **appended hidden to the creation brief** (a *"The user's guided-tour selections"* section, 2026-07-29), so four wizard steps of explicit choices still reach the model on the first build turn. That preserves §4.7's original promise — a compiled structured message hidden behind a friendly summary — under the new two-step shape. ⚠️ Do NOT "fix" this by flipping `draftTextForSeed`'s preference: that drops a machine-composed task list into a box the user is meant to read and edit.

The **< 90 second** target survives the split but stops meaning one thing, so do not read one figure as both: creation-to-running-starter is the platform's own number to defend (clone → install → dev server, no model involved), while wizard-completion-to-first-playable now additionally contains a build turn the *user* has to send — an unbounded human pause plus a per-token generation. Target the 90s at creation and instrument first-playable separately. Wizard config (genres, vibes, mechanics, fragments) is versioned JSON data, not code.

### 4.8 Share, Gallery, Remix & Export

> **Status: IMPLEMENTED (Stage 4, 2026-07).** Server: publishing checklist (`share/checklist.ts` — a secret is a BLOCKING refusal, a debug overlay a warning the user may accept, a network game auto-launched `?solo=true`); byte-faithful build upload with a path-traversal wall (`share/publish.ts`); a published project served origin-isolated (`app.$.tsx`; local dev is explicitly NOT the security boundary). **Addressing rebuilt 2026-08-03:** `SHARE_DOMAIN` alone decides the shape — unset means local dev (`localhost:5173/app/<shareId>`, bytes from `.data/storage`), set means deployed (`arcade-racer-k7m2p9qx4nrt.codewrx.app`, bytes from S3). The route is `/app`, not `/play`, because a generated game's OWN gameplay route is `/play` (§4.4c) and the old shape nested one meaning inside the other. The slug is decoration and the trailing id is identity, so names never collide and a rename never breaks a pasted link. Host→path rewriting happens at the SERVER ENTRY, never in a route; gallery returns admin-APPROVED only (`share/gallery.ts` — an allow-list projection, never the owner/project id); remix (`share/remix.ts` — `deriveRemix` names exactly what travels: files yes, ownership/link/share id no); anonymous report → admin queue (`share/reports.ts`). Client: a **Share** button + dialog (`components/share/` — builds in the WebContainer, renders the checklist's blocking/warning outcomes, shows the live link), the **`/gallery`** page, and the **`/remix/:shareId`** flow (clones then opens in the builder via the server-checkpoint resume path). Export ZIP already existed (binary-faithful, `workbench.downloadZip`). A minimal brand module (`app/config/brand.ts`) keeps the play badge from being a hardcoded mark. **Self-remix/Duplicate UI: BUILT (2026-07-15)** — a header `DuplicateButton` POSTs `{projectId}` to `/api/remix` and opens the clone via the resume path.

> **UPDATE 2026-07-28 (T17b — 🔴 every published game was broken, and always had been):** publish succeeded end-to-end while the game it published could not boot — root-absolute asset URLs under the `/play/:shareId/` prefix (the template's `base: "/"` shipped in the initial import, its comment claiming the opposite), and beneath that a router basename that matched nothing. Nobody had ever LOADED a published game; every test asserted a step short of "a stranger can play it". Fixed at three layers, live-verified (`/play/:id` plays): **(a)** the share build runs `--base=./` via `SHARE_BUILD_COMMAND` (`app/lib/runtime/build-command.ts`, exact-match allow-list — the model's output can only SELECT between fixed argv arrays), with a server backstop (`rootAbsoluteEntryRefs` → `RootAbsoluteAssetError`, 422 before any write) so the defect can never ship silently again; **(b)** the template resolves its router basename at runtime (`appBasename()`) and reloads prefix-preserving; **(c)** the serve layer (`share/serve.ts` `resolvePlayRequest`) answers in three modes — **asset**, **game document**, or **wrapper**: the wrapper's iframe loads the DIRECTORY URL with `?embed=1` (never `/index.html`, which leaves a route no router matches); `embed` or `sec-fetch-dest: iframe` gets the game document for any extensionless path (SPA fallback scoped to the iframe); a no-signal browser degrades to the wrapper — never 404, never recursion. Publishing mid-generation is refused by `decidePublishReadiness` (running `start` actions excluded — the dev server runs forever).

- **Share:** enforce the Toolkit **Publishing Checklist** first (agent-directed pre-share pass + build-time lint: `enableDebugKeys=false` on DebugInformation, debug overlays off, network-capable games forced `?solo=true` at launch — open question #17) → run `npm run build` in the WebContainer → upload `dist/` to the S3 play bucket (CloudFront-served) → mint `share_id`. `/play/[shareId]` serves the static build full-screen with a "Made with Babylon Toolkit — Remix this game" badge.
- **Remix:** clones the source snapshot into the visitor's account (sign-up gate) → opens in builder. Primary growth loop. **Self-remix / Duplicate:** any owner can remix their OWN projects (no share required) to spin variations — "Duplicate project" is the same snapshot-clone path within one account.
- **Gallery:** curated grid of shared games at `/gallery`; submission flag on share; admin curation. Playing is free to us (static hosting) — the zero-cost "take a peek" funnel.
- **Export (ALL users, never gated):** ZIP download of a proper Toolkit project (official Project Installation layout + README). Alongside **GitHub Sync (§4.13, also ungated)**, this keeps the users-own-their-games promise (§5A) true for everyone — no project is ever locked to the platform.

### 4.9 Assets Tab & GLTF Store

> **Status: IMPLEMENTED (Stage 4, 2026-07).** Server: asset introspection — the "agent knows what it just got" — `assets/introspect.ts` scans a glTF's `CVTOOLS_unity_metadata`/`extras` into a component reference injected into agent context, `assets/glb.ts` unwraps the GLB JSON chunk (doubling as structural validation), `assets/validate.ts` gates uploads (type allow-list, size caps, per-user quota, content sniff). `user_assets` store + upload route (introspects models at upload time) + static catalog (`app/config/assets.json`).
