- Fixed costs: AWS Lightsail (~$7–15/mo per env) + S3/CloudFront (cents→dollars), Supabase tier, ~~StackBlitz plan~~ (**never purchased — priced out 2026-07-31, §6/A.2; $0**), **BrowserPod Pro $20/mo if the §8 pilot passes**, Stripe fees, domains — tens of dollars/month at beta scale.
- Check Anthropic startup/credit programs and current API pricing before launch (docs.claude.com / anthropic.com).

## 8. Escape Hatch: Sandbox Swap (WebContainers → server containers) — **SEAM BUILT 2026-07-26**

Trigger conditions: StackBlitz terms unacceptable at scale, or WebContainer limits (install speed, memory alongside Babylon scenes) hurt UX. A third arrived in practice — **StackBlitz not responding to the licensing approach at all**; a dependency you cannot get a contract from is the same risk as one whose contract you cannot afford, and §6/`spec/licensing.md` make that plan a hard Phase-3 gate.

🔴 **The trigger FIRED, 2026-07-31: StackBlitz answered, and the answer was ~$10,000 per 8,000 API calls.** The escape hatch is no longer hypothetical — it is the shipping architecture. **WebContainer is local-dev only from here** (code retained per §2.1a; never served to external users), CodeSandbox is the server-side runtime, and a **third provider is being piloted to restore the browser-side option** WebContainer used to occupy. §6/A.2 record the closed negotiation; the cost of losing browser-side compute is priced in CREDITS.md (a Nano VM-hour is +50% on raw cost, ~72–75% GM → **~58–63%**).

### §8c Provider matrix — evaluated 2026-07-31 (do not re-litigate without new facts)

| Option | Compute | Cost | Licence | Verdict |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| **CodeSandbox** | their microVM | **$0.149/hr Nano** (measured) | commercial SaaS, self-serve | ✅ **SHIPPING.** Stays. Owner: *"it's working and the accounting is pretty good right now."* |
| **WebContainer** | browser | ~$0 marginal | **~$10k/8k calls** | 🔴 **PRICED OUT.** In-tree, local dev only. |
| **BrowserPod** (Leaning Technologies) | browser | **$0.01/pod-hr** (10 tokens/boot/hr @ $0.001) — **and $0 when idle, which is the actual point** | **$20/mo Pro, commercial expressly permitted, self-serve, no negotiation** | 🟡 **PILOT, 2 spikes run 2026-07-31.** Platform half PASSES (pre-baked `userImage` → built starter live in 1.2s/21MB; OSC contract intact). Blocked: rolldown's dep optimizer never completes under BrowserPod — a VENDOR bug, reproducible on a stock Vite app; see item 5. |
| **Nodebox** (CodeSandbox) | browser | n/a | 🔴 **Sustainable Use License v1.0 — commercial use FORBIDDEN** | ❌ **REJECTED.** See below. |
| browser-vite (divRIOTS) | browser | free (MIT) | none | ❌ **Archived 2025-10-08.** Architecture blueprint only. |
| Nodepod | browser | free | MIT + Commons Clause | ❌ 163★ side project, vendor-stated perf claims, unverified. |
| DIY ESM + import maps + Service Worker | browser | **$0** | none | ⏸️ **Fallback if BrowserPod fails.** Abandons real Node/npm/Vite plugins. |
| E2B / Daytona / Fly Machines / Northflank / Modal | server | $0.033–0.083/hr, **idle ≈ free** | low | ⏸️ Cheaper than CodeSandbox and worth revisiting **only after** the T12 VM-hours report gives real numbers. Do not migrate off an unmeasured estimate. |

**Why Nodebox was rejected (it looks like the obvious answer and is not).** It is CodeSandbox's *other* runtime — a hand-written JS reimplementation of Node's APIs in Web Workers, powering Sandpack embeds; codesandbox.io itself runs Firecracker. Four independent disqualifiers: **(a)** the **Sustainable Use License v1.0** permits use "only for your own internal business purposes or for non-commercial or personal use" — the same trap as StackBlitz, from the vendor we already pay, and Sandpack's Apache-2.0 explicitly does *not* cover it (their FAQ names the Vite/Node/Next/Astro templates as the restricted ones); **(b)** **no real shell** — `shell.runCommand(binary, args)` with no bash and no rc file, so `app/utils/shell.ts`'s `\x1b]654;` OSC contract is *unsatisfiable* and every agent-issued shell action would hang silently forever; **(c)** **abandoned** — last npm publish and last commit both 2023-11-29, with an open issue literally titled *"Hi, is the project active?"* (Oct 2025) unanswered; **(d)** the runtime is a **closed blob** served from `nodebox-runtime.codesandbox.io`, `npm install` is a call to the Sandpack CDN, and previews are on `*.preview.csb.app` — three uncontrolled third-party dependencies on a dead codebase.

🔴 **WHY A BROWSER RUNTIME AT ALL — IT IS THE ABSENCE OF A CLOCK, NOT THE HOURLY RATE (owner, 2026-07-31).** *"You do not have the hibernate support that is a MUST with cloud based VM, so when the user walks away from an open VM for an hour you are not paying for the wasted VM time. That was the ONLY reason I preferred the local browser based WebContainers."* A server VM bills wall-time whether or not anyone is looking at it, so idleness becomes an engineering problem (§8c T4 teardown, T5 hibernate-oldest) that must be built, tuned and trusted, and every gap in it is money. A browser runtime has no clock: an idle tab is **$0** and the walk-away case needs no machinery because it cannot occur. The margin arithmetic (a Nano VM-hour is **+50% on raw cost**, GM ~72–75% → **~58–63%**, CREDITS.md) is the second reason, and the $0.149 vs $0.01 per hour is the third. **Do not re-frame this as a cost-per-hour comparison and conclude that a cheaper server VM solves it — it does not; it is the same problem at a lower rate.**

**The BrowserPod pilot was gated on four measurements. TWO SPIKES RAN on 2026-07-31 and `spec/sandbox-browserpod.md` records every number. Verdict: the economic case is PROVEN and the original blocker is SOLVED, but a narrower new blocker replaced it — still DO NOT start the provider yet.** Spike 2 answered the open item from spike 1 (`userImage`) decisively in favour: a **pre-baked ext2 disk boots the fully-installed starter in 1.2s pulling 21MB of a 1.4GB image**, because `createHttpDevice` is a **lazy range-backed block device** we can serve from our own S3 — so "the starter does not install" is retired, and with it the fear that real Node workloads are fundamentally too heavy for a browser. What blocks it now is **Vite's dependency optimizer deadlocking** under Wasm Node (an ordinary bug in one tool, with an untried and plausible workaround), which is why **first paint of a real Babylon app has still never been observed**. The four, with outcomes:
1. **Preview latency with a real Babylon payload** — **RUN: ⚠️ CONCERNING, not yet disqualifying.** Measured through a live portal: a 5KB module is **164/167/346ms (min/median/max over 9 samples)** and the real 2MB `havok.wasm` takes **1,044ms then 884ms (~1.9–2.3 MB/s)**. ~165ms is a hard floor per request because there is no service worker — every request goes browser → Cloudflare Worker → WebSocket → back into the same browser. Vite's `optimizeDeps` bundles `@babylonjs/*` into few chunks, but app source is per-module, so first paint of the REAL starter is the number that decides this and it **cannot be measured until item 2 is solved**.
2. ~~**Does the real AppTemplate starter install and build**~~ — **RUN 2026-07-31: 🔴 in-pod install FAILS, ✅ but `userImage` RETIRES the question, and the original reasoning behind this item was half wrong.** The predicted blocker (Vite 8 / rolldown being a Rust native binary, needing `esbuild-wasm`/`@rollup/wasm-node` overrides) **did not materialise**: on `linux-wasm32` npm resolves `@rolldown/binding-wasm32-wasi` by itself and `vite/8.2.0` runs unmodified. In-pod install fails on **scale** (the full `@babylonjs/*` set plus AWS SDK devDeps on 1 core stalls: 0 packages after 30 min with the lockfile, 161 dirs/0 MB after 12 min without; `vite` alone installs in 43s). **Spike 2 removed the need to install at all.** `boot({ userImage })` takes **any http(s) URL we host** and `createHttpDevice` range-reads it lazily, so a pre-built disk costs almost nothing to boot: host-side `npm install --os=linux --cpu=wasm32` (**304 pkgs, 34s**) → `mke2fs -d` (**14s, 1.4GB**) → **boot in 1.13–1.28s using 3 range requests / 0.25MB**, tree byte-correct (symlinks survive, zero `.node` binaries in 81,494 files), **Vite dev server + portal in 7.0s** having pulled **~21MB (1.5%)** of the image. This is the direct analogue of the CodeSandbox fork-from-alias template and slots into **§4.4 pin-and-promote** unchanged — an image is an immutable artifact keyed by the template SHA. It also deletes the per-file mount, `waitForMountVisible` and `settleAfterCreation` for the starter, since the files precede our first line of code. ⚠️ Build it on **Linux in CI at the same absolute path the pod mounts** — see item 5.
3. ~~**Havok initialises in the portal iframe**~~ — **RUN: ❓ INCONCLUSIVE (harness bug — an Emscripten glue/wasm mismatch in the probe, not a BrowserPod failure; do not cite it).** Real finding: the preview has a **real GPU** (`WebGL2`, `ANGLE Metal Renderer: Apple M1 Ultra`, WebGPU adapter resolved). ⚠️ **Spike 1's isolation claim was WRONG and spike 2 corrects it:** previews are not un-isolatable — **the dev server has to ask**. Spike 1 ran a bare vite that sent no COOP/COEP; with the starter's real config the portal document returns `COOP: same-origin` + `CORP: cross-origin` + 🔴 **`COEP: require-corp` — Cloudflare UPGRADED the `credentialless` the starter asked for**, which is precisely the mode that blocks a cross-origin subresource lacking CORP. So the `repo.babylontoolkit.com` CORP question is **still unanswered and now sharper**. (Measured at response-header level; `crossOriginIsolated` was not re-read inside the document under the real config.)
4. ~~**The OSC rc trick works**~~ — **RUN 2026-07-31: ✅ PASSES.** The byte-identical `OSC_BASHRC` block from `codesandbox-provider.ts`, written to `/home/user/.bp_rc` and launched with `bash --rcfile … -i`, emits `begin` / `exit=0:N` / `prompt` with **real exit codes** (`false` → `exit=0:1`), consumed via xterm's `parser.registerOscHandler(654, …)`. ⚠️ **But only through the DEFAULT xterm terminal on a hidden mounted DOM element**: `createCustomTerminal` (headless) cannot run processes at all — the process never starts, never resolves, and **poisons the pod** so that subsequent `fs` calls and terminals hang too (reproduced 5× across 2.18.0 and 2.15.0). That makes DOM ownership a property of this provider, unlike every other one.

5. 🔴 **THE BLOCKER, ROOT CAUSE (spike 4, 2026-07-31): `Atomics.wait` NEVER RETURNS in BrowserPod — which deadlocks every Vite dependency optimizer. It is not rolldown, not Vite 8, and not our AppTemplate.** Spike 3 blamed rolldown; the owner's follow-up (*"is Vite the only framework… try something else"*) found the real defect one layer down. A **20-line probe with no bundler**: `Atomics.wait(arr,0,0,15000)` on the main thread never returns **and its 15s timeout never fires** (a spec violation in its own right), while the notifying worker never runs (`nproc`=1). Plain `postMessage` workers DO work — so `worker_threads` is fine; it is *blocking* that deadlocks. That one primitive is how both wasm bundlers do synchronous cross-thread calls, which explains every hang: **Vite 8** stock `npm create vite` (224s), same with everything-but-React excluded (357s), and **Vite 7.3.6 + the documented `esbuild-wasm`/`@rollup/wasm-node` overrides** (232s) — all with `node_modules/.vite/deps_temp_<hash>` created and never committed, pod answering `bash` in 0.5s throughout. Disable the optimizer and modules serve in **170–631 ms**. ✅ **AND THE WORKLOAD RUNS:** with no bundler at all (static server + import map) **BabylonJS renders in-pod at 6,033ms and Havok initialises (2,094,566 bytes) at 7,703ms** — first time observed inside a pod; host controls for both Vite 8 and Vite 7 also pass (Toolkit 138 exports, Havok init in 1,012ms). ⚠️ The **Toolkit cannot be used unbundled** — it re-exports via `@babylonjs/core`'s barrel, so it pulls all of Babylon as thousands of modules over a ~165ms relay (all 84 re-export targets individually return 200) — which is exactly why a bundler is non-negotiable and why this defect blocks the product. ⚠️ Also found: a **2.6GB `userImage` fails to boot** (`Invalid 'deviceUrl'`) while 1.5GB works — the stock `user.ext2` is 2,097,152,000B, so the ceiling is ~2GiB. **Action: send `scratchpad/REPRO-browserpod-vite.md` to Leaning and revisit.** Containment (bake `node_modules/.vite` on Linux at the pod's path so the optimizer never runs) still works but breaks on `npm install <pkg>`, an agent primitive.
6. ✅🔴 **AND THE WHOLE STACK RUNS IN A POD — but the EDIT LOOP is a SECOND vendor gap (spike 5, 2026-07-31).** The owner rejected the "blocked" verdict (*"is vite and a bundler the only way… what about webpack… UMD and script tags"*) and was right: **"you need a bundler" was wrong — you need bundled OUTPUT, which can be produced anywhere.** Bundling with **webpack on the host** and serving the result statically from the pod runs the complete product stack: **BabylonJS rendering 2,492ms · Babylon Toolkit 116 exports 6,420ms · Havok initialised (2,094,566 bytes) 7,142ms**, with boot 0.9s → portal 1.9s → done **9.5s**. Same shape as everything else here: do the tool-dependent work where the tools work (CI), ship it in the `userImage`, let the pod serve. **webpack is pure JS** (verified: zero `.node`, zero `.wasm` in the toolchain) so it sidesteps `Atomics.wait` entirely and genuinely runs in-pod — but 🔴 a **cold build took >11.5 min and did not finish** (host: 3.6s), and 🔴 the fix for that, webpack's persistent cache, **crashes on an incomplete `node:v8`**: `DefaultSerializer._setTreatArrayBufferViewsAsHostObjects is not a function`. A warm host rebuild is **1.9s over 3,510 cached modules**, so that one missing method is the whole difference between a usable in-pod edit loop and none — **a far cheaper ask than the `Atomics` fix, and the one to push hardest**. Both are in `scratchpad/REPRO-browserpod-vite.md`. **Net: we could ship view-only today (CI build → baked `dist/`); we cannot ship the agent edit loop without rebuilding on our servers, which claws back the compute saving that made BrowserPod attractive.**

⚠️ **Confirmed cost: the API is missing roughly half the seam contract** (the spike verified the shape of every gap below except `textSearch`). No `readdir`, no `rm`, no `stat`/`rename`, **no file watching**, **no process exit code**, **no `kill()`**, no atomic tree mount, and `teardown()` is a silent no-op. Leaning's own reference IDE shells out through `bash -c` for deletes and renames and calls it *"fire-and-trust… its view can lag the process world."* ⚠️ Spike 2 added four more, each of which would cost a day if met cold: **a wedged process can poison LATER servers in the same pod, non-deterministically** (this invalidated two of the spike's own bisection runs — budget one pod per measurement); **backgrounding the host tab permanently kills the portal relay** (fetches hang forever afterwards even refocused, while the pod itself stays alive — the pod's network is only as alive as its tab); **`node -v`/`node -p` do not work** at all (`handleExecve` treats the first arg as a module path — and `node <script>` hung where `npx <bin>` started in 3s); and **piping node's stdout through bash silently loses it**, so output capture must not be built on shell redirection. The compounding one: **no watch AND no readdir means we are blind to files the agent creates in-pod**, which bears directly on §4.2.8's "the model only ever sees visible" and on `waitForMountVisible`/`settleAfterCreation`. Budget a real shim and expect it to be lossy. Other standing risks: runtime is a closed blob `import()`ed from `rt.browserpod.io` (self-host is Enterprise-only), all pod networking tunnels `main.browserportal.io` with no SLA, the **API key is unavoidably client-side**, preview URLs are **public and unauthenticated**, and it is Chromium-only in practice despite the FAQ.

**The `SandboxProvider` seam now exists** (`app/lib/sandbox/`, `spec/sandbox-seam.md`): the interface declares its own types (it imports nothing from `@webcontainer/api`), a WebContainer adapter implements it, and `~/lib/sandbox` is the single place that decides which runtime backs the session. Every runtime store — files, previews, terminal, action runner, shell, mount, MCP bridge, git, search, deploy, share — is constructed with a `SandboxProvider`. Capability flags (`terminal` / `textSearch` / `watch`) let the UI degrade instead of throwing. Enforced by `sandbox-seam.spec.ts` as a **default-deny source scan with controls**, mutation-verified; verified live against a booted WebContainer (mount, byte-identity round-trip, watcher events, spawn, text search).

**The second provider is BUILT and driven live (2026-07-26/27): Together CodeSandbox** (`spec/sandbox-codesandbox.md` — chosen over Cloudflare because the disk SURVIVES hibernation, and over StackBlitz on licensing: self-serve API key + published rates vs. a vendor that does not answer). `codesandbox-provider.ts` + the server key-holder (`app/lib/.server/sandbox/`: per-user sandbox registry, session + preview-token two routes behind the verified-user wall, pure `decideSandboxStart`) + a build-time cutover switch (`VITE_SANDBOX_PROVIDER=codesandbox`; unset = WebContainer; the same flag drives `WORK_DIR` `/project/workspace` and drops COEP). Measured: alias-fork → playable preview in **7.6s** (template snapshot carries `node_modules` + a running dev server). The full creation path ran live through the real UI across three sessions, surfacing and fixing seven defects (OSC begin markers, port replay, preview tokenization, restore gating, inherited-port clearing among them). **Gating the cutover for real users (SUPERSEDED by the 2026-07-28 update below — the list is closed except metering and the A/B):** the 2026-07-27 review findings (`spec/sandbox-codesandbox.md` §11 — one CRITICAL: the per-user sandbox + warm-boot gate can adopt project A's files as project B; plus VM leak/reap/reset lifecycle, the `/home/project` literal family, `.codesandbox/` ignore rules, a shell-signal demux race), per-project `sandbox_id`, and the cost model: a `sandbox` ledger reason + metering sweep + overdraw policy + `packMargin()` re-run — VM time accrues **while the user is idle**, a billing shape the ledger has never had (§4.6). Until metering ships the VM cost is folded into `CREDIT_MARGIN` as a documented placeholder (CREDITS.md §"Sandbox compute": ~+25% raw ≈ 5–7 GM points at 4.0; 5.0 restores target; same placeholder applied to WebContainer builds by owner decision 2026-07-27 until real numbers exist on either side). The binary-bytes contract (§1.3 principle 10) also gains a network hop per read — bounded by `refresh-walk.ts`'s 8-way concurrency, unmeasured against the vendor's 3,600 req/hr cap. A/B against WebContainers per-deploy before cutover.

**UPDATE 2026-07-28 — the production plan executed (`_specs/codesandbox-production_plan.md`, T1–T16 + T17a/T17b shipped; T17 all ten scenarios live-driven; T17c fixed + live-verified 2026-07-29).** The paragraph above's gating list is **superseded**; every §11 finding is dispositioned in `spec/sandbox-codesandbox.md` (fixed-by-task or explicitly still-open). Current state:

- **Per-project sandboxes.** The per-user registry (`registry.ts`) is **DELETED** — `sandbox_id` is a nullable pointer on the project row (migration 0013, both stores, never on the client wire type as a comment-only claim: the exposure decision is recorded). The session/preview routes take a `projectId` behind `requireOwnedProject` (404-not-403); the create race converges by compare-and-set (loser VM disposed); `reset` disposes the old VM and creates are per-user rate-limited (a refused create is a described 429); a deleted VM falls back resume→create ONCE and records the new id (live-proven: fresh VM in 52s, no permanent 503, no silent template adoption). Client boot is lazy and project-pinned (a hibernated tab reconnects to the project it booted for; a reconnect that would CREATE fails loudly; preview URL cache keyed per (project, port)).
- **VM lifecycle.** Teardown on project delete (pinned by `delete-leaves-nothing.spec.ts`) + a one-shot legacy orphan sweep; running-VM cap `CODESANDBOX_MAX_RUNNING_VMS` (default **2**, hibernate-oldest, **never refuses** a session — hibernate is free and reversible); wake hook (a resumed VM with a dead dev server restarts it — live: preview back in 39s, exactly one vite, zero typing); append-only lifecycle marks → per-user VM-hours on the Admin tab.
- **Template pipeline.** CSB alias pin-and-promote mirrors §4.4 (`sandbox/template-pin.ts` + Admin panel; precedence promoted pin > `CODESANDBOX_TEMPLATE` > baked `btk@starter`; CodeSandbox holds the bytes, we hold only the pointer history — rollback is recorded, not byte-immutable). ⚠️ **Alias propagation lag (measured):** a fork 19s after promoting a freshly built alias got the PREVIOUS bytes (self-healed in minutes) — never trust the first fork after a promote; validate with a probe fork.
- **Deploy contract.** `VITE_SANDBOX_PROVIDER` is a BUILD-time switch baked into the image (`Dockerfile` ARG/ENV; rollback = deploy the previous image, never an env flip); `CODESANDBOX_*` documented in DEPLOY.md's SSM table; the SDK is lazily imported (its module-scope `createRequire` killed every route under workerd); health reports `codesandbox` config-presence-only, and only on builds that use it.
- **Billing.** VM cost folded into `CREDIT_MARGIN` as the documented placeholder (bake-vs-meter, below) — now ASSERTED (`effectivePackMargin` floor over packs AND plans, `billing/vm-cost.ts`) and MEASURED (T12 VM-hours report). The flat creation price charged exactly 500 on all three live creations — ⚠️ a **dated 2026-07-28 reading of the RETIRED `CREATION_FLAT_CREDITS`**, not a current price: since 2026-07-29 the flat charge is `PROJECT_CREATE_CREDITS` (default 150) on project registration and the build turn bills per-token (§4.4a, §4.6). The point it was making survives the reprice: there is no contradiction between the two rules — the flat price covers the creation, the placeholder covers VM time.
- **Live verification (T17, real provider, numbers in `spec/sandbox-codesandbox.md`):** ~26 REST calls/81min vs the 3,600/hr cap; byte-identical binaries across VM → ZIP and VM → publish → `/play`; two browsers on one VM clean; A→B→A project switching lands on the correct VMs; kill recovery 52s; wake 39s; Pico survives the publish build at 1,958/2,053MB peak.
- **Share serving (T17b — every published game was broken, and always had been):** the share build passes `--base=./` (`SHARE_BUILD_COMMAND`, exact-match allow-list), a `RootAbsoluteAssetError` 422 backstop refuses root-absolute entry HTML pre-write, the template resolves its router basename at runtime, and `resolvePlayRequest` serves three modes (asset / game document / wrapper — iframe loads the directory URL `?embed=1`; `embed` or `sec-fetch-dest: iframe` gets the game document for extensionless paths; no-signal browsers degrade to the wrapper). Publishing mid-generation is refused by `decidePublishReadiness`. Live-verified: `/play/:id` actually plays. See §4.8.
- **Still open before cutover:** metering (the `sandbox` ledger reason); the per-deploy A/B; the HMR-over-wss browser check. ~~T17c~~ **FIXED + LIVE-VERIFIED 2026-07-29** (`persistence/checkpoint-run.ts` — settle-wait, per-attempt timeout, spaced retries, LOUD failure; `dist/` excluded at the map layer; live: seq 0/1/2 per generation and kill-recovery restored the POST-EDIT state). T17 scenario 3 (Assets-tab upload) was driven 2026-07-28; the owner promoted the template and committed the AppTemplate changes 2026-07-29.

### §8 Decisions log (sandbox cutover — newest last)

1. **Bake, don't meter (owner, 2026-07-27; formalized T11 2026-07-28).** VM cost is folded into `CREDIT_MARGIN`/pack prices at launch — no per-user metering, no `sandbox` ledger reason yet — because the reporting to justify metering did not exist. The fold-in must be visible: two explicit inputs (`SANDBOX_VM_USD_PER_HOUR` measured, `SANDBOX_EST_VM_HOURS_PER_KCREDIT` estimated) and a tested margin floor over packs AND plans. T12's VM-hours report is the measurement that decides whether metering ever needs to exist.
2. **Cap = 2 running VMs per user, hibernate-oldest, NEVER refuse (owner, 2026-07-27; T5).** Hibernate is free and reversible, so the cap makes room instead of refusing a session — a refusal would hold a user's project hostage to their own tab habits.
3. **The T17b fix set (2026-07-28):** platform-side CLI `--base=./` override (repairs the installed base with no rebuild) AND template-side runtime `appBasename()` (stops producing the bug) AND serve-layer game-document routing (`resolvePlayRequest`) — all three, because each fixes a different copy of the defect; a serve-layer rewrite of built bytes was rejected (served bytes must equal built bytes).
4. **T17c filed 2026-07-28; DECIDED + FIXED 2026-07-29.** The decision taken: strict serialization STAYS strict (a checkpoint missing a file still refuses to write — §4.12's poisoned-checkpoint rule), and the policy around it buys time and honesty instead of partial maps — wait for the turn's actions to settle (bounded), time-box each serialize attempt (a dead sandbox connection HANGS `fs` calls rather than erroring — measured), retry with spacing, and make every failure LOUD (toast + retry-next-turn + pessimistic `workingCopySafe`); the conversation save is decoupled so a file-serialize failure cannot lose the transcript; `dist/` is excluded at the map layer (regenerated by any build, never tracked). Live-verified on the real provider: checkpoints advance per generation and kill-recovery restores the latest state.

4a. **🔴 A FAILED RESTORE WRITE MUST NOT ENTER THE FILE MAP — the phantom that permanently disabled checkpointing (found + fixed 2026-08-09, `FilesStore.restoreFiles`).** Reported as *"I am losing edits, all my AI edits are not surviving a refresh… not saved to indexedDB or wherever local changes should be saved until the changes are synced with github"* — and the report was exactly right about the consequence while pointing at the wrong layer. `writeSerializedFileMap`'s `onError` fires ONLY when the write threw, i.e. the bytes never landed; `restoreFiles` then recorded the **whole incoming map** (`restorable`) into the store regardless, leaving a map key with no file behind it. Nothing notices at the time. But binaries are the ONLY entries `serializeFiles` reads from disk (text comes straight from the map), so on every later turn strict serialize hits that key, gets ENOENT, and throws — and strict serialize is what `checkpointProject` runs, returning **before `createLocalSnapshot` AND before `saveWorkingCopy`**. The project therefore keeps NO local checkpoint and NO recovery copy, on every turn, and because the phantom is re-recorded from the same incoming map on each mount it never clears. For a LINKED project that is total loss of unsynced work: with no local checkpoint `selectMountSource` sees `localSeq === undefined` and returns `repo` (the working copy is deliberately not ranked against a repo, §4.5.4b), so the reload mounts the last commit **over** every AI edit since. **MEASURED in the owner's own IndexedDB:** a repo-mounted project with four consecutive file-writing turns — two of them fully settled and BILLED (`usage`+`credits` annotations, so `onFinish` definitely ran) — held exactly ONE snapshot, the `"Loaded from repository"` mount itself, with `nextSeq: 1` proving `createLocalSnapshot` was called once. The healthy projects beside it serialized 22/22 and 15/15 binaries cleanly, which is why this presents as intermittent rather than broken. Fixed by recording only what LANDED. **Never regress, and note the two sets differ in OPPOSITE directions:** the map records `landed` (it must describe the disk), while the DELETE plan keeps using `restorable` (a write failure must not be escalated into deleting the copy already on disk — the recoverable direction inverted). Pinned by `files-restore-writethrough.spec.ts` (4 tests, mutation-verified: reverting to `restorable` fails the map assertion and the strict-serialize one) **with a control** proving a restore that recorded nothing at all would not pass. ⚠️ **Still open:** WHY a write fails on Nodepod is unproven — a live probe of both open projects showed every binary readable, so the failure is intermittent and its trigger is not yet identified. This fix stops one failed write from permanently disabling a project's safety net; it does not stop the write failing. The 2026-08-08 diagnostics in `serializeFiles` (`lastError` in the log line) are what will name the cause next time.

5. **WebContainer is priced out; the browser-side slot gets a THIRD provider, not a replacement of CodeSandbox (owner, 2026-07-31).** StackBlitz quoted ~$10k/8k calls. The instruction was explicit: *"I STILL WANT TO HAVE CODESANDBOX as it working and I have an accounting pretty good right now… I want to add a third BrowserPod."* So this is additive — CodeSandbox keeps serving, BrowserPod is piloted into the slot WebContainer vacated, and WebContainer stays in-tree as local-dev default (§2.1a). Rationale for not simply switching to a cheaper *server* provider (E2B/Fly/Northflank are all cheaper than CodeSandbox): the CodeSandbox integration is working and its accounting is settled, and the ~$0.14/hr it costs is a known quantity, whereas the whole reason to want a browser runtime is to get marginal compute back to ~$0 and restore §7's cost model. Those are different problems; solving the second does not require breaking the first.
6. **Nodebox rejected on licence before capability (2026-07-31).** Recorded in §8c so it is not re-proposed: it is the intuitive "browser runtime from a vendor we already use" answer and it fails four ways, the first being that its Sustainable Use License forbids commercial use.
7. **Measure before migrating off CodeSandbox for COST reasons.** `SANDBOX_EST_VM_HOURS_PER_KCREDIT = 8.33` is an operator ESTIMATE that CREDITS.md itself flags as "genuinely unknown until T12's report exists" — and T12 shipped. Any server-provider migration argued on price must cite the Admin VM-hours report, not the estimate. This repo has twice nearly bought a fix for a defect that did not exist (the cache warmup misread; the six-sample KIE probe).

**Standing rule (from §1.3):** no WebContainer-specific coupling outside the provider — now a test, not prose. It remains management's negotiating leverage with StackBlitz.

### §8a Decisions log (project-first creation — newest last)

1. **Creation and the build are TWO steps (owner, 2026-07-29).** New Project clones the pinned starter, installs it, serves it, and stops — no model is contacted — *"nothing else should be able to stop the project from getting created."* Rationale: creation used to end by firing the most expensive generation in the product automatically, so every way that generation could fail was a way a project could fail to feel created, and the user paid for a design pass they had not yet had a chance to steer.
2. **The prompt is CARRIED, not fired.** It goes back into the textbox for the user to edit; sending it is the build. This strengthens §4.4a's precedence rule (explicit input > inference > guidance) rather than altering it — the user now literally edits the inference before it runs. Corollary: the brief must NOT embed a copy of the prompt as it stood at creation, or an edited request arrives with a stale second request underneath it.
3. **Billing splits: flat for creation, per-token for the build (owner, 2026-07-29).** `PROJECT_CREATE_CREDITS` (default 150) at registration; the build turn bills like any other turn; `CREATION_FLAT_CREDITS` retired and refused. Rationale: the flat price existed because creation cost was cache-luck the user could not see — that argument applies to the clone, which is now the only fixed-shape step. The build turn's cost is proportional to what the user asked for, which is a number they can influence.
4. **The credit refusal is the ONLY permitted stopper.** It refuses before anything is provisioned, so a refusal leaves nothing half-made — categorically different from a mid-creation failure, and the only reading under which "a flat creation charge" and "nothing may stop creation" are both true.
5. **The landing/chrome pass is decided by the MODEL from the request, never by a keyword table here (owner asked for context detection; §4.4c records the mechanism).** A stated default with a stated exception. The hardcoded-classifier alternative has cost this repo twice, and the constraint is now enforced by a source-scan spec rather than by memory.
6. **`isCreationTurn` → `isFirstBuildTurn`: a behavioural fact, not a price.** Ten protections still hang off the marker; no billing does. Decoupling also closed the forged-marker pricing exploit.
7. **The paid-rung lock STAYS on the first build turn even though it now bills per-token.** The measured Fable-5/KIE gateway timeout is a function of ARTIFACT SIZE, not of pricing, and this is still the largest artifact in the product. *(Scoped to "premium" until 2026-07-31, then to every paid rung of the §4.6.1a ladder. ⚠️ It was `firstBuildLocked: true` when this entry was written; every rung has shipped `false` since 2026-08-03, so read this as a dated log entry rather than as current behaviour.)*

### §8b Decisions log (model tier ladder — newest last)

1. **The two-model premium boolean is SUPERSEDED by an ordered three-class ladder (owner, 2026-07-31).** §4.6.1a's recorded decision — "a **boolean toggle** between exactly two operator-configured, operator-priced models" — is replaced by **Standard (`claude-sonnet-5`) · Premium (`claude-opus-5`) · SuperMax (`claude-fable-5`)**, each rung a `*_MODEL` selector plus a `*_MINIMUM_CREDITS` threshold. The superseded text is retained in §4.6.1a because it explains the shape of the replacement. Nothing in the old decision's *reasoning* was wrong — the constraint it protected ("model choice is config, never a free-form user string") is unchanged and is what makes the ladder safe: the client sends an enum **tier id**, never a model.
2. **Generalized, not copied (owner, 2026-07-31).** One pure decision over an ordered list, rather than a third branch beside the premium one. The threshold, the first-build lock and the decline-to-Standard fallback are per-rung DATA, so each rule is stated once and a fourth rung is config rather than surgery.
3. **The platform default flips to `claude-sonnet-5` IN CODE, not just in env (owner, 2026-07-31).** `DEFAULT_MODEL` was `claude-opus-5`; Opus 5 becomes the Premium rung. The revert is config-only (`LLM_MODEL=claude-opus-5`, no rebuild — §4.2a), which is what makes the flip safe to make in code. 🔴 **The revert is CURRENTLY REQUIRED on KIE.** A catalogue re-probe (2026-07-31, `kie-model-health.mjs`, 136 requests) found KIE serving only the Opus 4-8 / Opus 5 line: `claude-sonnet-5` and `claude-fable-5` returned HTTP 500 on every attempt, so **two of the three rungs cannot run there and a KIE deploy MUST set `LLM_MODEL=claude-opus-5`**. ⚠️ Do not cite the earlier 2026-07-30 reading (77% sonnet-5 500s against a 21/22 Opus control) as evidence about sonnet-5 specifically — `constants.ts` records that as a misreading: it sampled two models, and with the full catalogue in view the shape is an OUTAGE that spared one model line. A one-model probe cannot tell a model fault from an outage. That is why the revert path must stay documented and why `kie-model-health.mjs` is committed.
4. **Both thresholds ship at 1500, as two independent variables.** They unlock together today; keeping them separate means separating them later costs an env edit rather than a code change. The in-code defaults stay 1200/1500 so a deploy with no env at all still gets a monotonic ladder.
5. **An unrecognised tier id resolves DOWN to Standard, never up.** Mirrors `parseUserEffort`. The value arrives in a browser body and spends the platform's credit pool; inventing a more expensive class than the user asked for is the costly direction.
6. **A rung reports `available` and `serveable` separately.** One field would force either "locked on the screen you just paid on" or "an enabled control that hard-fails" — the degrade-to-off rule needs to distinguish *you cannot afford this* from *the operator has not configured this*.

7. **🔴 THE THIRD RUNG IS RETIRED, AND THE MASTER FLAG IS RENAMED (owner, 2026-08-08).** `supermax` /
   `SUPERMAX_MODEL` / `SUPERMAX_MINIMUM_CREDITS` are gone after one week (2026-07-31 → 2026-08-08); the
   ladder is **Standard · Premium** again, with `PREMIUM_MODEL` free to name whatever the operator wants
   (the owner's deploy points it at `claude-fable-5`, the model that used to be the third rung).
   `ENABLE_EXTENDED_MODELS` → **`ENABLE_PREMIUM_MODEL`** in the same change. Decision 2 above is what
   made this cheap: because the ladder was generalized to a LIST rather than copied into a twin, removing
   a rung touched the table and none of the rules.
8. **All three retired names are REFUSED, not ignored (2026-08-08).** `refuseRetiredModelTierEnv`
   (`billing/premium-model-flag.ts`) throws a `NotConfiguredError` naming the replacement. The flag is
   the reason the refusal is not optional: it **defaults ON**, so a deploy carrying
   `ENABLE_EXTENDED_MODELS=false` would, on upgrade, stop being read, fall through to the default, and
   silently start serving the expensive rung to everyone — the costly direction, throwing nothing and
   logging nothing. `SUPERMAX_*` joins it for the sibling reason (a selector nothing reads is a
   configuration the operator believes they have), and treating one by hand and the other by judgement
   is how the two halves of a pair drift. ⚠️ **Placement is the safety argument, not the throw:** it sits
   inside `getModelTier`, which `getModelTiers` wraps per rung — so the MONEY path (`getTierModel`,
   before a generation) throws, and the READ path (`/api/me`) degrades to a locked row carrying the
   message. Never on `kieRates`, which runs on every settlement.
9. **A request still naming `'supermax'` resolves DOWN with reason `standard_requested`, not
   `unavailable` (2026-08-08).** Both outcomes are deliberate and they are different facts: `unavailable`
   is documented as the one OPERATOR-fault reason and drives a decline notice, so using it for a deleted
   rung would tell a user their SuperMax request was declined — naming a class the product no longer
   has — and would send an operator hunting a misconfiguration that does not exist. The client agrees by
   construction: `isModelTierId` reads the shipping `MODEL_TIER_IDS`, so a stored `'supermax'` coerces to
   `'standard'` rather than to the nearest surviving paid rung.
10. **The coverage lost with the rung is RECORDED, not silently replaced by weaker tests (2026-08-08).**
    Four properties needed two PAID rungs and are unwritable on one — never-step-down-one-rung, a broken
    selector leaving its sibling priced, `getTierModel` naming the requesting rung's own env var, and the
    panel's rendered "unavailable, never a threshold" copy (with one paid rung an unserveable Premium
    means there is no picker to inspect at all). Where a structural equivalent existed it replaced the
    behavioural one; where none did, the loss is written into the spec file's own header with an
    instruction to restore it if a rung returns. ⚠️ A *hypothetical* fixture rung does not work and the
    reason is worth keeping: `decideModelTier` validates the requested id against `MODEL_TIER_IDS`
    **before** consulting the ladder it was handed — the table IS the whitelist, which is what stops a
    browser body naming a rung into existence — so a fake rung resolves to `standard` whatever the
    fixture says.

---

11. **🔴 THE SECOND PAID RUNG IS BACK AS `platinum` (owner, 2026-08-10), AND THE FLAG NAME FLIPPED WITH IT.** `claude-fable-5` / `PLATINUM_MODEL` / `PLATINUM_MINIMUM_CREDITS` (2000) / `ENABLE_PLATINUM_MODEL`, and the master switch returns to **`ENABLE_EXTENDED_MODELS`** — so `ENABLE_PREMIUM_MODEL` is now the REFUSED name, and `SUPERMAX_*` stays refused. **Platinum is the rung SuperMax used to be under a better name, NOT a revival of its env vars:** a deploy still carrying `SUPERMAX_MODEL=x` must fail loudly rather than be silently adopted into a rung whose threshold and price it was never checked against. ⚠️ **This log ended on entry 10 — the RETIREMENT — until 2026-08-11, so its last word contradicted §4.6.1a's body for a day.** That is the one failure mode an append-only log exists to prevent, and it only works if somebody appends when the thing changes. ⚠️ Entry 10's recorded coverage loss became WRITABLE again here; **two of its four properties are written and two are not** — see §4.6.1a.

### §8d Decisions log (prompt + skills durability — newest last)

1. **🔴 The prompt and skills stores persist through `ObjectStore`, not the container filesystem (2026-08-01).** Both wrote to `platformDataDir()` unconditionally, which `spec/hosting.md` already forbids — *"the app is stateless; anything stateful in the container is a bug."* A Lightsail container filesystem does not survive a deployment and **there is no boot-time doc-sync by design** (§4.3: generation must have zero GitHub dependency at request time), so on AWS **every deploy would have landed a container with no prompt version, failing every generation** with `NotConfiguredError('The system prompt')` until an admin pressed Refresh. Reproduced before fixing (a store on an empty root reports `getActive() → null`). `skills/store.ts`'s own header had promised this backend since it was written ("resources in S3 under a `storage_prefix`") while doing the opposite — **a comment describing an intention reads as a description of the code**, the same way `shell-strip`'s false claim survived review.
2. **ONE implementation, not a local class beside an S3 class.** `ObjectStore` is already "S3 when `S3_BUCKET` is set, the filesystem otherwise", so routing through it gives production durability and leaves local dev byte-identical one directory over. A second local-only store would be two writers of one state — the drift this codebase keeps re-learning (two Syncs, two `started` flags, two prompt copies).
3. **The hot path is untouched and was checked, not assumed.** `active.ts` memoises the active version for 30s in-process, so this costs at most one round of GETs per 30 seconds, never one per generation.
4. **`/healthz` reports `systemPrompt`, the report's only non-config entry (§9a).** Its `platformKey` sibling exists because a deploy once reported *"healthy AND ready while 503ing every generation"*; a missing prompt version is that failure exactly, so the one check §9a keys on must see it. Guarded to report `degraded` rather than throw — observability may never take down the endpoint it reports on. Live-verified: `degraded` → sync → `ok`.
5. **⚠️ The earlier claim that split stores caused cache/billing divergence between instances was WRONG, and is retired.** The version id embeds a timestamp but **never reaches the model**, and the prompt sections are static files with no clock, so identical docs produce byte-identical prompt text on any container. Confirmed live: a fresh sync of unchanged docs produced build hash `71e75fcf` — the same hash as the version built the day before. The real defect was the outage above, which is worse and simpler.

---

### §8e Decisions log (repository import — newest last)

1. **🔴 A RUNTIME-CAPABILITY FLAG WAS ANSWERING A MONEY QUESTION, and one provider imported for free (fixed 2026-07-31, §4.4a/§4.6).** `openImportWorkspace` early-returned on `alreadyBooted || !SANDBOX_REQUIRES_PROJECT`, and `SANDBOX_REQUIRES_PROJECT` is `SANDBOX_PROVIDER === 'codesandbox'`. So an import registered a project and took `PROJECT_CREATE_CREDITS` on CodeSandbox and **registered nothing and charged nothing on WebContainer — the default provider at the time**. The condition asks "does this sandbox need a project id to boot?"; it was being used to answer "did we provision a workspace for this user?". Different questions, and nothing threw. Every import now registers **unconditionally, on every runtime** — `registerImportProject` calls `createProject` with no knowledge of the sandbox provider at all, which is the point: the charge is a server-side billing decision and the sandbox is irrelevant to it. The flag now decides only whether a FAILED registration is fatal (server sandbox: nowhere to put the files) or degrades to a browser-local import. ⚠️ **Do not read "both" as a count of providers — there are three ids and the number enabled changes.** `ENABLED_SANDBOX_PROVIDERS` is `['nodepod']` today (webcontainer and codesandbox are hidden-not-deleted, §8), and Nodepod's `requiresProject` is **false**, i.e. exactly the trait that used to mean "free". It is not free, because registration no longer consults the trait. A future fourth provider inherits the charge for the same reason. **A 402 is not an outage** and refuses on every runtime — degrading past it would hand a user with no credits a working project for free while telling them the server was unreachable, which is false twice. The one exemption is `alreadyBooted`: importing into a project the user already has open writes to a workspace already paid for.
2. **Clone became a server operation, and the seam gained one method to make it possible (2026-08-02, §4.13a).** `getDefaultBranch` joined `GitProvider` because `StarterTemplates` links to `/git?url=…` with no branch and a user pasting a URL is under no obligation to know whether the trunk is `main`, `master` or `develop`. Guessing is not a fallback — on a `master` repo it produces "branch not found", which reads as "your repository does not exist". Per §4.5.4c deviation 1 it had to be expressible for BOTH providers, so it is contract-tested from one body against GitHub and GitLab, with a two-repo control (different default branches) that a hardcoded `'main'` cannot satisfy.
3. **The route is an `op` on the existing project git route, not a new route — and that is strictly MORE secure than the feature spec asked for.** The spec assumed "at clone time there is no project yet" and specified `requireVerifiedUser` alone; the actual flow registers the project first, so both walls apply. It also inherits the route's `providerErrorResponse` mapper, without which a `GitProviderError` reaching the generic `errorResponse` flattens to a 500 and the client loses the `reconnect` signal that drives the connect prompt (`GitProviderError` has `status`, not `statusCode`, and is not in `SAFE_ERRORS`).
4. **Two live defects were found by TESTING the new module, not by reading it.** `findLfsPointers` inspected only files typed as text — but LFS exists precisely for `.glb`/`.png`/`.mp4`, which `classifyFetchedBlob` types as BINARY from the path, so the guard could never fire on any path a pointer is actually found at: present, free, and useless. And GitLab's `_listTree` built its own request and kept sending `Authorization: Bearer ` when the token was empty, so an anonymous clone of a public GitLab project read the branch head fine and then 401'd on the tree — surfacing as a connect prompt for a repository that needs no connection. Both were caught because the GitLab fake REFUSES an empty bearer rather than ignoring headers ("never be nicer than the real thing").
5. **⚠️ Two vacuous assertions were caught by mutation testing, in tests that had already passed.** A test named "checks the API origin before it fetches anything" asserted only that the guard was CALLED, and passed with the call moved to after the fetch. A source scan for the removed credential path enumerated five files, which is the "list of doors somebody thought of" shape §4.4a was rewritten twice to escape. Both were tightened (observe the request count from inside the spy; sweep all of `app/` for `prompt(`). **A test whose input cannot reach the rule it names is not a weak test, it is no test** — and the only thing that surfaced either was deliberately breaking the code and checking the suite went red.

---

### §8f Decisions log (KIE model families — newest last)

1. **Family derives from the MODEL ID, never from `LLM_PROVIDER` (2026-08-04).** KIE is one provider, one key and one bill fronting three protocols. The §4.6.1a ladder's three rungs may point at models from three families simultaneously while the provider is one value per deploy, so the model id is the only thing that varies per rung. The alternative — a second provider entry per wire — would have forked the key, the price list and the tier resolution three ways to express one account.
2. **An unknown id REFUSES at model resolution; it never gets a guessed wire.** Refusing costs one loud, immediate, free error naming the id and the accepted prefixes. Guessing costs a hard 400 before a token on a model the operator believes is configured — which is exactly what the pre-existing Claude-shaped defaults would have produced for a `gpt-*` id.
3. **The Claude family is untouched, and the branch STRUCTURE is what guarantees it.** Every Anthropic wrapper stays wrapped around the Claude branch only, pinned by a default-deny source scan rather than by review. `KIE_BASE_URL` stays Claude-scoped for the same reason: an operator who set it meant the Claude endpoint, and one variable silently repointing three wires is a config value whose meaning changed underneath them.
4. **"Cache rates are never quoted in the list" is EXTENDED into family policy, not broken (owner-visible consequence).** Claude keeps derivation and still refuses the keys; gpt rows must quote KIE's published pair atomically; gemini refuses the pair and bills cached tokens at full input. The forcing evidence was one number — KIE's gpt Cache Writes price is **1.25×** input (the five-minute tier), not the 2.0× every Claude row derives, so deriving it would have over-charged that class by 60% on every cold turn with nothing throwing. **A rule that holds for one family is not a rule.**
5. **Gemini ships with NO cache economics, flagged and accepted (owner, 2026-08-04).** KIE quotes no cached rate and their wire reports no cached-token counter, so cached tokens bill at the full input rate — never a discount we cannot verify they grant, never a surcharge we cannot observe. A warm Gemini edit therefore costs what a cold one costs, by design; the margin report must not read it as a caching regression.
6. **No Grok (owner, 2026-08-04).** Noted as a later candidate: the feed prices its Cached Input, but its endpoint shape is unverified and FR9 forbids shipping an id on feed evidence.
7. **An id ships only on a live probe, and the pricing FEED is not such a probe.** The feed's display names are not the API ids (`gpt-5.6-sol` vs `gpt-5-6-sol`), so a row copied from it prices a model that cannot be called. `gpt-5-6-terra` was deliberately held out of the initial plan and shipped only after its own probe came back clean — priced and listed in the same edit, which is the only way the two can never drift into the priced-but-unlisted silent mis-bill.
8. **The delivery table is DATA, and an unmeasured surface is assumed to STREAM.** Keyed by (provider, family) since the same key and account measured KIE-claude batched and KIE-codex/gemini streaming. If KIE's Claude adapter heals, re-measure and flip one word. Claiming `batched` for an unprobed surface would manufacture the despair the panel exists to prevent, and would be unfalsifiable from the user's side.
9. **The harvested retry strings are defence-in-depth, and the code says so rather than claiming a win.** KIE reports faults as HTTP 200 plus a `{"code":N,"msg":"..."}` envelope, and replaying that shape through the real SDKs raises **no error on any family** — the stream finishes clean with empty text, and the proxy's `!producedText` guard (which sits AFTER the retry loop) is what fails and refunds it. Moving that check inside the loop is a real behaviour change with its own money implications and was deliberately left to its own task.
10. **Which models earn rungs is an OPERATOR decision after a manual eval, not part of this build.** The code only makes the families available. Every skill, brief and measurement in this repo is Claude-tuned, output quality on gpt/gemini is unevaluated, and there are still no output-quality evals — so all three rungs still name Claude models.

---

### §8g Decisions log (second gateway + the provider ladder — newest last)

1. **Comet is a second PROVIDER, not a second model family (2026-08-10).** It fronts the same Claude models over the same Anthropic-native Messages API, so switching is a cost/reliability decision and never a capability one. That is what keeps `LLM_PROVIDER` a config swap.
2. **The family names a DIALECT; the (provider, family) pair chooses the wire.** `gpt-5-*` rides Responses on KIE and chat-completions on Comet, so neither half can answer the question alone. The `chat` family was added for Comet's OpenAI-compatible resale ids, with cache profile `none` for gemini's reason: no verifiable cached rate, no cached-token counter.
3. **Price lists are PER PROVIDER.** Two gateways reselling one model at two prices cannot share one document, and the failure of pretending otherwise is that the last-promoted list prices the other gateway's turns — silently.
4. **The charged Comet rate is `pricing × ratio`, read per ROW.** Three rows carry `1.0`; a hardcoded `0.8` would under-charge exactly the newest and most expensive models. ⚠️ **The OQ1 half of this item was WRONG and is retracted (2026-08-12):** it read *"Comet's feed reporting official $2/$10 for Sonnet 5 confirms the introductory-pricing note rather than contradicting it, and `MODEL_RATES` deliberately stays at $3/$15."* $2/$10 is now the standard price (the September increase was cancelled), the feed was correct, and the deliberate deviation was a 1.5× over-charge on the default model. `MODEL_RATES` is $2/$10.
5. **Media is an independent switch, and the provider is stamped on the task record.** A render outlives the request that started it, so the record — not the current config — decides who is polled, who serves the bytes and who is refunded. Unset `MEDIA_PROVIDER` inherits `LLM_PROVIDER`, which on an Anthropic deploy honestly means "no media".
6. **`AUTO_MODEL_SELECT` ships DEFAULT OFF and picks ONCE, at request start (owner decision, 2026-08-10).** One gateway serves a turn from first token to settlement, because `rawCostUsd` prices a turn against ONE rate table and a mid-stream failover would settle A's tokens at B's rates. The flag exists so the ladder cannot change a single generation until an operator opts in.
7. **A ladder rung must be able to PRICE what it would run, in its own right.** `ratesFor` falls back to a provider's most expensive row, so a gateway chosen for its discount would otherwise become the biggest bill in the product — silently, with the credit count going up for no visible reason.
8. **Cooldown is a preference, never a wall.** Every rung cooling still runs the turn on the best one: trading one refunded generation for every generation is the wrong direction. The window is minutes because the cached prefix is per gateway and flapping costs ~8× a prefix in cache writes.
9. **Gating the ladder on NATIVE rung pricing was considered and REJECTED.** `MODEL_RATES` has no `claude-fable-5` row, so that rule would drop Anthropic — the deliberate last-resort rung — out of the ladder for an ordinary rung selector, trading a bounded mis-bill for having nowhere to fail over to. The residual risk (a rung model a gateway does not price natively settles at the marketplace rate there) is **pre-existing to the ladder** and is recorded at `withTiers`, where a comment previously claimed a refusal that could not fire.
10. **The discount is shown to the user, and it is refused rather than guessed.** Credits are cost-proportional, so a gateway discount is the user's purchasing power and invisible without this. Every unanswerable case says nothing at all: a savings figure is a marketing claim printed beside a money number.
11. **Turn kind is decorated onto ledger rows at READ time, never written to the ledger.** The ledger is append-only behind a locked SQL writer; a display label must not become a new way for that path to fail — and deriving it lights up every historical row instead of only future ones.
12. **🔴 Whether `promptTokens` counts the cached tokens is a declared per-family fact about the VENDOR'S WIRE (`FamilyPolicy.promptTokensIncludeCacheRead`), never inferred and never assumed.** `costForRates` ADDS prompt-at-input-rate and cache-at-cache-rate, so it is correct only when the two do not overlap — true on Anthropic (siblings) and false on OpenAI and Google (the cached count is a breakdown OF the prompt total). From the multi-family cutover until 2026-08-11 the cached portion was billed twice on gpt/gemini/chat; a live turn measured **1.86×**, and the error GREW WITH CACHE WARMTH, so it was worst on exactly the turns §4.2.8 exists to make cheap. The field is REQUIRED, so a new family cannot ship without an answer. **The general rule: a vendor-neutral field documented with a single vendor's convention is a defect the moment a second vendor arrives — and none of 6,500 tests could see this one, because every usage fixture in the repo was Anthropic-shaped.**
13. **A gateway's health is measured on the wire it actually serves, not on a status code.** KIE returns **200 in 0.25s** and then fails mid-stream: its whole `claude-*` catalogue measured **92–100% failure** on 2026-08-11 while `gpt-5-6-*` and `gemini-3-5-flash` sat at **0%**. This is why the §4.6.1a rungs and `AUTO_MODEL_SELECT`'s cooldown key on generation-level failure rather than on reachability, and why the Comet cost comparison must be stated against the provider the platform is ACTUALLY running rather than against a healthy KIE that does not currently exist.
14. **🔴 CUT OVER TO COMET (owner, 2026-08-11 — `_specs/cometapi-provider_spec.md` OQ7).** `LLM_PROVIDER=Comet`, with `LLM_PROVIDER_CHAIN=Comet,Anthropic`. **The rationale is not the one the question was written to ask.** OQ7 asked whether a ~2× cost increase was acceptable in order to leave KIE; entry 13's measurement removed the thing being compared against, because there is no working KIE Claude to pay 2× relative to. Against the provider the platform was ACTUALLY running — the Anthropic stopgap — Comet is **~20% cheaper**, streams, and returns real thinking text. So the decision is a cost REDUCTION on the live bill, and both figures stay recorded because only the pair is honest. **Two consequences, stated rather than discovered:** REACH still halves against the KIE the credit packs were priced around — a §4.6 positioning fact, and one that must NOT be answered by lowering `CREDIT_MARGIN`, which would trade the platform's margin for the user's reach and bury a provider cost change inside the pricing model where nothing could attribute it back; and KIE is dropped from the CHAIN ORDER, never from the config (FR9) — with `AUTO_MODEL_SELECT` on, leaving a 92–100%-failing gateway at the front of the ladder spends one generation into the 5-minute cooldown every window, forever, for a fully-known reason.
15. **🔴 A MODEL ID IS NOT PORTABLE ACROSS GATEWAYS — the enhancer takes a selector per gateway (owner, 2026-08-11).** Haiku 4.5 is `claude-haiku-4-5` on KIE and Anthropic and only `claude-haiku-4-5-20251001` on Comet, so `<PROVIDER>_ENHANCE_PROMPT_MODEL` outranks `ENHANCE_PROMPT_MODEL`, resolved against the LADDER-SELECTED gateway. Driving it live found four defects no test could: a model **priced but not LISTED** ran as `modelsList[0]` and settled at the requested model's rates (`env-models.ts` now lists every configured model for both gateways); **`claude-haiku-4-5` does not support adaptive thinking**, falsifying §4.2a's "the legacy set is CLOSED" — treat it as open in both directions; the enhancer billed **zero input tokens** and now settles from `result.steps` like every other path; and `provider` was used to PRICE every turn and never stored (**migration 0021**, NULL = unknown, never a default). **Every table keyed on a model id — prices, model lists, capability flags — needs the gateway's spelling, not the model's name.**

---

## 9. Milestones (LAUNCH/GATING ORDER — NOT CONSTRUCTION ORDER)

> **Read §1.3 principle 0 first.** These phases exist to sequence *launch gates* (what must be true before external users, before billing, before scale) — they do NOT sequence the build. **Build every feature in every phase now.** A feature's phase tells you when it must be *live and gated*, not when you're allowed to write it.

### Phase 9a — The Credential Pass (runs whenever keys arrive; NOT a build blocker)

Per §1.3 principle 0, every feature below is BUILT during Phases 1–2 with config-driven degradation. This pass is pure configuration + verification — no new features:

| Credential | Unlocks | Until then (built, degraded) |
|---|---|---|
| `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` (Console org) | Credits-mode generation (`DEFAULT_MODEL` = `claude-sonnet-5`, §4.6.1a Standard) | Credits UI renders; generation returns "platform key not configured"; devs set `PRO_FEATURES_ENABLED=true` + BYOK locally |
| ~~License service `ValidateSubscription`~~ (RETIRED 2026-07-20) | — | External license service retired; BYOK/Pro disabled by default and manual/testing-only (§4.6.1). No credential needed |
| `STRIPE_*` | Credit purchases | Checkout UI built; buy button shows "payments not configured"; ledger/grants fully functional |
| `SUPABASE_*` | Hosted auth/persistence | Local/dev persistence path; feature-flagged |
| AWS `S3_*` | Snapshots/play builds | Local filesystem/dev bucket adapter |
| `MONITORING_WEBHOOK_URL` / `ANALYTICS_WEBHOOK_URL` (§5A) | Ship errors/alerts + funnel events to a collector | Monitor logs locally; every call site already wired (no-op transport) |
| ~~StackBlitz plan~~ | — | **CLOSED 2026-07-31 (§6, A.2): priced out at ~$10k/8k calls, never purchased. Gates nothing.** WebContainer is local-dev only and is never served to external users |
| `BROWSERPOD_API_KEY` (if the §8 pilot passes) | Browser-side sandbox provider | CodeSandbox remains the served runtime; a BrowserPod build simply is not deployed |

Verification checklist per credential: set config → restart → the previously-degraded UI path now completes end-to-end.

### Phase 0 — Prompt proof (days; throwaway; start immediately)
Script: prompt → Anthropic API (system prompt assembled from Agent Reference + a hand-built skills index; `load_skill` tool resolving from a local clone of the skills repo) → parse file actions → write into a local clone of the golden starter template (`babylontoolkit/AppTemplate` — self-contained, no submodules) → verify Vite HMR shows the change.
**Exit:** 10 varied prompts appropriate to the chosen template's genre (feature adds, visual changes, HUD elements) each yield a runnable change with ≤ 1 manual fix, and at least one prompt demonstrably triggers a skill load that improves the result. If this fails, fix the docs/skills/prompt layer before fork surgery. (APEX BURNOUT on Lovable is informal evidence this passes.)

### Phase 1 — Fork surgery (local, single-user; no external users)
Fork bolt.diy → strip/replace per §2.3 → doc-sync v1 (build-time prompt bake; manual rebuild) → **skills sync v1 + `/skill-name` slash invocation with autocomplete + description-triggered `load_skill` loop in the agent proxy (tool loop built MCP-ready)** → AppTemplate-DEFAULT project creation (registry promoted; upstream blank/import retained under Advanced) → server-side agent proxy with platform key + prompt caching + usage recording (incl. `skills_loaded`) → branding pass.
**Exit:** a local user builds and plays a modified game (golden template) through the forked UI with zero manual setup; a physics/racing-flavored request visibly routes in the relevant **Component Reference on-demand block** (§4.3) and yields batteries-included code. Skills demo: `/bt-spec <feature>` in the chat loads the bt-spec skill and produces its workflow output against the project.

### Phase 2 — Hosted layer + billing built (still gated; internal/demo only)
Supabase auth/projects/snapshots/messages/generations → credit ledger + Stripe packs behind `BILLING_ENFORCED=off` → free-grant flow with verification gating → **Pro Tools entitlements v1 (Pro-gated BYOK — the original external `ValidateSubscription` integration was RETIRED 2026-07-20, BYOK now disabled by default and manual/testing-only, §4.6.1)** → **generation Stop + checkpoint restore (§4.12)** → **MCP v1: parse project `.mcp.json`, launch stdio servers in the WebContainer, tool-call bridge into the server tool loop (§4.14)** → admin prompt **and skills** refresh endpoints → self-healing loop → **ops baseline (error tracking, analytics events, backups — §5A)** → deploy to staging on app.babylontoolkit.com behind auth wall.
**Exit:** end-to-end demo on the real domain: sign up → wizard-less new project → generate → **break it → restore checkpoint** → snapshot/resume → (simulated) purchase → ledger correct → **a Pro Tools subscriber account shows BYOK unlocked and generates on its own key**. **This is the management/acquirer demo build.**

### Phase 3 — Beta launch (FIRST EXTERNAL USERS — ~~StackBlitz plan/license must be active~~ **no sandbox licence gate remains, 2026-07-31**)
Invite-only, ≤ 500 WebContainer sessions/month under the paid-plan allowance. Guided Tour wizard → share + `/play` pages (with Report link) → **remix + self-remix/Duplicate (§4.8)** → gallery v1 → **Game Backends v1 (connect own Supabase; leaderboard wizard toggle; RLS pre-share warning — §4.15)** → **chat image attachments + user asset uploads (§4.12, §4.9)** → GitHub webhook auto-refresh for docs **and skills** → skills usage dashboard (which skills fire, cost impact) → **ToS/Privacy live (§5A)** → decide `BILLING_ENFORCED` on (recommended: on, with generous grants — revenue data > free-user data for the acquisition story).
**Exit:** external non-developer completes wizard → playable shared game, unaided; unit economics measured (real cost/generation, cache hit rate, margin).

### Phase 4 — Public launch & growth
~~StackBlitz negotiated license~~ (**closed — priced out, §6/A.2**) → sandbox-provider decision per real session volume (**CodeSandbox served today; BrowserPod pilot per §8**) → remaining genre templates → remix loop → Assets tab with first premium GLTF packs → export ZIP + **full GitHub Sync (§4.13: link, push, pull, divergence flow)** → **MCP hardening: `.mcp.json` edit UX, secret-scan on push/share, per-server enable toggles (§4.14)** → external deploy buttons surfaced (Netlify/Vercel/GH-Pages, §2.3) → subscription option → admin dashboards complete. (Community skills marketplace: recorded intent in §4.11, post-launch.)

## 10. Open Questions

1. ~~Golden starter template~~ — decided: `babylontoolkit/AppTemplate` (ES6 by design; MIT; template repo). ~~Residual: submodule vendoring in snapshots~~ — **resolved: the starter vendors `src/babylon` directly; it has no submodules (§4.4).** Residual: WebContainer install time/memory with the full Babylon dependency set.
2. WebContainer perf with a real Toolkit project (install time, memory next to a Babylon scene) — measure in Phase 1; feeds the escape-hatch decision.
3. ~~StackBlitz written terms~~ — **CLOSED 2026-07-31: quoted ~$10,000 per 8,000 API calls; not purchased (§6, A.2).** Residual, now the live question, and narrowed twice by the two spikes of 2026-07-31: the platform half of the **BrowserPod** pilot **passes** (pre-baked `userImage` boots the built starter in 1.2s at ~$0 marginal compute, OSC shell contract intact), so the blocker is now IDENTIFIED and is **somebody else's: `Atomics.wait` never returns in their Wasm Node, deadlocking every Vite dep optimizer (rolldown AND esbuild-wasm alike), while Babylon + Havok themselves run fine in-pod** (§8 item 5). We can contain it (bake a dep cache so the optimizer never runs) but not fix it, and the containment breaks on `npm install <pkg>`. Report it to Leaning and revisit. If it is never fixed, the browser-side option is a DIY ESM+Service-Worker build or nothing, and CodeSandbox carries the product alone at ~58–63% effective GM (CREDITS.md) **plus the hibernate machinery an always-billing VM requires**.
4. Retail credit pricing/pack sizes (needs Phase 2–3 usage data).
5. Asset pack licensing text for premium GLTF content used in user games.
6. ~~Hosting target~~ — decided: **AWS** — Lightsail Container Service (Docker) for the Remix app, S3 + CloudFront for snapshots and the separate-domain play origin (existing AWS account); see `spec/hosting.md`.
7. ~~Upstream pull cadence / divergence cutoff~~ — decided: indefinite pull compatibility, monthly cadence, rules in §2.1a. Residual: the merge around upstream's planned agent-backend rework will be the hardest single pull — schedule dedicated time when it ships.
8. Anthropic startup credits / current model pricing — verify before Phase 3.
9. Skills repo: (a) spec-validity audit of existing workflow skills (bt-spec, bt-plan, bt-design…) — these are first-class platform skills, slash-invocable in chat; (b) SKILLS_BACKLOG.md domain/pattern skills are OPTIONAL future content (a `leaderboards` skill or training example is wanted before the Phase 3 wizard toggle); (c) description quality tuning for auto-triggering.
10. Account deletion & data retention: jurisdiction requirements (GDPR et al.) for PII purge vs. financial-ledger retention — legal review before public launch.
11. ~~Payment provider strategy~~ — decided: **Stripe only** (the platform's sole payment + subscription signal; the external license service is retired, §4.6.1). Residual: revisit Merchant-of-Record (Paddle/Lemon Squeezy) only if international VAT/sales-tax burden becomes material — finance to monitor after `BILLING_ENFORCED=on`.
12. ~~`ValidateSubscription` endpoint~~ — obsolete (RETIRED 2026-07-20): the external ASMX license service is no longer called; BYOK/Pro is disabled by default and manual/testing-only, and the only subscription signal read is the live Stripe plan (§4.6.1, §4.18). No endpoint to build.
13. ~~Tier allowance sizing~~ — obsolete: Pro perk is BYOK, not credit allowances. Residual: whether tiers ever differentiate platform features (all tiers = same BYOK at launch).
14. Toolkit version migration: templates pin `toolkit_version`; policy for existing user projects when the Toolkit releases breaking updates (stay pinned forever vs. agent-assisted upgrade flow) — decide before Phase 4.
15. Transactional email provider (verification, receipts, entitlement notices): Supabase's built-in email is not production-grade — pick Resend/Postmark/SES-class provider in Phase 2.
16. Product analytics tool selection (§5A) — Phase 2.
17. **Online multiplayer scoping:** the Toolkit ships a full Colyseus stack out-of-box, but online play requires a Colyseus server endpoint. Launch scope: single-player + split-screen local multiplayer; shared builds of network-capable games run `?solo=true`. Platform-hosted Colyseus endpoints ("platform Multiplayer Hosting") = a natural premium service and expansion intent (§1.4) — decide post-launch.
18. **Final product brand:** neutral working name is "Babylon Toolkit App Builder." A distinct product brand (candidate: CodeWRX at app.codewrxai.com, with Babylon Toolkit as the "powered by" technology layer) is under consideration — decide before Phase 3 beta; execution is a brand-module swap + domain-migration runbook (§2.5, spec/hosting.md), an afternoon of work either way.
19. Scene CDN readiness: CORS headers + content-encoding for `.gz.gltf` on repo.babylontoolkit.com for WebContainer-preview and play-domain origins; confirm CDN-class hosting/bandwidth since it sits in the serving path of every scene-referencing preview/play (Phase 1 verification).
20. **Per-user rate limiting — BUILT for the clone route 2026-08-02; the SHARED-STATE half is what stays open.** `enforceUserRateLimit` (`security/user-rate-limit.ts`) keys on the authenticated user id, not the caller's IP, and the import op is capped at `CLONE_RATE_LIMIT` (10/hour) counted **before** `parseCloneTarget` so a refusal costs no egress (429 + `Retry-After`, `RateLimitedError` in `SAFE_ERRORS`). That closes the hole an individual could exploit at will — the inherited `checkRateLimit` is keyed `${clientIP}:${endpoint}`, so changing network reset it, while punishing everyone behind one NAT as a single caller. **What remains open, and is stated rather than hidden: the default store is IN-PROCESS, so a multi-instance deployment grants N × the limit.** `setUserRateLimitStore` is the seam a Redis/Postgres implementation plugs into (same shape as `setGitTokenStore`), so the completion is a wiring change. Per-instance-per-user is strictly tighter than per-instance-per-IP — do not "simplify" back to the IP limiter. Decide before Phase 4.

21. **Git-LFS policy for imported repositories.** Currently REFUSED with a message naming the count and the paths (§4.13a) — the alternative is a project whose every model, texture and sound is 130 bytes of pointer text, which mounts cleanly and cannot run. Supporting it means the LFS batch API and a second byte path; §4.5.4c already declares LFS out of scope for the SAVE direction, so import inherits that boundary. Revisit only if real users hit it — the owner's own `BabylonJS/BabylonToolkit` does, which suggests Toolkit-shaped repos are more likely to than average.
22. ~~A pre-existing two-writers defect: `GitHubSyncButton.link()` omitted `provider`~~ — **FIXED 2026-08-02.** `GitHubSyncDialog` takes a `provider` prop, `GitStatusChip` passes `providerToUse`, and `link()` now goes through `linkProjectToRepo` — the one wrapper, which makes `provider` a required argument precisely so no call site can fall back to the route's legacy `github` default. Fixing it exposed a **second, worse** instance of the same assumption: the not-connected branch still sent the user to `startConnect('github')`, so a user in GitLab mode with no GitLab connection authorised **GitHub**, came back still unconnected, and saw the identical screen — a loop with no error anywhere. Every user-facing string in the dialog now routes through `providerLabel`. ⚠️ The lesson generalises past this dialog: **a prop with a sensible default makes a MISSING prop invisible**, so the chip's wiring is pinned by its own source scan (with a control) rather than left to the default to paper over.

## 11. Working Agreement

- This SPEC.md + CLAUDE.md read at the start of every AI coding session.
- Sub-specs split out as areas deepen: `spec/anthropic-models.md`, `spec/billing.md`, `spec/binary-files.md`, `spec/context-budget.md`, `spec/doc-sync.md`, `spec/hosting.md`, `spec/licensing.md`, **`spec/model-families.md` (the family names a dialect and the provider chooses the wire — four families, the per-family cache/usage/delivery policy, and the reproducible per-gateway probe procedure)**, `spec/sandbox-browserpod.md`, `spec/sandbox-cloudflare.md`, `spec/sandbox-codesandbox.md`, `spec/sandbox-seam.md`, `spec/skills.md`, `spec/spend-holes.md`, `spec/wizard-config.md`, **`spec/fail-loud.md` (owner directive 2026-07-25: a paid request never fails silently — four terminal states, the standing rules, and the staged kickoff plan for the owed guards)**. This file remains the index and source of truth for scope.
- Changes to the action protocol handling, the sandbox seam rule, the credit formula, or the licensing posture update this spec in the same PR.
- The previous custom-build spec is archived for reference as the potential long-term destination; do not build from it.
